# Periya Tirumadal

## 1. Manniya palpori ser

*manniya palpoṟi sēr āyira vāy vāḷ aravin senni maṇik kudumit teyvac cuddar naduvuḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

manniya – may you live long!; pal poṟi sēr āyira vāy vāḷ aravin – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan, the divine mattress for emperumān), who has many dots (circular spots) on his body and thousands of mouths as well as radiance – his; senni maṇik kudumi – from the carbuncles which are present in the raised hoods of ādiśēśan; deyvam sudar naduvuḷ – among the rays which are extraordinary

**Перевод:**

**manniya – let auspiciousness prevail. Just as King janaka said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 73-28 on seeing perumāḷ and pirātti \[śrī rāma and sītā\] “bhadram tē” (let auspicious things happen to you). just as nammāḻvār mercifully wished mangaḷāśasanam (wishing well) at the beginning of tiruviruttam 2 “vāḻiyarō” (may you live long) and just as jatāyu maharaja sang pallāṇḍu (wishing well) to śrī rāma after seeing him in separation, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 67-15 “yāmōśadimivāyuśman anvēśasi mahāvanē” (May you live long! Whosoever you are searching for, just like looking for a rare medicine in a huge forest, that (sītā) dēvi and my life have both been usurped by rāvaṇa), she (parakāla nāyaki, who is tirumangai āḻvār in feminine form) sings pallāṇḍu as soon as she decides to engage with madal. Since she knows well the power of her speech, she says that instead of being destroyed due to the curse of her speech, it is better to fall at her feet and survive.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**palpoṟi** – having many spots. Just as ruby is embedded in gold, this talks about the beauty \[of the spots\] which make one to keep staring at them without taking the eyes off.

**sēr** – the way the spots are mingled on the divine body of ādiśēśan is just like milk and sugar candy are mixed together intimately, unlike ripened mango and bitter gourd which can never mix well. Since there are a thousand spots, would they appear grotesque? No, they will appear homogeneous, just like *naratvam* and *simhatvam* (the reference here is to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) who incarnated with the head of a lion and the body of a human).

**āyira vāy** – thousand heads. Since ādiśēśan has the constant touch of emperuman’s soft, smiling divine form, it appears as if he takes a thousand heads as an outlet for the overpowering flood of happiness.

**vāḷ aravin** – vāḷ refers to radiance. Due to the touch of [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), ādiśēśan’s radiance too gets displayed resplendently. Just as daśarata cakravarty said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 12-104 “*aham punar dēvakumārarūpam alankrutam tam sutamāvrajantam* I *nandāmi paśyannapi darśanēva bhavāmi druśtvaiva punaryuvēva*  īī” (as soon as I see rāma, my son, who is like a celestial prince and is well decorated, with my inner eyes, I feel overjoyed. If I see him in person, I feel like a youth, once again) and just as vasudēva felt that he had become a young man once again after seeing kriṣṇa, his son, as said in the ślōkam “*mahōtsavamivāsādya putrānanavilōkanāt* I *yuvēva vasudēvō’bhūt vihāyābhyāgatām jarām* īī ” (since he got to see his son’s face, vasudēva felt as if he had seen a great festival, getting rid of his old age and becoming a youth again), is there any surprise that ādiśēśan felt the way as described above, on being touched by [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)!

**senni maṇikudumit teyvaccudar naduvuḷ** – *senni*: head. Here, this term refers to ādiśēśan’s hood. *maṇi* – the carbuncle on the head. *kudumi* – lustre of the gem (carbuncle). d*heyvam* – divine; *aprākrutam* (not composed of the three qualities of *satva* (good)*, rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance) which are seen in samsāram, the materialistic world). *sudar naduvul* – in the midst of this radiance.

In the earlier line also, there was mention of radiance (*vāḷ*). Here too, there is a mention of radiance. What is the difference between these two? The earlier one referred to the naturally occurring radiance of ādiśēśan, common to the species of snakes while this radiance is occurring due to experiencing [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/). Unlike the radiance of materials which are found in this world, unlike the radiance of emperumān himself, this is the radiance obtained by constantly enjoying emperumān. This radiance is not seen in emperumān too.

āmidst the extraordinary radiance of lustrous carbuncle which is located on tiruvanantāḻvān’s (ādiśēśan’s) head, just like rising out of the watery waves of river gangā  which is flowing fiercely in white colour, the one who is reclining….

We will take up the next two lines of the tirumadal in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 2. Manniya nagattanai melor

*manniya nāgattaṇai mēlōr māmalai pōl minnu magara kuṇḍlangaḷ vil veesa*

**Пословный перевод:**

manni – fitting well; a nāgattaṇai mēl – on top of that mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); ōr māmalai pōl – reclining like a distinguished dark mountain; minnu maṇi magara kuṇḍalangaḷ vil veesa – the ornaments worn in the ear have rare gems studded in them and these gems emit radiance similar to rainbow

**Перевод:**

**manniya nāgattaṇai mēlōr māmalai pōl – all these are similes.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**manniya nāgattaṇai** – for the one who lies down on this mattress, the novelty \[of the bed\] remains forever, without the need for a person to make up the bed every day.

**manni** – fitting well. Due to the greatness of tiruvanantāḻvān, [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) is relaxing peacefully such that he will not get up even if he were nudged by periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) with her bosom, like a bow

**a nāgam** – that ādiśēśan who is radiant. The lustrous ādiśēśan.

**aṇai mēl** – atop tiruvanantāḻvān, who is like a mattress.

**ōr māmalai pōl** – he is a like a blue mountain reclining on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān. This is similar to [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/), who is having the form of a dark mountain, reclining on the milky  white ocean of *tiruppāṟkadal*.

**minnu magara kuṇḍalangaḷ vil veesa** – the effulgence from his divine ear rings which are studded with gems, appears to be holding a lamp to drive away the darkness emanating from his dark, divine form. *veesa* – to spread out. The ear rings are in the shape of fish.

We will take up the next two lines of this prabandam in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 3. Tunniya taragaiyin

*tunniya tāragaiyin pēroḷi sēr āgāsam ennum vidānattin kīḻāl – iru sudarai mannum viḷakkāga ēṟṟi maṟi kadalum*

**Пословный перевод:**

tunniya tāragaiyin pēroḷi sēr āgāsam ennum – sky which is said to be lit up by the densely located stars; vidānattin kīḻāl – under the canopy; iru sudarai – the twin sources of radiance, viz., divine chakra and divine conch; mannum viḷakkāga ēṟṟi – like lighting a lamp which never gets put out

**Перевод:**

**tunniya tāragaiyin pēroḷi sēr āgāsam ennum vidānattin kīḻāl – under the divine canopy of pearls which is nothing but the luminous sky, having the splendour of stars which are close to each other. Alternatively, under the sky which is the divine canopy of pearls.  The reason for bringing in sky as a simile is to bring in clarity that the pearls are luminous, similar to stars.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**iru sudarai mannum viḷakkāga ēṟṟi** – Wouldn’t  cakrattāḻvān (divine discus) and sangattāḻvān (divine conch) go wherever [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) takes incarnation and goes? Lighting them as twin lamps which do not get put out ever. Alternatively, the term *irusudar* would also refer to *candrasūrya* (moon and sun) who are famous in samsāram (materialistic realm). The context here is emperumān reclining and contemplating about creating the worlds at the end of deluge. Since the worlds are yet to be created, how can there be a reference to sun and moon at this point of time? Since this does not gel properly, the earlier reference (to divine discus and divine conch) was made. Alternatively, reference to sun and moon could also be to hint about divine discus and divine conch. Since the poet ([tirumangai āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/)) came much later, there is no shortcoming in his bringing reference to sun and moon who had come ahead of him.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 4. Pannu tiraikkavari

*pannu tiraikkavari veesa nila mangai             4 tannai muna nāḷ aḷavitta tāmarai pōl manniya sēvadiyai vāniyangu tāragai mīn    5*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟi kadalum pannu tiraikkavari veesa – the king of ocean fanning with the covry (fly-whisk) of agitating waves; nilamangai tannai munanāḷ aḷavitta – measuring earth at an earlier time \[as trivikrama\]; tāmarai pōl manniya sēvadiyai – towards the lotus-like divine feet

**Перевод:**

**maṟi kadalum pannu tiraik kavari veesa – looking at the beauty of emperumān who is reclining on anantāḻvān \[ādiśēśan\], samudra rājan (king of oceans) is very happy and throws up the sand from ocean floor to its surface. This appears as if he is fanning emperumān with a covry (this is an implement used in temples for fanning emperumān during processions either inside or outside the temple). To emperumān it appears as if the huge waves are breaking up into smaller waves and these small waves gently massage his divine feet, thus inducing him to sleep. What is the reason for the ocean to fan him? We should rearrange the words of the verse as follows: manniya sēvadiyaip pannu tiraik kavari veesa – the ocean is fanning his divine feet. How are those divine feet?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**nilamangai tannai muna nāḷ aḷavitta tāmarai pōl** – it is the earth, which has been praised in bhūsūktam as “*bhūmir bhūmnā*” (she is referred to as *bhūmi* because of her greatness), which cannot be measured. ḍidn’t emperumān measure such inestimable earth with his divine, just then blossomed lotus-like feet! Lotus is taken as the simile here for emperumān’s divine feet on account of its softness, coolness, complexion, fragrance, beauty etc.

**muna nāḷ** – once upon a time; even though emperumān had measured the earth many yugas (ages) back, āḻvār is saying *nāḷ* (day) due to his ecstasy on thinking about it.

**aḷavitta tāmarai pōl** – it appears that it could be proved that his divine feet, because they had measured the earth earlier, had become sore and are reddish in colour.

**manniya sēvadiyai** – lotus was taken as a simile only because of a few qualities such as softness etc. After the lapse of a few hours lotus will lose all its qualities of softness, fragrance, complexion etc while the divine feet have their qualities lasting forever (*manniya*).

Now, the pāsuram discusses the way bhūmippirātti (bhūdēvi) massaged the divine feet.

We will see the next part of this prabandam, along with the meaning for the term *vāniyangu tāragai mīn ennum* in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6. Ennum malarppinaiyal

*vāniyangu tāragai mīn ennum malarppiṇaiyal ēynda maḻaik kūndal*

**Пословный перевод:**

vān iyangu tāragai mīn ennum – the stars which roam around in the sky; malar piṇaiyal ēynda – decorated with kadamba mālai (garland strung with a medley of flowers); maḻaik kūndal – having a flowing tress similar to clouds

**Перевод:**

**vāniyangu tāragai mīn ennum malarp piṇaiyal ēynda maḻaik kūndal – being the greatest among women, she needs to have black, long tress and a garland made with different types of flowers to decorate that tress. Does she have them? Her garland is made of tāragai (tārakā, stars) and various other stars. Her tress is nothing but the dark clouds which are invigorating, removing the ennui of the beholder, and containing within itself the garland mentioned above. If she unties and lets down her tresses once, her beloved will have all sorts of enjoyments to all his senses. tāragai – the primary twenty seven stars starting with aśvini and ending with rēvaty. mīn – all the other stars.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

For women, the most important parts are head and bosom. Head has been spoken of, above. Now, the pāsuram talks about her bosom…

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 7. Anna nadaiya anange

*anna nadaiya aṇangē(y) adiyiṇaiyai                         7*

**Пословный перевод:**

anna nadaiya – having a gait similar to that of a swan; aṇangu tān – bhūmippirātti who is a divine lady

**Перевод:**

**anna nadaiya – having a gait resembling swan’s, if she walks four steps ahead of him, as a prelude to their union, he will become a slave only to her style of walking and will not think of anything else with his heart. The gait is such that the attention will be only on it, until time lasts (forever).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**aṇangē** – Just like the old saying “*sūrumaṇangum deyvappeṇṇē*” since the word refers to a celestial lady, it implies that she will have a behaviour which is *aprākrutam* (different from normal; special, spiritual). Instead of referring to lowly states of pleasure derived from bosom and union, this refers to higher state of pleasure which is extraordinary. Alternatively, so far the pāsuram had spoken of her (bhūmippirātti’s) characteristics which are sweet for [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) while now, the word *aṇangē* refers to her divine characteristics which makes us, her children, to attain her. Just as vālmīki had mercifully said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 77-30 “*dēvatābhi samā*” (sīta is identical to dēvatās (deities)) and in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 55-26 “*trayāṇām bharatādīnām bhrātrūṇām dēvatā ca yā rāmasya ca manakāntā:* ” (one who was like a deity for his three younger brothers and who was sweet to rāma’s mind), with hanumān saying that sītāppirātti was fit to be attained by all four of them, āḻvār too mercifully states similar context here \[being sweet to emperumān and fit to be attained by us, her children\].

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam, along with the meaning for the term *adiyiṇaniyai*.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 7. Tennan uyar poruppum

*ennum ivaiyē mulaiyā vadivamainda*

**Пословный перевод:**

tennanuyar poruppum deyva vadamalaiyum ennum ivaiyē mulai A – the mountain of tirumālirum sōlai and tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai hill) as her bosom; vadivu amainda – having a fitting form

**Перевод:**

**tennan uyar poruppum deyva vadamalaiyum ennum ivaiyē mulaiyā – tirumālirunjōlai, which was enthusiastically claimed by cēra, cōḻa and pāṇḍiya kings of the south as theirs, even as south was abused by the ancient vaidikas (those who follow vēdam, sacred texts) and tiruvēngadamalai (tirumalai) which is the epitome of the greatness of simplicity such that even samsāris could attain it; it is emphatically said that these two mountains are spoken of as the tall, expansive and firm bosom of this great lady. While there are mountains such as mēru and himālaya which are taller, more expansive and firmer than these two mountains, why are these two mountains being spoken of as her bosom and not those greater mountains? emperumān has taken permanent residence only in tirumāḷirunjōlai and tiruvēngatam and hence these are mentioned as her bosom. tiruvāimoḻi 10.7.4 says “then koḷ tisaikkut tiladamāy ninṟa  tirumālirunjōlai” (tirumālirunjōlai stands as the identifying mark for the southern direction) and tiruvāimoḻi 3.3.7 says “paran senṟu sēr tiruvēngadam” (tirumalai where emperumān has reached), thus identifying these two mountains as divine abodes of emperumān. A ślōkam from śrī rāmāyaṇam has been explained here by periyavāccān piḷḷai to bring out the greatness of tirumālirunjōlai:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

It is only because of the greatness of tirumālirunjōlai that vālmīki bhagavān too cited this as a simile for citrakūta mountain in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 98-12  “*subhagaś citrakūtō’sau girirājōpamō giri*”. girirāja is an alternative name for tirumālirunjōlai. citrakūta is very beautiful like tirumālirunjōlai. *subhaga*:  – very beautiful. Even if it is beautiful, it may not be liked by its beloved. Is it like that? *girirājōpamō giri*: – it matches tirumālirunjōlai which is considered as bhūmippirātti’s bosom. Does its beloved reside in it just as tirumālirunjōlai’s beloved does? ya*smin vasati kākusta*: – śrī rāma has taken a liking to this  \[citrakūta\] since it is similar to the bosom of bhūmippirātti. *yadadyāstē mahātējā*: *– kubēra iva nandatē* – just as kubēra (one of the celestial entities, protector of northern direction) lives continuously in his caitraratam (chariot) with happiness, the greatly radiant śrī rāma too lives continuously in this citrakūta mountain which is inhabited by wild animals. *asau citrakūta*: *subhaga*: – there is nothing greater than such citraūta.

**vadivamainda** – the divine form of bhūmippirātti is matching the mountains which are like her bosom.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 8. Tannudaiya angaigalal

*tannudaiya angaigaḷāl tān tadavat tān kidandōrunniya yōgattu uṟakkam talaikkoṇḍa                                      8*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya am kaikgaḷāl adiyiṇaiyai tadava – gently massaging the divine feet \[of emperumān\] with her beautiful hands; kidandu – reclining eminently; ōr unniya yōgattu uṟakkam talaikkoṇḍa pinnai – after assuming the posture of reclining where he is thinking about protecting the world

**Перевод:**

**adiyiṇaiyai – the soft, divine feet which even she (bhūmippirātti) who is  extraordinary, would hesitate to touch**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tannudaiya am kaikaḷāl** – with her beautiful and great, divine hands which even sarvēśvaran (lord of all) has to obtain by carrying out penance

**tān tadava** – is it possible to sustain by asking someone else to drink for self? Is it possible to live by asking someone else to breath for self? Hence, pirātti is carrying out the service (of massaging [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)’s divine feet) with her own hands. She will sustain herself if she massages his divine feet by herself or else she will perish. Thus, sarvēśvaran is sleeping, with the ocean fanning him with covry (fan whisk) and bhūmippirātti massaging his divine feet.

**tān kidandu** – being omniscient by his nature, being without any bewilderment for any reason, he is now sleeping because of her mere touch, forgetting even that the ocean is fanning him and that she is massaging his divine feet.

**ōr unniya yōgattu uṟakkam talaikkoṇḍa pinnai** – after sleeping through his yōgam which is unique since he is fully aware even when he is sleeping. For people like us, the heart is already very dark. When we are awake, the external entities will be seen to an extent; once we close our eyes, even they will disappear and everything will become dark. For him ([emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/)), this is not the case. He is not one who sees with his eyes “*paśyatacakśu:*” (seeing with eyes). His closing his eyes, thus, is to enable his thoughts to develop further. The meaning of the term u*nnuvadu* is to meditate. *yōgattu uṟakkam* is to be aware while one is sleeping. *yōgattuṟakkam* – he does not sleep like people like us who would sleep when rajastamō guṇas (qualities related to passion and ignorance) overtake us. He is similar to what nammāḻvār had mercifully said in [tiruvāimoḻi 5.4.11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/07/thiruvaimozhi-5-4-11-uranguvan-pol/) “*uṟanguvān pōle yōgu seyginṟa perumān*” (emperumān who is thinking about the well-being of all even as he is sleeping). He is sleeping with the thought “what is the way in which these cētanas (sentient entities) would be able to come out of the ocean of samsāram?”

**talaikkoṇḍa pinnai** – the pāsuram now starts talking about entities who are created, now that the time for creation has arrived, just like flowers which are ready to bloom in their season.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9. Pinnait tan nabi

*pinnait tan nābi valayattup pēroḷisēr manniya tāmarai māmalarpūttammalar mēl       9*

**Пословный перевод:**

than nābi valayattu – in the region of his navel; pēr oḷi sēr – being with great radiance; manniya tāmarai māmalar pūttu – making the forever indestructible lotus flower to blossom; a malar mēl – atop that lotus flower

**Перевод:**

**than nābi valayattu – just as bhūmaṇḍalam (earth) is referred to as bhūvalayam (the region of earth), the region of navel present in emperumān’s divine form is referred to as nābhivalayam to signify its greatness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pēroḷi sēr manniya tāmarai māmalar pūttu** – the great radiance of such lotus (navel) which is far greater than the radiance of the entire world created by it.

**manniya tāmarai māmalar pūttu** – in the world, when the potter starts making pots from mud, the quantum of mud, which is the cause for the pots, will start decreasing as he goes on making pots, which are the effect. Ultimately, when all the pots have been made, there will be no mud at all. However, this lotus, which is the cause for creation of all the worlds, will remain without getting lessened, without getting destroyed and be present forever, when the worlds created by it start growing more and more.  When the word manniya (firmly, forever) is used as the adjective for lotus, this is the meaning which we get. Instead of this, if we rearrange the verse to get *manniya pēroḷi sēr tāmari māmalar pūttu* it would mean that the great radiance of this lotus is permanent (forever).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 10. Munnam tisaimuganai

*munnam tisaimuganait tān padaikka – maṟṟavanum munnam padaittanan nānmaṟaigaḷ ammaṟaidān               10*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnam tisaimuganait tān padaikka – even as he created brahmā during the time of creation (after deluge); maṟṟavanum – that brahmā too; munnam nānmaṟaigaḷ padaittanan – he obtained the four vēdas (from supreme being); ammaṟaidān – those vēdas

**Перевод:**

**ammalarmēl munnam tisaimuganait tān padaikka – during the time of sruśti (creation), emperumān created brahmā, whose four heads appeared like four lotuses, from his navel which itself was like a lotus.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**maṟṟavanum** – Was it not said that once sarvēśvara (lord of all) created brahmā, that brahmā wondered  about [emperumān](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/sriman-narayanan/) ”Is he sarvēśvara?”  and occupied the earth on the sides, above and below as mentioned in puruśa sūktam 1-5 “*tasmād virāda jāyata* I *virājō adi pūruśa*: I *sa jātō atyaricyata I paścāt bhūmimatō pura:* īī ” (brahmāṇḍam (a huge ovoid) formed out of that viṣṇu; brahmā formed inside that; once he was born, that four headed brahmā occupied earth on the sides, top and bottom)?

**munnam  padaittanan nānmaṟaigaḷ** – it would appear that brahmā created the four vēdas. vaidikas (followers of vēdam) have said “vēdam was not created by paramapuruśa (sarvēśvara); he too, just like one after awakening from sleep thinks of the earlier events, thought of the collection of vēdas which had been there before creation and instructed such vēdas to nānmugan (brahmā)” thus confirming that vēdas are not born out of the intellect of *puruśa* (sentient entity) and that they are *apauruśēyam* (not by puruśa). Thus, to say that brahmā created vēdas will go against the grain of what vaidikas have said. It will also go against the *vēda vacanam* (saying of vēda) as said in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli “*yō brahmāṇam vidadāti pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai . . .* ” (whosoever created brahmā initially, whosoever instructed him the vēdas …). How to interpret this without being antagonistic to vēda and vaidika saying? Just as it is commonly said that a person, after his birth, inherits wealth from his father, the word *padaitta* (as stated above, *munnam padaitta*) could be interpreted as both created and inherited.   Thus, here the meaning is that brahmā inherited the vēdas from sarvēśvaran, after he was created.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 11. Mannum aram porul

*mannum aṟam poruḷ inbam vīdu enṟu ulagilnanneṟi mēmbattana nānganṟē nānginum                        11pinnaiyadu pinnaip peyar tarum enbadōr*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannum aṟam poruḷ inbam vīdu enṟu – being firmly established as righteousness, wealth, love and mōkśam (attaining paramapadam or śīvaikuṇṭam); ulagil nal neṟi mēmbattana nāngu anṟē – these are the four ultimate benefits as said in this world as being attained through correct means; nānginum – among these four benefits; pinnai peyar tarum enbadu – after this physical body is gone \[after death\], it will get another body (as mentioned in śāstras)

**Перевод:**

**ammaṟaidān – those vēdas**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannum aṟam poruḷ inbam vīdu enṟu ulagil nanneṟi mēmbattana nānganṟē** – in the world, the four ultimate benefits of aṟam (righteousness), poruḷ (wealth), kāmam (love) and vīdu (mōkśam) are the ones which are realised through proper means. Initially it says *mannum aṟam* (firmly established righteousness). *aṟam* by itself means righteousness. Why qualify it with the word *mannum aṟam*? Righteousness is effected by poruḷ (wealth) and righteousness is the path for attaining kāmam and vīdu (love and mōkśam). Hence it is firmly established through these two forms. *poruḷ* is the benefit which is also referred to as *arttam*. *inbam* is the benefit which is cherished by [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) \[here it is the love towards sarvēśvaran and not the lower variety of physical love, indulged in by samsāris\]. *vīdu* is the fourth benefit which is spoken of by others.

**nānginum** – among these four benefits

**pinnaiyadu pinnai** – the benefit mentioned at the end (of the four) is referred to as *pinnaiyadu*. *pinnai* – it will keep getting delayed, saying “later”. There is none who has attained this benefit with this physical form. Could one talk derisively of the supreme benefit which is mentioned as readily available by śāstras (vēdams, sacred texts)? Since [āḻvār](http://acharyas.koyil.org/index.php/thirumangai-azhwar/) has the desire to enjoy it with his present physical form, he is mentioning this way. Could one term an experience which he gets after giving up this physical form, going to a very faraway place and enjoying it at some distant point of time as something worthy? No. Even that experience has only been heard from vēdas and vaidikas (followers of vēdas). There is none who has recounted it after seeing or experiencing it.

**peyar tarum enbadu** – giving up this physical form, acquiring another and enjoying . This is similar to one person carrying out penance and another person enjoying the benefit of that penance. Alternatively, we can group the verse as *pinnaip peyar tarum* and the meaning will be as said before.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 12. Ennum ivaiye

*ennum ivaiyē nugarndudalam tām varundi*

**Пословный перевод:**

ivaiyē nugarndu – eating these two; udalam varundi – tormenting the physical body

**Перевод:**

**ennum ivaiyē nugarndu udalam tām varundi – doesn’t one observe penance by eating the dried leaves and fruits which are without any juice? Since he is using the word ivaiyē, with the long syllable E at the end, it is clear that when one penance is completed and the next is begun, during the interregnum  too, normal food is not taken. This implies that the physical body should be continuously tormented.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**nugarndu** – while eating (the dried fruits and leaves), enjoying them. Since there is desire in the end benefit, one should have deep affection for the means too. As a corollary, while eating the dried leaves and juiceless fruits, he relishes them as if he were eating very tasty food items.

**udalam tām varundi** – after having seen the desired entity (arccai deity), tormenting the physical form which is to be enjoyed here.

**tām varundi** – in this world, due to one’s karma (past deeds), one could go to lowly places and torment the physical body. Alternatively, yama et al could take this body to their worlds and torment it. āḻvār says here that this person is tormenting the body himself and that it is not the torment created by someone else.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 12. Tonneriyai venduvar

*tonneṟiyai vēṇḍuvār vīḻkaniyum ūḻilaiyum                             12*

**Пословный перевод:**

ōr tol neṟiyai – that paramapadam (or arcirādi mārga, the way to reach paramapadam) is distinguished and is existing for a very long time; vēṇḍuvār – those who desire to attain; vīḻkaniyum ūḻ ilai ennum – fruits which had ripened and had fallen on their own as well as leaves which dried up

**Перевод:**

**ōr tonneṟiyai vēṇḍuvār – those who wish to get rid of samsāram (life in materialistic world) and desire the matchless joy of mōkśam. ōr – unique. ōr tol neṟiyai vēṇḍuvār – those who desire the unique, anciently spoken of mōkśam. The word neṟi refers to the path as well the place that the path leads to. It will not be a mistake to consider it as referring to the path itself. If that place is desired, vouldn’t the path which takes one to the place, also be desired? vēṇḍuvār – could there be those who desire such mōkśam, considering it too as an object! When there is a fruit on hand, will anyone look up at a fruit which has to be plucked with a tool? āḻvār is thus expressing his displeasure for that benefit (mōkśam).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

Next, he describes the means adopted by those who desire for attaining that benefit, mōkśam.

**vīḻ kaniyum** – the fruit which has dropped on its own. Since the fruit has fallen on its own, it is not a healthy fruit (in other words, fruit without any juice).

**ūḻilaiyum** – *ūḻ* refers to withering as well as to being ancient. This refers to a leaf which has fallen even when it was tender and has become dried up fully, as well as to an old leaf, which has withered after it has become old. The opinion in this is that here too, it is not healthy (it is a fully dried leaf, without any nourishment).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 13. Tunnum ilaik kurumbai

*tunnum ilaikkurumbait tunjiyum venjudarōn                                     13 mannum aḻal nugarndum vaṇ tadattin utkidandaum*

**Пословный перевод:**

tunnum ilaikkurumbait tunjiyum – lying inside hermitages which are close to each other; venjudarōn – sun who has cruel rays; mannum aḻal nugarndum – eating (the sun’s) hot rays which are established firmly; vaṇ tadattin utkidandum – immersing in the bountiful ponds

**Перевод:**

**tunnimilaik kurambait tunjiyum – sleeping inside hermitages which are worn with closely knitted leaves. kurambai – house; ilakkurambai – hermitage (thatched house made of palm tree leaves or coconut tree leaves). When lying inside these thatched houses, the dust falling from the house will enter the eyes and create great distress; yet sleeping with such distress inside these hermitages. The word tunjudal refers to both sleep and death. Since the sleep that he has inside these hermitages gives the pain which death gives, āḻvār is using such a word here.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**venjudarōn mannum aḻal nugarndum** – eating the hot rays of sun who emits such cruel rays. m*annum aḻal* – firm heat. Here, it refers to the fact that the hot rays of the sun enter his body and establish themselves firmly inside. This implies that the hot rays are not only the nourishing food for him, but also the sustaining water. He nurtures his body by eating wind and hot rays, not even taking water

**vaṇ tadattin utkidandum** – he is unfortunate not to enjoy the magnanimity of the pond. *vaṇ tadam* – magnanimous pond. The pond will beckon those who wish to enjoy it by telling them “please come”. Instead of entering the pond in the summer season, with womenfolk who he would desire, and engaging in water sports, he is immersing himself in the winter like a water bird, inside the pond, carrying out penance, having his body covered with moss.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 14. Innador tanmaiyaray

*innadōr tanmaiyarāy īngudalam vitteḻundu           14 tonneṟikkaṇ senṟār enappadum sol allāl innadōr kālattu inaiyār idu peṟṟār                            15 ennavum kēttaṟivadillai uḷadennil*

**Пословный перевод:**

innadōr tanmaiyar āy – thus, having such nature; īngu udalam vitteḻundu – leaving the physical body in this world and rising up; tol neṟikkaṇ senṟār enappadum sol allāl – other than saying that he reached an ancient place; innadu ōr kālattu idu peṟṟār – on a specific day, a particular person reached mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); ennavum kēttaṟivadillai – has not been heard, as told by someone

**Перевод:**

**innadōr tanmaiyarāy – being constantly amidst difficulties, acquiring that (sorrowful state) as his nature, even if he gets heavenly bliss as a result of his penance, can he relish it?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**īngudalam vitteḻundu** – in this world, wherein one can enjoy whatever one thinks of, he leaves behind his body, which he considers as a favourable entity having lived with it for such a long time, and rises up.

**tonneṟikkan senṟār enappadum sol allāl** – other than hearing that he had reached the ancient mōkśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)

**innadōr kālattu** – at a specific point of time

**inaiyār idu peṟṟār ennavum kēttaṟivadillai** – we have not heard from someone, through his (her) own words that he (she) has attained this benefit. While we have heard of proof that someone has reached mōkśam  through vēdas, we have not heard it in person, as proof. We have heard that “śukar has attained *mukti* (reached mōkśam)” or “*vāmadēva has attained mukti*” but we have not heard anyone say “I have reached mōkśam and enjoyed the *muktas* (those who have been liberated from samsāram and who have attained śrīvaikuṇṭam) there”. While ruśis have said that śukar and vāmadēva have attained *mukti* , can we not hear this from śukar or vāmadēva? This is not possible since there is none who has said “I had gone to śrīvaikuṇṭam; this is how it is there”.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 15. Mannum kadum kadiron

*mannum kadungadirōn maṇḍalattin nannaduvuḷ                     16 annadōr illiyinūdupōy vīdennum tonneṟikkaṇ senṟāraic collumingaḷ sollādē                             17*

**Пословный перевод:**

uḷadu ennil – in the event that (the mōkśa whose presence I deny completely) is present; mannum kadum kadirōn maṇḍalattin nal naduvuḷ – right at the central region of sun, whose rays are permanent and fierce; annadu ōr illiyin ūdu pōy – through the path of that subtle hole, which cannot be described by words; vīdu ennum tol neṟikkaṇ senṟārai sollumingaḷ – show those who have gone to that location which you call as mōkśam, and returned; sollādē – instead of showing such persons

**Перевод:**

**uḷadennil – āḻvār says that to start with, such a mōkśa does not exist at all. To say that mōkśam exists is an oxymoron, much similar to saying “Let him be drenched by fire”. Even if one were to say stubbornly that it exists, how is that mōkśa? How to reach it? āḻvār says . .**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannum kadum kadirōn maṇḍalattin nannduvuḷ** – one has to traverse through the central region of sun whose rays are everlasting and are such extremely hot that one cannot approach them. Even when sun is hundreds of thousands of miles away from him, his rays are so hot that he is not able to bear it. And, it seems that he has to not just go abutting that sun, but go right through his central region.

**annadōr illiyinūdu pōy** – when that *mukta* (soul liberated from samsāram and proceeding towards mōkśam) goes through, he cannot walk on a broad pathway but go through a narrow path.

**annadōr illi** – other than saying *annadu* (it is like that), it cannot be described clearly as it is that fine a hole (extremely narrow).

**vīdennum tonneṟikkaṇ senṟārai sollumingaḷ** – it seems that this *mukta* will be greeted by everyone, with the greatness of having the regal connection with [bhagavān](https://granthams.koyil.org/2013/03/12/thathva-thrayam-iswara-who-is-god/) appearing on him, and will be treated like royalty. It also appears that he (bhagavān) will wait till the samsāri’s (one who lives in samsāram, the materialistic realm) body falls and then will reveal his face, just as it is said in brahmā sūtram 4-2-16 “*hārdādanugrahīta*:” (the *mukta jīva* (the liberated soul) travels, knowing the path to take, having been blessed by paramātmā who is residing in his heart). It seems that *sarvēśvara* (lord of all) will hand over the *mukta* to a set of celestial entities called *ādivāhars* and that he himself would lead them, holding a lamp in his hand. Showing them the path \[to mōkśam\]. Let me see if you could show one person who says “I have gone to mōkśam which is being spoken of like this; I have seen this; I have enjoyed this” and has come back. At the least, if one who has gone there and returned, says this, that could be considered as proof. But there is no way that this could be done.

**sollādē** – Instead of praising this after revealing the proof (of the presence of mōkśa) either through *pratyakśa* (explicit means) or *anumāna* (inductive logic)  . . . .

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 16. Annade pesum

*annadē pēsum aṟivil siṟumanattukku āngu annavaraik kaṟpippōm yāmē adu niṟka                         18*

**Пословный перевод:**

annadē pēsum – repeating the oft-repeated saying (that śāstram indicates that there is mōkśam); aṟivu il siṟu manattu avarai – those who do not have rationale and who have narrow minds; kaṟpippōm yāmē – should we instruct them?; adu niṟka – let the discussion on mōkśam remain as it is

**Перевод:**

**annadē pēsum – is there any benefit in the opponent when he keeps repeating, on being asked any question “śukar attained mōkśam; vāmadēva attained mōkśam ”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**aṟivil . . . .**  – those who speak like this are fools who do not have the ability to make any distinction between right and wrong; they lack in the sense of rationale. They have no knowledge and keep parroting what others say.

**aṟivil siṟu manattu** – it is understood that they do not have knowledge at this point of time. If they have broad mindedness (keeping their mind open for receiving others’ points of view), they would at least develop knowledge in the future. But they have very narrow minds.

**āngu annavaraik kaṟpippōm yāmē** – Alas! It has become our duty to instruct and correct such people who are in a pitiable state. Instead of taking *madal* in the hand and roaming around (just like *namperumāḷ*) during *tiruvadyayanōtsavam* (a celebration in the month of *mārgaḻi*), bewildering those who have gathered on both sides of the street, it has fallen on us to instruct those ignorant people!

**adu niṟka** – let us not get engaged with that activity right now.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 17. Munna nan sonna

*munna nān sonna aṟattin vaḻi muyanṟa*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnam nān sonna aṟattin vaḻi muyanṟa annavar tām – those who carried out their activities in the path of righteousness that I had mentioned earlier

**Перевод:**

**munnam nān sonna aṟattin vahzi muyanṟa annavar – At the very first instance when I had mentioned about the four puruśārttams (end benefits) of aṟam, poruḷ, vīdu and inbam (righteousness, wealth, mōkśam and pleasure), when I had referred to the first puruśārtam of aṟam I had qualified it by saying mannum aṟam (righteousness which is remaining firmly) considering that it is the readily available means as well as the means to be carried out for attaining śrīvaikuṇṭam. I had only compared it with the puruśārtam of wealth that one could obtain in this world and said that it is well established. In reality, even that puruśārtam is not firm. Not knowing this, some ignorant people might have thought that this is very firmly established. Not only is it infirm, but it will also create a fear of “When will I fall down?” śrīviṣṇu purāṇam 6-6-50 says “na kēvalam dvijaśrēśta narakē du:kka patdati: I svargē’pi pāta bhītasya kśayiṣṇōr nāsti nirvruti: īī” (Oh the head of brāhmaṇas! Do not think that it is only in narakam that one undergoes a series of difficulties; for the person who is in svargam, since the count of his virtues is coming down, he will keep carrying the fear of ‘When will I be pushed down?’ and thus svargam is also not permanent). To emphasise this meaning, I will tell you this more lucidly. Listen to me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**munnam nān sonna aṟattin vaḻi muyanṟa annavar** – those who followed the dharma śāstram (path of righteousness) and carried out their activities in that path. Since it is referred to as the path of righteousness, it reveals the severity of following activities such as jyōtiśtōma yāgam etc (ritual of observing jyōtistōma, for reaching *svargam*). If one misses by a hair’s breadth, it will lead to death. If there is any mistake in pronouncing the *mantras* or if there is any shortcoming in observing the means, one would be later born as a *brahmarakśas* (a demon).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 18. Annavar tam

*annavar tām kaṇḍīrgaḷ āyirakkaṇ vānavar kōn                            19*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyiram kaṇ vānavarkōn pon nagaram pukku – reaching the beautiful land of indra, who has a thousand eyes

**Перевод:**

**annavar tām kaṇḍīrgaḷ – those who painstakingly composed that righteousness**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**āyirakkaṇ vānavar kōn** – the pleasures of such dēvalōkam (land of celestials) are such that indra, who is the leader of all those who go from here to that place, needs a thousand eyes to enjoy them .

**ponnagaram pukku** – being the greatest in that land of celestials, reaching that capital city *amarāvati* which is such that one would think “Living in this is bissful”

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 19. Ponnagaram pukku

*ponnagaram pukku amarar pōṟṟiseppap pongoḷi sēr konnavilum kōḷarimāt tān sumanda kōlam sēr               20*

**Пословный перевод:**

amarar pōṟṟi seppa – to be praised by the celestial entities; pongu oḷi sēr – having a radiance which keeps growing; kol navilum kōḷarimāt tān sumandu kōlam sēr – being beautiful and supported by a strong lion which talks slanderously about the activity of killing

**Перевод:**

**amarar pōṟṟi seppa – just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-8 “mudiyudai vānavar muṟai muṟai edehir koḷḷa” (nityasūris, who are crowned with the title of servitorśip, welcome muktātmās in the proper way), the celestial entities (in svargam) praise those who reach svargam by exclaiming “Such a great person is coming who, when he was on the earth, was engaged with accumulating wealth, and desirous of this puruśārtam (ultimate benefit), he has carried out several penances, torturing his body and observing activities in line with that! ”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pongoḷi sēr** – unlike entities on earth which lose their lustre as days pass on, the entities in svargam have their radiance growing by the day, since it is the land of *puṇyapalam* (result of carrying out virtues).

**kol navilum kōḷarimā** – lions which (have been sculpted) appear to be strong and ready to pounce and kill those who are approaching

**tān sumanda** – appearing as if they are live lions which are supporting, with affection in their hearts

**kōlam sēr** – being fit with several courtyards which have formed in a natural way

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 20. Manniya singasanattin

*manniya singāsanattin mēl vāṇedungaṇ kanniyarālitta kavarippodi aviḻndu āngu                              21 inniḷam pūndenṟal iyanga marungirunda*

**Пословный перевод:**

manniya singa āsanattin mēl – on top of the throne which is staying firmly (being seated); vāḷ negungaṇ – having eyes which are sharp like a sword; kanniyarāl itta – fanned by celestial women; kavarippodi aviḻndu – the bundle of covry (fly-whisk) blossoming (nearby); in iḷa pū tenṟal iyanga – sweet, gentle and fragrant southerly wind wafting in; marungu irunda – being nearby

**Перевод:**

**manniya singāsanattin mēl – on top of the throne, which, because it allows one to sit on it a bit longer than it would on the earth, is termed as staying firmly.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vāḷ nedungaṇ** – this speaks about the pleasures that one has with the celestial ladies.

**vāḷ kaṇ** – those who have eyes which will make the people facing them to fall down, by a mere glance, just as a sword would do.

**nedungaṇ** – even if one enjoys them forever, they will appear as if they have fresh beauty in their eyes.

**kanniyarāl itta kavarip podi aviḻndu** – the covry (fly whisk) which has been used by such ladies to fan, loosened out

**āngu inniḷam pūndenṟal iyanga** – with a soft southerly breeze, carrying sweetness, gentleness and fragrance, wafting softly in that place

**marungirunda** – there are other celestial ladies who are at proximity to them. Just as one cannot oppose the flow of water, one cannot get close to such ladies who have an abundance of sweetness and can only enjoy them by the side.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 21. Minnanaiya nun marungul

*minnanaiya nuṇmarungul melliyalār veṇ muṟuval                            22munnam mugiḻtta mugiḻ nilā vandarumbaannavar tam mānōkkam uṇḍāngu aṇimalar sēr                             23*

**Пословный перевод:**

minnanaiya nuṇmarungul melliyalār – tender women having waist as subtle as lightning; munnam mugiḻtta – revealed at the beginning itself; veṇ muṟuval – (their) white teeth; mugiḻ niḷa vandu arumba – young moon, spreading; annanvar tam – women-folk like that; mān nōkkam uṇḍu – enjoying their look which is like the look of a doe; āngu – at the same time; aṇimalar sēr – with abundance of beautiful flowers

**Перевод:**

**min anaiya nuṇ marungul – those who have their waists which would make people who look at them to cry out “Take care! You might fall down”, being thinner than lightning.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mel iyalār** – they have tenderness as a natural attribute

**munnam mugiḻtta veṇ muṟuval mugiḻ nilā vandu arumba** – their white smile, which is like a just rising moon spreading its radiance, would already indicate their opinion.

**munnam mugiḻtta** – the opinion is that their smile is already giving out information which is going to be mentioned next, of the activity of plucking flowers from orchards.

**annavar tam mān nōkkam uṇḍu** – enjoying the look of such persons whose eyes are like a doe’s

Next, the pāsuram talks about an orchard which forms instantly in front of the eyes due to the result of virtues (in *svargam*) and plucking of flowers and playing as mentioned in the earlier line through the reference to smile.

**aṇi malar sēr** – the orchard is full of flowers which are decorative for that world

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 22. Ponniyal karpagattin

*ponniyal kaṟpagattin kādudutta mādellāmmanniya mandāram pūtta maduttivalai                                  24innisai vaṇḍamarum sōlaivāy mālai sērmanniya māmayil pōl kūndal maḻaittadangaṇ                          25minnidaiyārōdum viḷaiyādi vēṇḍidattu*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon iyal kaṟpagattin kādu udutta – surrounded by a forest of golden hued kaṟpaga trees (wish-fulfilling, celestial tree); mādu ellām manniya mandāram pūtta madu tivalai – in the drops of honey secreting from the flowers of pārijāta trees (another celestial tree, with fragrant flowers), which are seen on all sides; in isai vaṇḍu amarum sōlai vāy – in the orchards, inside which beetles are humming with sweet music; mālai sēr manniya māmayil pōl kūndal – being decked with flowers and having tresses which look like the long feathers of peacocks, fitting well; maḻai tadam kaṇ – having expansive eyes which are cool and dark like rain bearing cloud; min idaiyarōdum – with celestial damsels who have their waists slender like lightning; vēṇḍidattu viḷaiyādi – playing joyfully in places which they desire

**Перевод:**

**ponniyal kaṟpagam – being the apt, golden kaṟpagam for the golden town (kaṟpaga is a wish-fulfilling tree seen in celestial places). If the text is manniya kaṟpagam it would mean well established (firmly rooted) kaṟpaga trees.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kaṟpagattin kādudutta** – it is an attribute for the orchard. An orchard, surrounded by *kaṟpaga* trees.

**mādellām manniya mandāram pūtta maduttivalai** – in the drops of honey which are falling from the mandāra trees (pārijāta tree unique to *svargam*) which are seen everywhere.

**innisai vaṇḍamarum sōlai** – it is an orchard where beetles are humming sweet songs. Since they are immersed in honey, the beetles are not visible; however their presence is detected by the music that they keep humming.

**sōlaivāy** – in the orchard.

Next, the pāsuram describes the tresses of the women in that place.

**mālai sēr** – tress decorated with flowers.

**manniya māmayil poḷ kūndal** – they have tresses which resemble the huge feathers of peacocks, which are present naturally.

**mālai sēr . . . . kūndal** – this shows the decorations which they have done to their tresses.

**maḻaittdangaṇ** – these women have eyes which are dark and cool, like rain bearing clouds and are expansive.

**minnidaiyārōdum viḷaiyādi** – playing with these women who have waists which are like lightning.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 23. Mannum manittalattu

*mannu maṇittalattu māṇikka manjariyin                                          26 minnin oḷi sēr paḷingu viḷimbu adutta mannum pavaḷakkāl semponsey maṇḍabattuḷ                                     27 anna nadaiya arambaiyar tam kai vaḷartta innisai yāḻ pādal kēttu inbuṟṟu iruvisumbil                                              28*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannum maṇi talattu – having a floor studded with distinguished gem stones; māṇikka manjariyin – having flowery decorations made with ruby stones; minnin oḷisēr paḷingu viḷimbu adutta – having cornice (similar to tiṇṇai that we see in traditional houses) made with crystals which have radiance similar to lightning; mannum pavaḷakkāl – having glittering pillars of red coral; semponsey maṇḍabattuḷ – inside the hall made of reddish gold (pure gold); annam nadaiya arambaiyar tam kaivaḷartta – being nurtured by caressing with their own hands, by celestial women who have a gait similar to that of a swan; in isai yāḻ pādal – the song from vīṇai (a stringed, traditional musical instrument) which has sweet notes; kēttu inbuṟṟu – hearing it and feeling joyful

**Перевод:**

**Now the pāsuram describes the hall in the court of celestials through the sequence vēṇḍidattu . . . . kēttu inbuṟṟu.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vēṇḍidattu** – in the places which they desired

Starting from  *mannu maṇittalattu* until *semponsey*, these are attributes for the court-hall.

**mannu maṇittalattu** – it has a floor which is studded with gem stones which are distinguished.

**māṇikka manjariyin** – the decorations are made up by stringing them with rubies instead of flowers.

**minnoḷi sēr** – the hall is full of the effulgence emitted by these ruby decorations.

**paḷingu viḷimbu adutta** – it has cornices which are made with crystal stones

**mannum pavaḷakkāl** – since it is a hall in which great celestial danseuses such as rambā, mēnakā, tilōttamā, ūrvasi et al relax by reclining on them, it has several pillars made of shiny red coral gems.

**semponsey maṇḍabattuḷ** – inside the hall made of purest form of gold

**anna nadai arambaiyar tam kaivaḷartta** – the music which has been nurtured by the celestial ladies with their own hands, just as they nurture plants by watering them with their hands.

**innisai** – it is the music of the celestial world, far superior to the music that we hear on earth.

**yāḻ pādal kēttu inbuṟṟu** – enjoying that music emanating from the musical instrument *yāḻ* (similar to modern day vīṇā).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 24. Mannum malai tavalum

*mannu maḻai tavaḻum vāṇilā nīṇmadi tōyminnin oḷi sēr visumbūrum māḷigai mēl                                             29mannu maṇi viḷakkai mātti maḻaikkaṇṇār*

**Пословный перевод:**

iruvisumbil mannu maḻai tavaḻum – in the expansive ethereal layer, with the region of clouds fitting well; vān nilā nīḷ madi tōy – with the huge, beautiful moon fitting well; minnin oḷi sēr – having the lustre of lightning; visusmbu ūrum māḷigai mēl – in the aerial vehicle which is roaming around in the sky; maḻai kaṇṇār – women with cool eyes; mannu maṇi viḷakkai mātti – hanging the lamps of rubies, which will never get put out

**Перевод:**

**Now onvards, until the verse innamum māndiyiruppar, the pāsuram talks of the lowly pleasures which he enjoys with the celestial women.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**iru visumbil** – in the expansive world of celestials

**mannu maḻai tavaḻum** – the clouds will always settle on the mansion, without any distinction between summer and monsoon time. They are the rest houses built for the clouds to take rest when they roam around, laden with water, in order to precipitate as rain at various locations, as mentioned in tiruviruttam pāsuram 32 “*uyiraḷippān māgangaḷ ellām tirindu nannīrgaḷ sumandu num āgangaḷ nōva ….* ” (in order to nurture life, you are roaming in all the skies, taking in good water (in order to precipitate as rain on earth), hurting your bodies in the bargain . . . .)

**vān nilā nīṇmadi tōy** – it is the resting place for moon also, which is continuous in terms of sweetness, and having the shining moon light

**minnin oḷi sēr** – having a radiance which will be blinding on the eyes

**visumbūrum** – the mansion will be located on  highlands such that it could be said that those places where the clouds and moon are resting could be termed as troughs. Thus it will have both crests and troughs.

**māḷigai mēl mannu maṇi viḷakkai mātti** – hanging rubies, after removing any surface defect on them, atop the mansion, to emit light like lamps, firmly.

**maḻaik kaṇṇār** – those who have eyes which are like rain-bearing clouds, being dark and cool. The eyes are so cool that if they see a person once, that person will have his teeth clattering for the rest of his life.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 25. Pannu vicittirima

*pannu  vicittiramāp  pāppadutta paḷḷi mēl                           30 tunniya  sālēgam  sūḻkadavam tāḷ  tiṟappa annam  uḻakka  nerindukka vāṇeelac                                        31 cinna  naṟundādu  sūdi ōr mandāram tunnu  naṟumalarāl  tōḷ kottik  kaṟpagattin                            32*

**Пословный перевод:**

vicittiramā pāppadutta – being spread out expansively; pannu paḷḷimēl – on the mattress which is to be extolled; tunniya sālēgam sūḻ kadavam tāḷ tiṟappa – with the doors of the closely fabricated windows opening out; annam uḻakka nerindu ukka vāḷ neelam naṟu cinna tādu sūdi – wearing the grains of fragrant, minute pollens from the blue water lily which had been crushed by the stamping of swans; ōr mandāram tunnu naṟumalarāl tōḷ kotti – decorating the shoulders with fragrant flowers which are from the distinguished mandāram tree (a celestial flower bearing tree)

**Перевод:**

**pannu vicittiramāp pāppadutta paḷḷi mēl – on the expansive mattress  which  has been spread out, with different colours, to be extolled upon.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tunniya sālēgam** – window with closely drilled holes in it. The holes have been drilled such that they would allow just sufficient volume of air to pass through, ensuring that it does not become a huge flow of air.

**sūḻ kadavam** – the doors for the windows, which are surrounding them.

**tāḷ tiṟappa** – they will open out of their own, when the air desires to flow in.

The pāsuram now describes the southerly wind which is entering the window as if it were a *mahāpuruś* (a great person).

**annam uḻakka nerindu ukka** – swans enter water bodies where blue Indian lilies are blossoming. They go so swiftly through the flowers that the honey (in the flowers), the pollen dust and pollen grains get ground to a fine paste, just like mud is agitated, and they form a fine powder.

**vāḷ neelac cinna naṟundādu sūdi** – just as kings don themselves with garlands, wearing such powdered pollens of white lily flowers as a garland

**ōr mandāram tunnu naṟu malarāl tōḷ kotti** – wearing the tightly strung, unique mandāra flower as a garland, just like kings would wear, on the shoulders.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 26. Mannu malarvay

*mannu malarvāy maṇivaṇḍu pintodara inniḷam pūndenṟal pugundu īngu iḷa mulai mēl                       33 nannaṟum sandanaccēṟu uḷpularttat tāngarum sīr minnidai mēl kaivaittu irundu ēndiḷamulai mēl                        34*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṟpagattin mannu malar vāy maṇivaṇḍu pintodara – being followed by beetles which had settled on the flowers of kalpaka vrukśa (celestial, wish-fulfilling tree); in iḷa pū tenṟal – the most enjoyable southerly wind; īngu pugundu – wafting inside wherever these people are going; iḷa mulai mēl – on top of the young bosom; nal naṟu sandana sēṟu uḷ pulartta – drying up a fragrant sandal wood paste; tānga aru sīr min idai mēl kai vaittu irundu – keeping the hand on the (subtle) difficult-to-bear waist which is like a beautiful lightning; ēndu iḷa mulai mēl – on top of the well-risen bosom

**Перевод:**

**kaṟpagattin mannum malarvāy maṇivaṇḍu pintodara – the beetles have given in writing that they have been enslaved by the honey dripping from the flowers of kaṟpaga tree and are residing permanently inside these flowers. Such beetles, forgetting even that, are totally won over by the sweet fragrance of the southerly winds and are following the wind, humming continuously, just like court poets follow their king wherever he goes, singing paeans on him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**inniḷam tenṟal īngu pugundu** – being sweet to touch and being soft, the gentle southerly wind, carrying fragrance of flowers, enters the place where they are present

**iḷa mulai lēl nannaṟum sandanaccēru uḷ pulartta** – the bosom of celestial ladies are such that they will even bewilder sages who have given up attachment to lust and make them fall head over heels. The wind, spoken of in the earlier line, would gently fan the sandalwood paste smeared on such bosom and dry it.

**tāngarum sīr min idai mēl kaivaittu** – the celestial damsel , whose beautiful, lightning-like waist is so slender that it would find it difficult to bear the bosom on top, allows this person (who has reached svargam) to keep his hand on the waist and enjoy her completely.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 27. Pon arumbu aram

*ponnarumbu āram pulamba agam kuḻaindu āngu inna uruvin imaiyāt tadam kaṇṇār                                                                 35 annavar tam mānōkkam uṇḍāngu aṇimuṟuval innamudam māndiyiruppar iduvanṟē                                                          36*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon arumbu āram pulamba – chains (worn by the celestial damsel) make sounds; āngu – in that place; agam kuḻanidu – with the heart softening; inna uruvin imaiyā tadam kaṇṇār – having such great, expansive eyes; annavar tam – such celestial damsels’; mān nōkkam uṇḍu – enjoying the look of a doe’s eyes; āngu – along with that; aṇi muṟuval innamudam māndi iruppar – they would have consumed the nectar of beautiful smile

**Перевод:**

**pon arumbu āram pulamba – the chains made of golden coins are making noise. During union, since the bodies are in embrace, the chains also move and make a noise. The chains are made of three or five rows of coins and the noise which they make is termed as pulambal (lamenting).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**arumbu āram pulamba** – Even during union, the chains cannot be removed as they are such beautiful. It appears that they (the chains) are saying “āren’t we a decoration for her!” even as they appear to be blossoming from her bosom. āḻvār is indirectly hinting at the pleasures of union.

**agam kuḻaindu** – agreeing to that union, with the heart softening.

**āngu** – in that celestial land \[svargam\]

**inna uruvin imaiyāt tadam kaṇṇār** – **innavuru** – their inexplicable beauty is similar to gold which has been melted in the furnace \[and drawn to form jewels\]

**imaiyāt tadam kaṇṇār . . .** – looking at the sight of those damsels who have  beautiful eyes  like those of a doe. The term *imaiyā* does not refer to the nature of celestial people who do not close their eyelids as a natural behaviour but their inability to close the eyes due to the beauty of the object being seen. Once the food of their vision is digested, they let out a smile to blossom from their lips. The visitors would drink the juice of their lips and satiate their thirst.

**iruppar** – This shows that due to the excessive pleasures, they are unable to let go of this and will hold on to it.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 28. Anna arattin

*anna aṟattin payanāvadu oṇporuḷum anna tiṟattadē ādalāl kāmattin                                           37 mannum vaḻi muṟaiyē niṟṟu nām mānōkkin anna nadaiyār alarēsa ādavar mēl                                                       38*

**Пословный перевод:**

anna aṟattin payan āvadu idu anṟē – this is the benefit that one gets for that puruśārtam darmam (which I had spoken of, earlier); oṇ poruḷum – the benefit for the distinguished arta (wealth) puruśārtam; anna tiṟattadē – it is as lowly and as temporary (as this); ādalāl – for both the puruśārtams aṟam (darmam or righteousness) and poruḷ (wealth), since kāmam (love) is the benefit; kāmattin – for that puruśārtam kāmam; mannum vaḻi muṟaiyē – in the path of love towards bhagavān which is the well established path; nām niṟṟum – we will be firmly involved with; mān nōkkin annam nadaiyār – women who have eyes like a doe and gait like a swan; alar ēsa – to be abused (by worldly people); ādavar mēl – on the male (towards men)

**Перевод:**

**idu anṟē anna aṟattin payan āvadu – īsn’t this (insignificantly) small benefit, the end result of the activities of darmam which one observes so arduously? īsn’t this similar to getting a handful of grains after putting in so much of efforts? In other words, the benefit is not commensurate with the efforts.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**oṇ poruḷum anna tiṟattadē** – Even the benefit of the lustrous poruḷ (wealth) is as lowly and as weak as this (what we have just seen). Since jīvātmā (who toils hard towards such lowly benefits) has forsaken ācāryas who are capable of making him attain *paramporuḷ* (supreme being, emperumān) and thinks that wealth is the benefit which is most apt for him, āḻvār calls such *poruḷ* as *oṇ poruḷ* (shining wealth) more as per the jīvātmā’s view rather than his own view.

**kāmattin mannum vaḻi muṟaiyē niṟṟum nām** – Let us remain steadfast on the path of love towards bhagavān which is well established and not on the path of lust which is the lowly benefit one accrues out of following bodily lust, just like the paths of righteousness and wealth.

**nām** – we, who have given up on everything other than the path of love (towards bhagavān).

**maṇ nōkkin anna nadaiyār** – while the males can engage in *madal* based on the beauty of their (the damsels’) eyes and gait, how strange is it that the women wish to engage in *madal* on the men! This is the view of tamiḻars (people living in tamiḻ speaking land)

**alar ēsa** – the view of tamiḻars is also that people can talk ill of men for engaging with madal on women but such reproach should not be pointed towards women who wish to engage with *madal* on men.

**ādavar mēl** – on the male. The opinion of tamiḻars is that *nāyaki* (the leading lady), who is subservient to the *nāyakan* (the leading man), should not cause any reproach for him.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 29. Mannum madalurar

*mannu madalūrār enbadōr vāsagamum tennuraiyil kēttaṟivaduṇḍu adanai yām teḷiyōm                          39 mannum vadaneṟiyē vēṇḍinōm vēṇḍādār*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannum madal ūrār enbadu ōr vāsagamum – a saying “one should not engage in madal to sustain (one’s life)”; then uraiyil kēttu aṟivadu uṇḍu – we have heard of in tamiḻ language; nām adanai teḷiyōm – we do not consider that as having any clarity (it is not apt); mannum vadaneṟiyē vēṇḍinōm – (in this matter of engaging in madal) we will follow the method prescribed in great samskruta literature; vēṇḍādār – those who do not like (to cross the limit as mentioned in samskruta texts) – what type of people are they?

**Перевод:**

**mannum madal ūrār – they will not engage in madal, even if they have to sustain their lives. The opinion of tamiḻars is that for the leading lady, her svarūpam (basic nature) is more important than even her life.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**enbadu ōr vāsagamum** – a saying (in tamiḻ) which expresses this opinion (as mentioned above)

**ōr vāsagamum** – this implies that this saying, which does not seem to know the greatness of love, exists. How amazing!

**tennurai . . .**  – can a saying which is seen in a language spoken by lowly people, in the material world, show the greatness of love? \*\*

**urai** – it is just a saying; it does not have deep meaning.

**adanai nām teḷiyōm** – Even if this saying has any meaning, I am not able to decipher it.

**mannum vadaneṟiyē vēṇḍinōm** – We would only subscribe to the view expressed in samskruta literature such as śrī rāmāyaṇam that it is *puruśārtam* (ultimate benefit) for a woman, who has separated from their beloved, to engage in *madal*.

**vēṇḍādār** – those who follow the southern (tamiḻar) methodology (of not engaging in *madal*), rejecting the samskruta method do not know at all, what love is.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

\*\* – Due to his urge in engaging with madal, āḻvār is speaking ill of tamiḻ language and the culture of tamiḻars. It is not that āḻvār is manifesting any hatred towards tamiḻ. Even though he intended to engage in madal, he too did not complete the task of engaging in madal. Thus, tamiḻ and tamiḻars’ culture alone succeeded in the end.

(The above-mentioned is the note from arumpadavurai)

## 30. Tennan podiyil

*tennan podiyil seḻum sandanak kuḻambin                                   40 annadōr tanmai aṟiyādār āyan vēy innisai ōsaikku irangādār māl vidaiyin                                                  41 mannu maṇi pulamba vādādār peṇṇai mēl*

**Пословный перевод:**

tennan podiyil sezum sandanak kuḻambinannadu ōr tanmai aṟiyādār – those who do not know that property of the sandalwood paste made from sandalwood trees which grow in the podigai malai (podigai mountain range) in the kingdom of pāṇḍyas; āyan vēy innisai ōsaikku irangādār – those who are not disheartened after hearing the sweet music played by the shepherd on his flute; māl vidaiyin – the bull which comes infatuated (with the cow); mannu maṇi pulamba vādādār – those who are not dispirited after hearing the great sound of the bell (tied to that bull’s neck)

**Перевод:**

**tennan – despite living in the country of tamiḻars, not knowing its greatness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tennan podiyil** – those do not know that sandalwood is a botanical product grown in the podigai mountains of tamiḻ nādu (the country of tamiḻars).

**seḻum sandanak kuḻambu** – the paste of that sandalwood which will make even a totally renounced sage to feel intoxicated. It is the kind of sandalwood paste which, when applied even on an inanimate piece of iron, will make it feel freshened  and capable to dance.

**kuḻambin annadōr tanmai aṟiyādār** – those who do not know that if this paste were applied on a lady who has separated from her beloved, it will make her body to boil over (sandalwood is by nature a material which cools down the body; however, in this case, it generates so much of heat that the body boils over).

**annadōr tanmai** – those who do not know that once its properties are described, the mouth will become ulcerated.

**āyan vēy innisai ōsaikku irangādār** – they are people who will not melt, after listening to the music played by shepherd on his flute,  saying “Oh! How sad is it that we are not able to enjoy this music by being together with our beloved!” Their hearts are so hardened that instead of thinking of their beloved, they will think that it is a lullaby and fall asleep. The vēdic chanting of brāhmaṇas, the singing of pāṇas (a class of people who are specialised in singing) and the music played on the flute by shepherds will be engrossing for those who listen.  However, these people (those who do not get engrossed) are the ones who cannot appreciate such music, which, like brahmāstra (a weapon), will never miss its mark.

**innisai ōsai** – this is like an arrow dipped in poison. Arrow is the sound and poison is the music. Why does it become a poison? Since it is reminding the lady of her beloved’s sweet words and torturing because of that.

**mālvidaiyin mannu maṇi pulamba vādādār** – **mālvidai** – bull which is infatuated (with cow). These are the people who will not wilt, just like a tender leaf will wilt on being torched by fire, upon hearing the continuous ringing of the bell from the bull. Why should they wilt? While the bull is coming towards the cow for uniting, the cow is standing without showing any interest, on looking at the bull approaching it. The bull, on its own, falls atop the cow to unite with it, due to which the bell, tied to its neck, is ringing. This will remind the *nāyaki* about the non-arrival of the *nāyakan* even though she is agreeable, and will torment her.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 31. Pinnum avvanril

*pinnum avvanṟil pedai vāyc ciṟukuralukku                                         42 unni udal urugi naiyādār umbar vāy*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṇṇaimēl pinnum – hooking its beak with its male’s, on top of the palm tree; avvanṟil pedai vāy siṟu kuralukku – hearing the feeble voice from the mouth of the female love-bird; unni – thinking back on the union with their males; udal urugi naiyādār – those who do not have their physical forms destroyed, by melting; umbar vāy – in the sky

**Перевод:**

**peṇṇai mēl pinnum avvarnṟil pēdai vāyc ciṟukuralukku unni udal urugi naiyādār – two love birds, a male and a female, are sitting on top of the palm tree and each has caught the beak of the other bird, during their act of uniting. The female bird is making some feeble sounds. On hearing these sounds, instead of reminiscing about how they had united with their beloved and how they were making these noises, there are those who will not melt in this situation. When there is a flood, it will rip open both its banks and flow. In the same way, the body, on reminiscing about the moments spent with the beloved, will become very weak and get destroyed. If they have to start speaking, they will have to take rest ten times, before completing the first word. This is how weak they will be. But these people will not have such torments.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 32. Tunnu madiyugatta

*tunnu madiyugutta tūnilā nīṇeruppil                                         43 tammudalam vēvat taḷarādār kāmavēḷ mannum silaivāy malar vāḷi kōtteyyap                                            44 ponnedu vīdi pugādār tam pūvaṇaimēl*

**Пословный перевод:**

tunnu madi ugutta – emitted by moon which has dense rays; tū nilā nīḷ neruppil – in the huge fire, also known as pure moon; tam udalam vēvat taḷarādār – those who do not weaken such that their bodies are burnt; kāmavēḷ – manmada (cupid); silai vāy mannum malar vāḷi kōttu eyya – even when manmada strings arrows of flowers on his bow and shoots (affirming that engaging with madal is the puruśārtam (benefit) now); pon nedu vīdi pugādār – those who do not enter the beautiful, long streets; tam pū aṇai mēl – on their bed of flowers

**Перевод:**

**tunnu madi ugutta tū nilā nīḷ neruppil – moon, which has densely packed rays, emitted pure moonlight which was like cruel fire.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**madi ugutta tūnilā nīḷ neruppu** – unable to bear the heat of the rays, she thinks that the moon too is emitting out the hot rays, instead of keeping them with it.

**tūnilā** – the moon becomes pure, after the hot rays have been emitted.

**nīḷ neruppil** – it is a fire which does not die down even after rendering to ashes, all the materials which were to be burnt.

**neruppu – nīḷ neruppu** – one could say that fire is very cold when compared to the heat emitted by this moon. Fire becomes moon and moon becomes infinite fire. Fire will burn down only the body; the infinite fire *nīḷ neruppu* is capable of burning out the inner soul too and hence, she is calling it as *nīḷ neruppu*. Normal fire would be put down by water. This infinite fire cannot be put out by water. *nīr vaṇṇan* (emperumān who has the complexion of water) is required for putting out this fire.

**tam udalam vēvat taḷarādār** – it is not a body which has been borrowed. It is their own bodies (*tam udalam*). When it is being burnt, how is it possible to stand and not be affected! Unlike nammāḻvār who had mentioned mercifully in [tiruvāimoḻi 8-8-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/04/thiruvaimozhi-8-8-3-unarvil-umbar/) “*uṇarvum uyirum udambum maṟṟu ulappinavum paḻudēyām uṇarvaippeṟa*” (knowledge about worldly matters, vital air (life), body, all other entities relating to primordial matter are all wasted), these people are not the ones who have attained the knowledge to hate their bodies; these people nurture their bodies.

**kāmavēḷ silai vāy mannum malarvāḷi kōtteyya pon nedu vīdi pugādār** –  unlike those people who, as soon as cupid shoots fitting arrows of flowers from his bow at them, will walk down the beautiful and long streets with madal in their hands, they will stay indoors, after latching their houses.

**pon vīdi** – beautiful street; the street will welcome those who step on it, engaging with madal, beckoning them repeatedly, manifesting its beauty.

**nedu vīdi** – the street is so long that one could engage with madal for a *kalpa kālam* (day of brahmā).

**pugādār** – they will not enter the street at all, even if *manmada* keeps shooting arrows of flowers at them. They lack in the knowledge to enter the street which is like a fort of stones.

**tam pūvaṇaimēl . . . ponnaṇaiyār** – they are the fortunate ones who are capable of lying on the bed of flowers, imagining the touch of northerly winds on their bodies as the touch of their beloved’s hand.

**tam pūvaṇaimēl** – when separated from their beloved, while they are expected to sleep on the floor, they prepare their beds of flowers themselves and lie on them.

**tam pūvaṇai** – while the bed is meant for two (*nāyaka* and *nāyaki*), only one (*nāyaki*) is lying on it.

**pūvaṇai** – Just as seen in tiruvāimoḻi 9-9-4 “*menmalarp paḷḷī vempalli*” (the soft bed of flowers is like a bed of fire), how are they lying on a bed which appears to be pouring molten fire for us!

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 33. Cinna malarkkulalum

*cinna malarkkuḻalum algulum menmulaiyum                        45 inniḷa vādai tadavat tām kaṇ tuyilum*

**Пословный перевод:**

cinnam malar kuḻalum – tresses, adorning themselves with flowers which had just then blossomed; algulum – waist; mel mulaiyum – soft bosom; in iḷa vādai tadava – being stroked by the sweet, youthful northerly winds; kaṇ tuyilum – sleeping (happily)

**Перевод:**

**cinnam malarkkuḻalum – having tresses which have spread flowers. While they are not supposed to adorn their tresses with flowers unless the nāyaka himself strings it, as mentioned in tiruppāvai 2 “malarittu nām mudiyōm” (we will not plat and adorn our hair with flowers) it is amazing that they have decorated their hair with flowers! cinnam – to spread out**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kuḻalum algulum menmulaiyum inniḷavādai tadava** – how they get the pleasures when touched by the northerly wind, just as they would get if stroked by their beloved on each and every limb separately!

**inniḷa vādai** – it is like an arrow with poison applied on its head.

**iḷa vādai** – the wind is becoming youthful by torturing others repeatedly.

**tām kaṇṭhuyilum** – can they, being separated from their beloved, sleep? Should they not be similar to what has been mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 5-5 “*porukayaṟkaṇṇiṇai tunjā*” (my eyes which are similar to two fighting fish are not sleeping)? Since they are sleeping it is possible that these are not their eyes, but eyes borrowed from others!

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
