## 34. Pon anaiyar pinnum

*ponnanaiyār pinnum tiruvuṟuga – pōrvēndan*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon anaiyār tām – those who are fortunate; pinnum tiru uṟuga – let them shine with more and more of physical beauty; pōr vēndan – rāma, who is like a tiger in war

**Перевод:**

**pon anaiyār – since they are sleeping on the bed of flowers which is like sleeping on fire, they are similar to gold which gains lustre after being burnt in fire. Alternatively, the term pon anaiyār would refer to those who are fortunate. āḻvār is mocking them saying that since these people are capable of sleeping even amidst forest fire, they are indeed fortunate ones!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pinnum tiruvuṟuga** – let this fortune be with them forever.

The prabandam now talks about sītāppirātti who followed śrī rāma to the forest. As a precursor to it, the prabandam talks about the quality of śrī rāma wherein he does not expect anything from anyone.

**por vēndan** – one who has great valour, like a tiger, in war. One who is called as *raṇamukharāman* \[*rāna* is war; *mukham* is face\]

**vēndan** – even though daśarata cakravarty was ruling ayōdyā, rāma had the strength to rule the country, making daśarata to remain aloof. Just as bharata had said “*na mantarāyā na ca mātrasyā dōśō na rajyō na ca rāghavasya* I *matpāpapamēvātra nimittamāsīt vanapravēśa raghunandasya*  īī” (it was not due to the fault of mantarā (servant of kaikēyi) or of my mother that śrī rāghava (śrī rāma) had to leave the kingdom and go to the forest. It was purely due to my sins that he had to go) the opinion of bharata is that one who was capable of piercing seven *sāla* trees with one arrow could not have gone to the forest because of lack of any strength.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 35. Tannudaiya tadai

*tannudaiya tādai paṇiyāl arasu oḻindu*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya tādai paṇiyāl – as per the counsel of his father daśarata; arasu oḻindu – avoiding ruling of the land (and setting forth for the forest)

**Перевод:**

**tannudaiya tādai – being affectionate towards his father daśarata cakravarty such that he would think “īsn’t perumāḷ  one who obeys my authority? He would do just as I tell him to do”. Alternative meaning – son of daśarata.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**paṇiyāl** – He did not reach up to daśarata’s thoughts. daśarata thought “Instead of going to the forest, would he not tie down my legs and my neck and take over ruling of this country?”

**arasu oḻindu** – when ordered by daśarata “leave the country and go to the forest”, instead of hesitating “Should I leave this country?” śrī rāma was focussed on being a forest dweller. When he was told that he was going to take charge of the country, people saw that his face had blossomed with a beauty. When he was told that he has to go to the forest, people said that they had seen  aiśvaryam (opulence) which had formed on his face which they had never seen before. It appeared as if when daśarata cakravarty told him to go the forest, śrī rama felt relieved as if a huge load had been removed from his head, a load which created *svātantriyam* (independence) in him. When bharatāḻvān caught hold o f śrī rāma’s legs (in citrakūtam) and compelled him to return to ayōdyā, śrī rāma behaved as if he had adorned himself with a garland of fresh flowers and removed that garland once the freshness was gone \[here, garland refers to the crown worn by kings; śrī rāma was happy that he had removed the crown that he would have worn had he become the king\].

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 36. Ponnagaram pinne

*ponnagaram pinnē pulamba valam koṇḍu                                                47 mannum vaḷanādu kaivittu mādirangaḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

ponnagaram – all the people who were residing in the beautiful city of ayōdyā; pinnē pulamba – even though they came behind (śrī rāma) crying (telling him not to go to the forest); valam koṇḍu – not stepping back from his from resolve; vaḷam mannu nādu kaivittu – renouncing the wealthy state of kōsala

**Перевод:**

**ponnagaram – ayōdyā matches the aprākruta (not belonging to material world) land of  śrīvaikuṇṭam which is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-8-1 as “ponnulagu” (golden place). The reason for this is that just as nityamuktas (permanent residents of śrīvaikuṇṭam as well as those who went from material world after attaining liberation), the people of ayōdyā took great joy in mingling with śrī rāma. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayodyā kāṇḍam 59-9 says “api vrukśā: parimlānā: sapuśpānkura kōrakā:” (Even insentient entities in ayōdyā such as trees with flower, tender shoot and bud were withering (due to separation from śrī rāma)).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**ponnagaram pinnē pulamba** – the people of ayōdyā went behind śrī rāma, wailing, and calling out to him, unable to sustain being separated from him. Did not kulasēkara āḻvār too say in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 8-5 “s*uṟṟamellām pintodara*” ((śrī rāma) was followed by relatives and others)!

**valam koṇḍu** – emperumān who incarnated as śrī rāma to display qualities of easy approacability, softness etc relating to simplicity, hardened his heart to manifest a deep resolve since he was going away from those who are dear to him. *valam* – strength.

**mannum vaḷanādu kaivittu** – though he gave up his right over the throne, did he at the least stay in the country? Did he not leave the country and go to forest?

**mannum vaḷanādu** – the state of kōsala, which is sweeter than ayōdyā and over which he had the  right to rule over.

**kaivittu** – leaving ayōdyā, with even animals suffering from the pangs of separation, just as it is mentioned by daśarata cakravarty’s minister sumantra “*mama tvaśvā nivrutasya na prāvartanta vartmani* I *uṣṇamaśru vimunjcantō rāmē samprastitē vanam* īī” (my horses (after leaving śrī rāma) could not go on the correct path; after śrī rāma went to the forest, they shed tears of sorrow). bhūtattāḻvār says in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 15](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/10/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-15/) “*tirindadu venjamattut tērkadavi pirindadu sītaiyai mān pin pōy*” ( was it he (śrī rāma) who entered the forests and got separated from sītā?)  He normally sleeps on the serpent bed (ādiśēśan). Did he sleep on the ground? Is it not an act which makes others to feel very much worried for him? Just as kulasēkara āḻvār says in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 9-2 “*evvāṟu nadandanai emmirāmāvō*” (How did you manage to walk in the forest, our dear śrī rāma?) and in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 9-3 *kallaṇai mēl kaṇṭhuyilak kaṟṟanaiyō kākuttā*” (Oh śrī rāma! Did you learn to sleep on a bed of stones?) did he not carry out activities which made those who were dear to him have a churning feel in their stomachs?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 37. Minnuruvil ven ter

*minnuruvil ve(vi)ṇ tēr tirindu veḷippattu                               48 kanniraittu tīyndu kaḻai udaindu kāl suḻanṟu*

**Пословный перевод:**

mādirangaḷ min uruvil veṇ tēr tirindu – with the mirage which is like lightning, spread in all directions; veḷippattu – everything appears to be arid land, with not even a blade of grass seen anywhere; kal niṟaindu tīyndu kaḻai udaindu kāl suḻanṟu – being full of stones, grass having been burnt, bamboo cracking up, with tornado continuously striking

**Перевод:**

**From now onvards, until the verse vengānam, these are all attributes to forest.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mādirangaḷ** – in all the directions

**minnuruvil veṇ tēr** – mirage which is like lightning. The words *veṇ tēr* and *pēyttēr* refer to mirage.

**veḷippattu** – there is not a blade of grass seen anywhere in sight. Everything is like absolutely dry land.

**kal niṟaindu** – everywhere it is tightly packed with stones, without any vacant space.

**kaḻai udaindu** – even green bamboo sticks, due to excessive heat, have all cracked up.

**kāl suḻanṟu** – tornado striking

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 38. Pinnum tirai vayirru

*pinnum tiraivayiṟṟup pēyē tirindulavāk                                        49 konnavilum vengānattu ūdu vengadirōn tunnu veyil vaṟutta vembaralmēl panjadiyāl                                50 mannan irāman pin vaidēvi enṟu uraikkum*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnum – moreover; tirai vayiṟu pēyē tirindu – having only ghosts, which are with empty stomachs (due to lack of food), which keep roaming; ulavā kol navilum – having only the sound of killing continuously being emitted; vem kānattūdu – inside the cruel forest; kodu kadirōn tunnu veyil vaṟutta vembaral mēl – on top of the pebbles which have been roasted by the rays of the hot sun; vaidēvi enṟu uraikkum – one who is referred to as vaidēhi

**Перевод:**

**pinnum – more than what had been said before**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tirai vayiṟṟup pēyē tirindu** – only ghosts were roaming there, with empty stomachs. They have not had any food and hence are famished. Since the verse says *pēyē* it implies that there is no other creature in that place.

**ulavāk kol navilum** – the only talk there seems to be “this person entered the forest and died; that person who entered the forest is struggling for his life”. It could also be considered as *ulavā vengānam* – the cruel forest which keeps extending continuously, just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-30 “*tē vanēna vanam gatvā*” (they entered one forest after another)

**vem kānattūdu** – amidst the most cruel forest

**kodum kadirōn** – one who is on the side of kaikēyi; one who created  hot rays just to torment śrī rāma.

**tunnu veyil** – full sunshine

**veyil vaṟutta vem paral** – the sunshine is roasting pebbles; as they are getting roasted, the pebbles become cruel themselves and become capable of roasting the sunshine; in the end, they attain sufficient heat even to burn the sun.

**vem paral mēl** – on top of such pebbles

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 39. Anna nadaiya

*anna nadaiya aṇangu nadandilaḷē                                                      51*

**Пословный перевод:**

annam nadaiya aṇangu – sitāppirātti who has the gait of a swan; mannan irāman pin – behind that mahāraja, śrī rāma; panju adiyāl – with soft, like cotton, divine feet; nadandilaḷē – did she too not go?

**Перевод:**

**panjadiyāl – with divine feet which are soft like cotton. Just as it is said “padmasamaprabhā:” (having a radiance like a lotus), for radiance and softness, only lotus and cotton are referred to as similes.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannan** – leaving aside the crown which would have made him as the ruler of bhūdēvi (earth) since he chose to be the ruler of śrīdēvi (sītāppirātti) and going to the forest; hence he is called as king. Just as it has been mercifully mentioned by sītāppirātti in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 118-51 “*deeyamānām na tu tatā pratijagrāha rāghava*:, *avijñāya pituś candam*” (rāmapirān did not accept me even as my father offered me to him without getting his father’s consent), as soon as janaka maharāja was ready to give her hand in marriage to him, śrī rāma asked him “Has my father arrived?” Hearing this, just as she (sītāppirātti) lost out to his *pitrubhāravaśyam* (being subservient to father) the word *mannan* also refers her losing out to his greatness of giving up the kingdom.

**irāman** – if one has to go behind śrī rāma who makes everyone joyous, one can go not only to this forest but to any place; even to hell. Did sītappirātti not say in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 30-18 “*yastvayā saha sa svargō nirayō yas tvayā vinā*” (being together with you is svargam; being separated from you is hell)!

**vaidēhi** – being the daughter of vidēharāja who once said “there is nothing which belongs to me in the burning mitilā” she (vaidēhi) too has the greatness of leaving aside the kingdom and going to the forest.

**enṟuraikkum** – the whole of śrī rāmāyaṇam will say this. Did not vālmīki too say in śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 2-41 “*sītāyāścaritam mahat*” calling it the life story of sītāppirātti!

**enṟuraikkum** – it could also be said that she stood to be praised by the entire world as the tilak (eminent one) among all the women.

**anna nadaiya** – her gait was such that once śrī rāma sees her gait resembling a swan, he would engage in madal.

**aṇangu** – lexicon says “*sūrum aṇangum deyvappeṇṇē*” (both celestial women and aṇangu are divine women). śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 77-25 says “*tasya bhūyō viśēśēṇa mytilee janakātmajādēvatābhis samā* ” (mytili, the daughter of king janaka, was distinguished for śrī rāma, being on a par with dēvatas). Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundarakāṇḍam 55-26 “*tryāṇām bharatādīnām bhṟatrūṇām dēvatā ca yā*” (whosoever is the deity for the three brothers, bhratā et al ) and as mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 10-2-5 “*sītai enbadōr deivam*” (a deity called as sītā), she remains as the deity for all, śrī rāma as well as the others, without any distinction.

**nadandilaḷē** – did not one with such greatness walk along! When she, who is the mother for the entire universe, without looking at her svarūpam (basic nature) go along with him after compelling him to agree, to go the forest, what is wrong in us in engaging in madal when we do not attain him?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 40. Pinnum karu negungan

*pinnum karu nedungaṇ sevvāyp piṇainōkkinminnaṇaiya nuṇmarungul vēgavadi enṟuraikkum                                           52*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnum – apart from that; karu nedu kaṇ sem vāy piṇai nōkkin min aṇaiya nuṇ marungul – having dark, long hands, reddish mouth, looks of a deer and a slender waist similar to lightning; vēgavadi enṟu uraikkum – being spoken of as vēgavadi (name of a lady)

**Перевод:**

**Starting from pinnum, until puṇarndilaḷē: this narrates a story from mahābhārata, in which a gandarvastrī (a celestial lady) marries a gandarva (a celestial male); her elder brother tries to take her back by waging a war on the gandarva. Not minding about him, the celestial lady went to her husband and enjoyed with him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pinnum** – a lady who has the greatness to be spoken of in the same breathe after speaking about pirātti \[sītā\]

**karunedungaṇ** – Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-5 “*asītēkśaṇa*”  (one with long eyes) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 9-4-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/07/thiruvaimozhi-9-4-1-maiyar-karunganni/) “*maiyār karungaṇṇi  kamalamalar mēl seyyāḷ*” (reddish complexioned śrī mahālakśmi who has decorated dark eyes and who resides on a lotus flower), pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) has the greatness of having dark eyes. This vēgavadi has more expansive eyes than pirātti.

**sevvāy** – her reddish mouth was similar to sītāppirātti’s just as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 64-76 “*purēva mē cārudatīm . . . . jagat saśailam parivarttayāmyaham* ” (if sītā who has a beautiful smile is not handed back to me like before, I will pull this earth with its mountains, upside down) wherein sītāppirātti’s smile was capable of making even śrī rāma who is the protector of all to take such an action in separation. It was also similar to the one described in periya tirumoḻi 5-7-7 “*surikuḻal kanivāyttiru*” (śrī mahālakaśmi who has great tresses and lips which are similar to kovvai (common creeper of the hedges) fruit).

**piṇai nōkkin** – her looks were such that it would make one inseparable from her even for a moment as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 96-10 “*na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi*” (I cannot live even for a moment) and were similar to the one mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 11-5-1 “*mānamarum mennōkki*” (she had eyes which were as soft as those of a deer’s),

**minnaṇaiya nuṇ marungul** – this refers to her slender waist which creates a fear in him and makes him wonder whether it will snap and fall down, just as it is mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 3-9-5 “*minnaṇaiya nuṇmarungul melliyal*” (having a very slender waist similar to lightning).

**vēgavadi enṟu uraikkum** – one who is referred to as *vēgavadi*. Just as śrī rāmāyaṇam narrated the story of sītāppirātti, vēgavadi was such that mahābhāratam narrated her story.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 41. Kanni tan innuyiram

*kanni tan innuyirām kādalanaik kāṇādu tannudaiya mun tōnṟal koṇḍēkat tān senṟangu                       53 annavanai nōkkādu aḻitturappi vāḷamaruḷ kannavil tōḷ kāḷaiyaik kaippidittu mīṇḍumpōy                           54*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanni – a girl; than innuyir kādalanai kāṇādu – not allowing (her) to see her husband who is like her lifeline; tannudaiya mun tōnṟal koṇḍu ēga – her elder brother carrying her (preventing her from being with her husband); angu – in that state; annavanai – that elder brother; nōkkādu – not minding him; aḻittu urappi – abusing him by using ignominious words; tān senṟu – leaving, forcefully; vāḷ amarum – in a big battlefield; kal navil tōḷ kāḷaiyai – her beloved, who is having mountain-like huge shoulder and who is proud like a bull; kaippidihtu – marrying him; mīṇḍum pōy – reaching her dwelling place

**Перевод:**

**kanni – the incidence of sītāppirātti following śrī rāma (to the forest) after being united with him for twelve years (in ayōdyā) is not a great event; this girl (vēgavadi) who has not united with her beloved earlier, disregarding her elder brother and going behind her beloved is indeed a great act. Unlike what has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38-17 “samā dvādaśa tatrāham rāghavasya nivēśanē I bhunjānāmānuśān bhōgān sarvakāmasamruddinī īī” (for twelve years I was residing in the palace of perumāḷ (śrī rāma) experiencing aprākrutabhōgas (enjoyment not related to materialistic world) and had all my desires fulfilled completely) vēgavadi had not experienced the pleasures of uniting with her beloved.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**than innuyirām kādalanaik kāṇādu** – not seeing her beloved, who is sweeter than her lifeline and who is the epitome of love.

**tannudiaya mun tōnṟal koṇḍēga** – her elder brother unable to tolerate her going with her beloved, separated her from her beloved, to take her with him .

**tān senṟu** – leaving him (her brother) of her volition

**angu annavanai nōkkādu** – not bothering with affection for her brother, who tried to take her back and who was upset that she had not even taken leave of him when she went back to her beloved

**aḻittu** – destroying his true nature by using ignominious words

**urappi** – speaking abusive words such as “the way that you are trying to separate me from my beloved, it appears that you are thinking that the two of us should get married” and “despite being your sister, it appears that you want to make me as your wife and rule over me, you despicable!”

**vāḷ amaruḷ** – in the huge battle that broke out between her brother and her beloved

**kannavil tōḷ kāḷaiyaik kaippidittu** – the girl vēgavadi renounced her brother’s hand and caught hold of her beloved’s hand.

**kal navil tōḷ** – her beloved’s shoulder was like a firm rock, capable of holding on to, after renouncing her brother and other relatives.

**kāḷai** – even if he did not have strength in his shoulders, his (youthful) age was apt to follow him.

**kaippidittu** – holding on to the hand, in front of everyone, of the one who had drawn his bow for her sake.

**mīṇḍum pōy** – going away from her brother. It could also be considered as – going back to her dwelling place.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 42. Ponnnavilum agam

*ponnavilum āgam puṇarndilaḷē pūm gangai munnam punal parakkum nannādan minnādum                                       55*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon navilum āgam – in the chest (of her beloved) which is like gold; puṇarndilaḷē – did she not get embraced?; pū gangai punal munnam parakkum nal nādan – the head of the great country where the beautiful waters of the river gangā had earlier increased its flow abundantly; min ādum – having radiance

**Перевод:**

**pon navailum āgam puṇarndilaḷē – just as sītāppirātti had embraced śrī rāma as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 30-39 “bhartāram pariśasvajē” (tightly embraced her husband) after the annihilation of the demon kara, and healed the injuries caused by thousands of arrows with her bosom, (vēgavadi) gave fomentation to the injuries on the chest of her beloved, which looked like reddish gold, who had drawn his bow in the battle for her sake, by falling over his chest and embracing him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

(**pūm gangai . . . . kēttaṟivadillaiyē**) tirumangai āḻvār now recapitulates the story mentioned in the two hundred and thirty fourth chapter of ādiparvam in mahābhāratam which narrates how a *nagakanni* (a girl born in the clan of snakes) by name *ulūpi* saw *arjuna* when he was having a bath in the river gangā, during his pilgrimage, desired him lustfully, compelled him, pulled him to her place, expressed her desire for him without any shame and enjoyed him.

**pūm gangai** – did she (ulūpi) engage in madal for someone who was infamous? Was it not for the most famous arjuna who was born in a great, regal lineage!

**pūm gangai** – river gangā with orchards full of blossomed flowers on both banks. Did not periyāḻvār divinely mention in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-10 “*kān tadam poḻil sūḻ gangai*” (gangā surrounded by orchards with sweetly fragrant flowers) and in 4-7-9 “*karai maram sādik kaduttiḻi gangai*” (gangā striking against the trees on the banks with torrential force)!  Alternatively, as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-2 “*nalandigaḻ sadaiyān mudikkonṟai malarum nāraṇan pādattuḻāyum kalandiḻi punalāl pugar padu gangai*” (the radiant gangā river which flows down with the reddish *konṟai* (cassia) flower), which is worn by Siva who has matted hair purified by the śrīpāda tīrtam (divine water from the divine feet) of śrīman nārāyaṇa and the greenish tuḷasi which decorates the divine feet of śrīman nārāyana), this could refer to gangā which comes down with the flowers from the head of *Sankara* (Siva) and the divine feet of *caduppuyan* (four divine shouldered śrīman nārāyaṇa). Another interpretation – gangā  which is flowing with the kaṟpaga flowers (kaṟpaga is a celestial, wish-fulfilling tree) as mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-7-7 “*kaṟpagamalarum kalandiḻi gangai*”.

**munnam punal parakkum** – even if a small amount of water is formed in himālayā mountains, the water flows first in his country (the country of nāgas).  Being the head-sluice (gate from which water flows first). This refers to the abundance (wealth) of water in his country.

**nannādan** – this refers to the wealth of land.

Next the pāsuram refers to the wealth of the head of the country.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 43. Konnavilum nil vel

*konnavilum nīḷvēl kurukkaḷ kulamadalai tannigar onṟillāda venṟit tananjayanaip                                       56 pannāgarāyan madappāvai pāvai tan manniya nāṇ accam madam enṟivai agalat                                    57*

**Пословный перевод:**

kol navilum nīḷ vēl – having a spear, which is long and which knows how to kill, in the hand; kurukkaḷ kula madalai – one who was born in the clan of kurus; than nigar onṟu illāda venṟi – one with unparalleled victories; dananjayanai – arjuna; pannāgarāyan madam pāvai – woman with timidity, ulūpi, who is the daughter of gauravya, a king of snakes; pāvai than manniya nāṇ accam madam ivai agala – ridding herself of feminine qualities such as shyness, fear, timidity

**Перевод:**

**min ādum kol navilum nīḷ vēl – spear having radiance similar to lightning, moving very fast, having the ability to kill enemies even on sighting them, churning their stomachs, having a length such that it can kill enemies wherever they are.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kurukkaḷ kulamadalai** – one born in the clan of kurus; they are like the teachers for the whole world. One who is prominent among them.

**tannigar onṟillāda** – one who is such courageous that even śrī kriṣṇa cannot equal him

**venṟit tananjayanai** – arjuna who knows nothing other than victory

**dananjayanai** – one who has won over the *puruśārtam* (ultimate benefits) of wealth and lust. Did he not consider ūrvasi, who desired him, as his ‘mother’ \[since she is one of the consorts of his father, indra\]?

**pannāgarāyan madappāvai** – *pannagam: padā na gaccadīti pannaga*: (that which cannot move with its feet) – meaning a snake. The term *pannagarāyan* has got lengthened \[for one syllable\] and has got changed to *pannāgarāyan*. Daughter of the king of snakes, with abundant femininity.

**madappāvai** – There is none who is greater than she, in femininity.

**pāvai than manniya nān accam madam ivaiyagala** – The qualities of shyness, fear and timidity, which are natural rather than having been acquired after the time of birth, getting destroyed like an ocean drying up.

**nāṇam** – in activities which are inappropriate for a female, mind getting śrivelled.

**accam** – mind trembling even at the thought of such an activity.

**madam** – pretending to be ignorant even after knowing everything.

**payirppu** – loathing even if the dress of any male touches her

Since any woman with chastity will not give up the fourth mentioned quality (*payirppu*) it has not been taken here as an essential quality for a lady.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 44. Tannudaiya kongai

*tannudaiya kongai mugam neṟiyat tān avan tan pon varai āgam taḻeeikkoṇḍu pōyt tanadu                               58 nannagaram pukku nayandinidu vāḻndaduvum munnuraiyil kēttu aṟivadillaiyē – sūḻ kadaluḷ                                59 ponnagaram seṟṟa purandaranōdu ēr okkum*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya kongai mugam neṟiya – such that the face of her bosom became stiff; tān – (discarding her shyness) on her own; avan than – that arjuna’s; pon varai āgam – beautiful, mountain-like chest; taḻeeikkoṇḍu pōy – embracing; tanadu – her; nal nagaram pōy pukku – reaching beautiful place (in the land of snakes); nayandu – celebrating; inidu vāḻndaduvum – that she lived happily; mun uraiyil kēttu aṟivadillaiyē – have you not heard of, in mahābhāratam?; sūḻ kadaluḷ – in the expansive ocean; pon nagaram seṟṟa – one who destroyed the towns of hiraṇyāsura; purandaranōdu ēr okkum – it will parallel the greatness of dēvēndra (head of celestial entities)

**Перевод:**

**tannudaiya kongai mugam neṟiya – manifesting only her love, without waiting for reciprocation, to the extent of showing off the face of her bosom**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tān avan than ponvaraiyāgam taḻeeikoṇḍu pōy** – embracing his chest which looked like a golden mountain, even if he did not evince any interest

**pon varai āgam** – while all the people in the world were carrying out penance in order to attain celestial damsel ūrvasi, did not that ūrvasi very much desire to embrace arjuna? Such arjuna’s chest.

**tanadu nannagaram** – unlike the town here \[in materialistic world\] where people prevented others from engaging in madal, her (ulūpi’s) town had people who celebrated those who engaged in carrying out madal.

**pugundu** – entering \[such a town\]

**nayandu** – celebrating. Being united with him for a long time

**inidu vāḻndaduvum** – unlike people here who prevent \[anything good happening\], since she had friends who showed her ways to attain him and who praised her, she lived happily.

**mun uraiyil kēttu aṟivadillaiyē** – it vil not matter if one has not learnt *kāmaśāstra* (treatise on love). But vouldn’t one have heard of epics such as mahābhārata, for the sake of attaining *puṇya* (virtue)? If one had heard of it, one would have understood the greatness of engaging in madal.

From the verse *sūḻ kadaluḷ*, until the verse *pērinbam eydināḷ*, the pāsuram speaks about how *uśai,* the daughter of *bāṇāsura* captured *anirudda* the grandson of kriṣṇa, and enjoyed with him.

**sūḻ kadaluḷ ponnagaram seṟṟa purandaran** – indra (the head of celestial entities) who entered oceans and destroyed the demons of *hiraṇyapura* and attained the name *purandara*.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 45. Mannavan vanan

*mannavan vāṇan avuṇarkku vāḷ vēndan                                              60 tannudaiya pāvai ulagattut tannokkum kanniyarai illāda kātciyāḷ tannudaiya                                                61 innuyirt tōḻiyāl emperumān īntuḻāy mannu maṇivaraittōḷ māyavan pāviyēn                                             62*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannavan – being the king among kings; avuṇarkku vāḷ vēndan – being a radiant leader among asuras \[demonic entities\]; vāṇan tannudaiya pāvai – as the daughter of bāṇāsura; ulagattu than okkum kanniyarai illāda kātciyāḷ – uśai, who is so extremely beautiful, with none in the world equalling her in beauty; tannudaiya innuyir tōḻiyāl – with the help of her dear friend, citralēkai; mannu maṇi varai tōḷ māyavan – one with amazing activities, having divine shoulders adorned with divine tuḷasi garland and matching a mountain of gemstones

**Перевод:**

**purandaranōdu ēr okkum mannavan vāṇan – bāṇasura, the king among kings, who equalled the greatness of indra \[as mentioned in the previous verse\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**avuṇarkku vāḷ vēndan** – leader of demonic entities

**tannudaiya pāvai** – his daughter

**ulagattut than okkum kanniyarai illāda  kātcvhiyāḷ** – among girls of her age, there was none who could equal her in beauty

**tannudaiya innuriyt tōḻiyāl** – with the help of her friend, dearer than her life, *citralēkai*, who was like her right hand

**emperumān īntuḻāy** – aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār, one of the commentators for this prabandam mercifully says “apart from confirming that emperumān is full of auspicious qualities to counter those who say that the supreme entity is without any quality, just like emperumānār (bhagavat rāmānujar) who gets deeply involved in the auspicious qualities of emperumān, she too \[tirumangai āḻvār in feminine mood\] gets deeply engaged with the material \[sweet tuḷasi\] which will not allow her to go past and neither will it allow her to describe clearly.

**emperumān** – one who made me his servitor by making me engage with him full and set out with madal \[in order to attain him\].

**īntuḻāy mannu maṇi varaittōḷ māyavan** – amazing entity with śouders matching a mountain made of gem stones and having very sweet tuḷasi, fitting well on those shoulders

**māyavan** – even though āḻvār is in a state where he despises emperumān, the moment he thinks of emperumān, due to the involvement, he calls him as an amazing entity. He says “What a wonder is this! Even when I am setting out to engage in madal, he is making me get engaged with him!”

**pāviyēn** – since she is unable to attain the śouders which had embraced her earlier, she is calling herself as *pāviyēn* (one who has sinned). Alternatively, this could be considered as – isn’t *uśai* a fortunate girl having a friend who brought her beloved person as soon as she \[*uhsai*\] got involved with him! What sort of sin have I committed that even when I wish to engage in madal, I have friends who are trying to dissuade me?”  Another interpretation – “What sins have I committed! All those who are going in the long street, where I am setting out with madal, are dissuading me from engaging in madal”. Yet another interpretation – “getting caught in the deep river of *bhagavat kāmam* (love for emperumān), I am unable to extricate myself and reach the shore and engage in madal. What sin have I committed?”

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 46. Ennai idu vilaitta

*ennai idu viḷaitta īriraṇḍu mālvaraittōḷ mannavan tan kādalanai māyattāṟkoṇḍu pōy                                               63 kanni tanpāl vaikka maṟṟavanōdu ettanaiyōr manniya pērinbam eydināḷ – maṟṟivaidān                                                           64*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāviyēn ennai idu viḷaitta īr iraṇḍu mālvaraittōḷ – the four shoulders which are like a huge mountain and which torment me, who is a sinner; mannavan – king of kings; emperumān than – kaṇṇapirān (kriṣṇa)’s; kādalanai – aniruddāḻvān (grandson) who is dear \[to kriṣṇa\]; māyattāl koṇḍupōy kanni tanpāl vaikka – taken deceptively, to where she was staying and kept beside her; maṟṟavanōdu – with anirudda; ettanai ōr – unqiue in many ways; manniya pērinbam eydināḷ – she experienced permanent, heavenly bliss

**Перевод:**

**ennai idu viḷaitta – making me set out to engage in madal, like this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**īriraṇḍu māl varaittōḷ** – as he kept looking at the way I was getting tormented, his shoulders kept flourishing (by increasing in their size)

**mannavan** – He is just as it is mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-10 “*nāgaṇai misai namparar selvar periyar siṟumānidavar nām*” (emperumān, who is reclining on the mattress of tiruvandāḻvān (ādiśēśan) is opulent and superior to everyone else; we are the lowly people in samsāram ).

**mannavan than kādalanai** – vouldn’t the love towards grandson be double that of love towards son! aniruddāḻvān who appeared to be the epitome of emperumān’s love….

**māyattāl koṇḍu pōy** – uśai saw anirudda in her dream. She informed this to her friend, citralēkai, who started drawing the portraits of all the kings in the world. uśai kept rejecting each of these portraits. When citralēkai drew the portrait of kriṣṇa, uśai said that the person who she saw in her dream was looking similar in a way, but younger than kriṣṇa. citralēkai then drew the portrait of anirudda and uśai confirmed that he was the one she had seen in her dream. With the help of her *yōgabalam* (strength of yōgic powers), citralēkai immediately went to dvārakā and effortlessly abducted anirudda who was sleeping on his cot, being protected by palace guards, as if he were a calf born that day.

**kanni tanpāl vaikka** – she placed anirudda, unknown even to him, next to uśai who was longing to see the person who she had seen in her dreams, in person.

**maṟṟavanōdu** – the love which uśai had towards anirudda, blossomed in anirudda after he saw uśai in person.

**ettanaiyōr manniya pērinbam eydināḷ** – once vānan (bāṇāsura) came to know of this, he imprisoned anirudda. But since he also kept uśai along with anirudda in the prison, she attained unencumbered, distinguished, natural and heavenly bliss by uniting with him.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 47. Ennale kettire

*ennālē kēttīrē ēḻaigāḷ ennuraikkēn mannu malaiyaraiyan poṟpāvai                                                         65 minnu maṇi muṟuval sevvāy umai ennum*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēḻaigāḷ – Oh ignorant people!; maṟṟu ivaidān ennālē kēttīrē – Are you remaining to hear examples like these, from me?; en uraikkēn – How much could I tell (you)?; mannu malai araiyan pon pāvai – being the distinguished daughter of himavān, the unśakeable king of mountains; vāḷ nilā minnu maṇi muṟuval sem vāy – having a reddish mouth, with a beautiful smile matching the radiant moon

**Перевод:**

**maṟṟivaidān ennālē kēttīrē – Are you thinking of hearing thousands of stories like these from me, who attempted to engage in madal but lacking in strength to carry it out?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**ēḻaigāḷ** – Oh ignorant people! You have not even understood that this is not possible for me.

**ennuraikkēn** – Is it possible for me to narrate all the examples of those who had engaged in madal? How much could I say? However, I will tell you one more example finally, which cannot be missed out.

**mannu malaiyaraiyan** – In order to get the true value of gold, a piece of fine gold would be displayed as a representative sample. In the same way, there is a girl who is the example for all the girls in the world. He is the fortunate one who had such a girl as his daughter. One who is fortunate to have given birth to a girl who is the example for all those girls who want to engage in madal.

**mannu malaiyaraiyan poṟpāvai** – Was it some girl who is an anonymous, with regard to her town or parents, who had engaged in madal? Is she not the famous daughter of his, known all over the world!

**araiyan poṟpāvai** – She was born in a regal heritage!

**poṟpāvai** – being distinguished, full of feminine qualities.

**vāṇilā minnum maṇi muṟuval** – Was it someone who had only the heritage, but lacking in physical beauty, who set out to engage in madal? Shining like the splendrous moon!

**viḷangum** – one who has a beautiful smile.

**vāṇilā minnu muṟuval** – This manifests the whitish gleam from the set of teeth.

**maṇi muṟuval** – it is coveted like a gemstone; it is beautiful.

*nilā* is moon. *nilā* also refers to the rays of moon. Thus *vāṇilā muṟuval* could be taken as referring to the smile which emits the rays of moon. It could also be considered as referring to set of teeth which is like the moon, like lightning or like a gemstone.

**sevvāy** – having a reddish mouth which is in striking contrast with the white set of teeth.

**umaiyennum** – being famous throughout the world, with this name \[umā\].

**malaiyaraiyan poṟpāvai umaiyennum** – just as it is mentioned in kēnōpaniśat “*umām haimavatīm*” (umā, the daughter of himavān) one who is praised in upaniśats as one who has had vision of the supreme entity

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 48. Anna nadaiya

*anna nadaiya aṇangu vaṇangu nudangidai sēr                                                   66 ponnudambu vādap pulanaindum nondagala*

**Пословный перевод:**

umai ennum – having the name umā; annam nadaiya aṇangu – the divine lady (pārvati) who has the gait of a swan; nudangu idai sēr pon udambnu vāda – her beautiful form with the quivering waist, withering; pulan aindum nondu agala – her five senses getting distressed and leaving her

**Перевод:**

**anna nadaiya aṇangu – if she takes a few steps forward, all the anguish of her beloved would get relieved; did she not struggle for his sake with this gait of a swan?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**nudangidai sēr udambu** – one who has a form with a quivering waist. The beauty of her waist is such that one would fear for it, wondering whether it would snap and fall down.

**ponnudambu vāda** – her physical form was so great that he (rudra) had to give up half of his body for her. Since she did not attain him, it was withering like a tender leaf would wither after separating from a tree.

**pulan aindum nondu agala** – her five senses would think “We cannot suffer by remaining like this in her form when she is carrying out severe penance” and leave her. Even those who carry out severe penance in the world would eat a few dry leaves to sustain themselves over a period of time. But she did not take even the leaves and continued her penance, to be called as *aparṇa* (one who does not need leaves). kāḷidāsa, the poet, says in his literary work kumārasambhavam 5-28 “*svayam viśīrṇadruma parṇa vruttitā parā hi kāśtā tapasas tapā puna:*  I *tadapyapākīrṇamata*: *priyamvadām vadantyaparnōti ca tām purāvida*:  īī ” (that penance where one eats dried leaves which fall from the tree on their own is called as severe penance. pārvati did not consume even that. Hence our forefathers call this lady with sweet words as *aparṇa*).Since she was carrying out such a severe penance, is it amazing that her five senses felt distressed and left her?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 49. Tannudaiya kulai

*tannudaiya kūḻaic cadābāram tān tarittu āngu                          67 anna arum tavattin ūdupōy āyiram tōḷ*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya kūḻai – her tresses; sadābāram tān tarittu – she made it into matted hair and wore it; anna aru tavattinūdu ōy – completing that great penance (when asked what benefit she derived)

**Перевод:**

**tannudaiya kūḻaic cadābāram tān tarittu – kūḻai – tresses. She wore her tresses as matted hair. Just like śrī rāma had worn his tresses when he set out for the forest, she also wore her tresses in a similar fashion.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**anna arum tavam** – Nothing could be said more than saying “it (her penance) was like that”. Since she (āḻvār in feminine mood) was not satisfied with what she had said, she says *arum tavam* (rare penance). If one were to describe the qualities of this penance, the mouth will get burnt; if one were to think about the penance, the heart will burn \[it was such a severe penance\].

**tavattinūdu pōy** – did she get her beloved during the course of her penance and give up the penance? No, she completed the penance in full.

Now, the pāsuram speaks about his (her beloved’s) state even as she was suffering like this.

**āyiram tōḷ** – As she was suffering, he was growing manifold, thinking “How fortunate! There are people who desire us!” Unlike śrī rāma, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-42 “*naivadamśān na maśakān na kīdān na sarīsrūpān* I *rāghavō’panayēt gātrāt tadgatēnāntarātmanā*  īī” (since his heart was focussed on sītāppirātti  (after she was abducted by rāvaṇa), śrī rāma did not even remove various insects such as flies, mosquitoes, worms and snakes which were crawling on his divine form), he (the beloved of umā) is not one who feels distressed even if separated for just a few days.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 50. Mannu karatalangal

*mannu karatalangaḷ mattittu mādirangaḷ                                                68 minni erivīsa mēledutta sūḻ kaḻaṟkāl ponnulagam ēḻum kadandu umbar mēl silumba                                           69 mannu kulavaraiyum mārudamum tāragaiyum tannin udanē suḻalac cuḻanṟādum                                                            70*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyiram tōḷ mannu karatalangaḷ mattittu – (Siva) spreading his hands with thousands of shoulders in all directions; mādirangaḷ – directions; minni eri vīsa – such that fire and smoke got emitted; mēl edutta – lifted in the top direction; kaḻal sūḻ kāl – one foot with a valorous anklet; pon ulagam ēḻum kadandu – going past the beautiful worlds above; umbar mēl silumba – extending (the single foot) more and more, high above; mannu kulam varaiyum mārudamum tāragaiyum tannudanē suḻala – the firmly standing kula parvatams (mountains which sustain earth), wind and stars spinning with him; suḻanṟu ādum – one who was himself dancing with a spin

**Перевод:**

**mannu karatalangaḷ mattittu – when he dances, the thousand hands went and hit against various directions.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mādirangaḷ minni eri vīsa** – due to that, sparks and smoke got emitted in all the directions, letting out fire

**mēl edutta sūḻ kaḻal kāl** – the foot which went went up when he danced and had a valorous anklet on it. While the *nāyaki* (umā) was in distress after desiring him, he was decorating himself from foot to head and dancing in joy.

**sūḻ kaḻal kāl** – this could also be interpreted as foot which travelled all over the world.

**pon ulagam ēḻum kadandu** – going past the worlds which were measured by trivikrama

**umbar mēl silumba** – going here and there, all over the celestial world and dancing, without stopping at any place. *umbar* – celestial world. The celestial entities are in fear wondering “What is happening?”

**mannu kulavaraiyum** – the *kulaparvatams* which sustain earth by standing firmly.

**mārudamum** – wind, which goes to where the people are, like a father going to his son’s place, and which is the cause for the living of all beings.

**tāragaiyum** – the groups of stars which foretell the good and bad which happen to people

**tanninudanē suḻalac cuḻanṟādum** – when he dances, just like the *punnaippū* (a type of flower from mast-tree) spins around, all the entities mentioned above – the kulaparvatams, wind and stars – keep spinning around as he dances in a spin.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 51. Konnavilum muvilai

*konnavilum mūvilai vēl kūttan podiyādiannavan tan ponnagalam senṟāngu aṇaindilaḷē                                  71*

**Пословный перевод:**

kol navilum mū ilai vēl – one who has the weapon sūlam (spear), which has three leaves \[sharp edges\], which indulges in the occupation of killing; kūttan – one who is famously called as a dancer; podiyādi – one who is famous for applying ash all over his body; annavan than – sivapirān (rudra) who is like that; pon agalam āngu senṟu aṇaindilaḷē – did she not go to him and embrace his beautiful chest?

**Перевод:**

**kol navilum – it will prompt to kill; it is a weapon which keeps instructing him to kill since he is the deity for bringing ātmās to their end. It is unlike the weapon which is praised by nammāḻvār in tiruviruttam 33 as “aruḷār tiruccakkaram” (the divine chakra (disc) which is full of mercy) and in tiruvāimoḻi 2-10-5 as “aṟamuyalāḻi” (the divine disc which is always engaged in the righteous path of protecting devotees).  Even those who desire to see it will be overcome with fear and grief \[it is that cruel\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mūvilaivēl** – just as he is virūpākśa, one with three eyes, his weapon too has three leaves.

**kūttan** – is his dance similar to the beautiful dance as enunciated mercifully by periyāḻvār in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-6-6 “*tūya nadam payilum sundara*” (Oh one who has the beauty of dancing beautifully to attract the mind!)? His dance is like that of a ghost’s.

**podiyādi** – Please see the enjoyment which she (umā) is going to get after attaining rudra. He has applied ash as sandalwood paste.  Did she not take so much of efforts to attain this?

**annavan** – instead of saying with her (parakāla nayaki’s) mouth that he (rudra) has decorated himself with various other materials such as *ūmattai* (bush plant, thorn-apple), *erukkam* (Indian madddar plant, whose exuded sap is very poisonous), bones, tiger skin etc, she turns her face away so as not to see what he has worn and says “Someone like that”.

**than ponnagalam** – she considers his chest which has all the above mentioned decorative features as greatly enjoyable, since she is his wife.

**senṟāngu aṇaindilaḷē** – did he come even after she had performed this much of penance? Did she not go on her own volition and embrace him as if it is one-sided exhibition of love?  He is not the *akāravācyan* (one who is referred to by the single syllable ‘a’ (emperumān, the supreme entity)) who will melt and fall head over heels \[in doing great things for his devotee\] once the devotee performs an anjali (saluting with cupped palms). Is it not to attain such an entity that she (umā) had performed such great penance? When she had taken such efforts, is it any wonder that I (parakāla nāyaki) am engaging in madal to attain emperumān?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 52. Panni uraikkungal

*panni uraikkungāl bāradamām – pāviyēṟku ennuṟu nōy yān uraippak kēṇmin – irum poḻil sūḻ                                            72*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāviyēṟku – for me, the sinful person; panni uraikkum kāl – if I have to say more expansively (citing more examples); bāradamam Am – it will end just like mahābāradam \[will be so huge\]; en uṟu nōy – the disease which happened to me; yān uraippak kēṇmin – please listen to it, as I narrate it; iru poḻil sūḻ – surrounded by huge orchards

**Перевод:**

**panni uraikkum kāl bāradamām – It is not possible for me to speak this matter and bring it to an end. Only great personalities such as vyāsar can speak about it. Even if they speak about it, it will be only expansive like mahābāradam (which has one lakh twenty five thousand ślōkās) and cannot be narrated in a brief manner.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pāviyēṟku** – for me who has committed such sins that I do not have the strength to narrate it in an expansive way just as vyāsa did. The words are to be rearranged as *panni uraikkum kāl pāviyēṟku bāradamām.*

**ennuṟu nōy yān uraippak kēṇmin** – let it remain that it cannot be narrated till completion. There is no need for me to explain in a detailed way the sufferings of those people (the women whose stories parakāla nāyaki has told in the previous verses). If you could listen to my sufferings, as narrated by me, it could be taken to completion.  Let the experience of others as known to us, remain by the side. Listen to the distress that I have personally experienced.

**en uṟu nōy** – the disease which has attained me truly; the disease which has pervaded me completely. Just as nammāḻvār had mercifully mentioned in tiruviruttam 53 “*deyva nannōy*” (this is a good disease about emperumān), it is the disease which remains by my side as a great support, after I separated from tirunaṟaiyūr nambi and suffered. It is the disease which śrī bharatāḻvān suffered after getting involved with śrī rāma’s handsomeness.

**yān uraippak kēṇmin** – Listen to me who has got this disease. Since it cannot be narrated by anyone else, only I have to narrate it myself.

**kēṇmin** – Please listen; if my life has to be sustained, please listen. Just as it is said in uttararāma caritam “*pūrōtpīdē tatākasya parivāha: pratikriyā* I *śokakśōbhē ca hrudayam pralāpairēva dāryatē*  īī ” (if the water in pond starts overflowing, cutting the outlet channel alone will save the banks from getting broken. In the same way, if one were agitated due to excessive sorrow, only by crying out aloud does the heart sustain itself), listen to me so that I could sustain myself.

**kēṇmin** – this could also mean – listen to me if you want to reap the benefit of having been provided with ears.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 53. Mannu maraiyor

*mannu maṟaiyōr tirunaṟaiyūr māmalaipōl ponniyalum mādak kavādam kadandupukku                                             73*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟaiyōr mannum – place inhabited by vaidikas (those who follow vēdas); tirunaṟaiyūr – at tirunaṟaiyūr; mā malaipōl pon iyalum mādam kavādam kadandu pukku – managing to cross the divine door of the sannidi (place inside a temple where emperumān’s divine form resides), which is like a huge mountain and which is like gold

**Перевод:**

**irum poḻil sūḻ – the town of tirunaṟaiyūr which is surrounded by expansive, great gardens. Gardens which have palm trees so that those who are withering, due to separation from their beloved, can engage in madal.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**maṟaiyōr mannum tirunaṟaiyūr** – It is a town where people prove that the  meaning of all vēdas is to engage in madal. How can engaging in madal be the meaning of vēdas? Has it not been said thunderously in vēdas to engage in activities which are against one’s basic nature in order to attain the supreme being, such as *prajñām kurvīta* (keep reminiscing), *dyēya*: (meditate), *sō’nvēśtavya*: (investigate), *dyāyita:* (meditate intently), *upāsitavya*: (engage in devotion), *nidityāsitavya*: (meditate without a break)? īsn’t engaging in madal similar to these? Did not great sages who are great experts in vēdas say “*vyavasādrutē brahman nāsātayati tat param*” (Oh brāhamaṇa! It is not possible to attain supreme being without efforts by self)?

**māmalai pōl mādam** – temple which is tall like a huge mountain. śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 98-121 says “*subhagaś citrakūtō’sau girirājōpamō giri*: I *yasmin vasati kākutsa*: *kubēra iva nandanē* īī” (this citrakūta mountain is auspicious just like tirumālirunjōlai. (This is because) kākutsa lives here just like kubēra lives in his garden). Thus, just like tirumālirunjōlai is called as *girirāja* because the supreme being dwells there, this (tirunaṟaiyūr) is also referred to as *māmalai* (huge mountain). Even though tirunaṟaiyūr is located on a small hillock, due to āḻvār’s devotion towards emperumān, the hillock appears as a huge mountain.

**pon iyalum mādam** – it is a temple which is distinguished like gold, and has the quality of being desired by everyone.

**kavādam kadandu pukku** – entering after somehow managing to go past the door. *kadandu* – going with difficulty. The implied meaning here is that since the beauty of the door would steal the eyes, just like a waylaying robber, it is very difficult to go past it.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 54. Ennudaiya kan kalippa

*ennudaiya kaṇkaḷippa nōkkinēn nōkkudalum mannan tirumārbum vāyum adiyiṇaiyum                                                    74*

**Пословный перевод:**

ennudaiya kaṇ kaḷippa nōkkinēn – I saw such that my eyes rejoiced; nōkkudalum – to the extent that I had seen; mannan – (tirunaṟaiyūr) nambi’s \[emperumān who is complete in all aspects and who resides in tirunaṟiyūr\]; tirumārbum – the chest where pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) resides; vāyum – the divine mouth (which is full of his smile); adi iṇaiyum – the divine feet

**Перевод:**

**kadandu pukku nōkkinēn – Once he \[emperumān at tirunaṟaiyūr\] is seen, just as it is mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 34 “kālāḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇsuḻalum” (my legs will stagger, heart will crumble and the eyes will whirl around (in bewilderment)), it will lead to loss of strength. Since one escaped from it, which will immerse the person in a whirl, it was possible to enter going past the door which is distinguished like gold. The opinion here is that one can only enter before encountering the weakness of seeing him and not after seeing him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**ennudaiya kaṇ kaḷippa nōkkinēn** – since I did not attain him, I became very weak; my eyes burnt and became dry, just like a huge ball of fire would dry up the entire ocean. Now I saw him such that the burning eyes, as mentioned above, became very cool.

**kaḷippa** – just as this āḻvār himself has mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 7-10-9 “*kangaḷāraḷavum ninṟu kaṇṇamangaiyuḷ kaṇḍu koṇḍēnē*” (I saw (emperumān) at tirukkaṇṇamangai, a divine abode, until my eyes became fully satiated), I saw (tirunaṟaiyūr nambi) until my famished eyes became fully satiated with sumptuous food.

**nōkkinēn** – Just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/03/thiruvaimozhi-1-1-3-ilanathu-udaiyanithu/) “*naṇuginam nāmē*” (we were fortunate to attain the emperumān who has auspicious qualities), I, who am so lowly, saw emperumān who is the most distinguished entity. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 69-9 “*manyē prāptā*: *sma tam dēśam bharadvājō yamabravīt*” (I think that we have reached the place mentioned by sage bharadvāja), I reached my target which is like my ācāryan.

**kaḷippa nōkkinēn** – Has it not been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 8-4 “*druśta Eva hi na: śōkam apanēśyati rāghava*:” (rāghava (śrī rāma) is one who removes our sorrows as soon as he sees us)?

**nōkkudalum mannan** – as soon as I saw him, he became my king and made me his servitor. He changed  me from being the king of tirumangai, wielding a sword and shield into parakāla nāyaki \[tirumangai āḻvār in feminine mood\] who would engage in madal as soon as she sees him \[emperumān\].

**nōkkudalum mannan** – Just as it is mentioned in mahābhāratam āśva parvam “*rājādirāja*: *sarvēśām*” (he is the king of kings for all), he \[emperumān\] remains as the cakravarty (emperor) for all the worlds.

**mannan tirumārbum** – Since emperumān’s chest is the holding place for āḻvār as it is where pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi), who is the *puruśakārabhūtai* (one who recommends to emperumān) dwells, āḻvār is looking at his chest first, and not at his face. It could also be said that he is looking at the chest first since emperumān embraces him against his chest and gets rid of āḻvār’s longing due to separation. Another interpretation – due to natural shyness, he is looking at the chest and not at the face, first.

**mannan tirumārbum** – emperumān became the emperor because of pirātti’s connection, just as it is mentioned in “*tiruvirunda pakkam tiṟavidu*” (the entity on whose chest śrī mahālakśmi has taken residence fittingly, is the supreme bring) and in śrī rangarāja satakam “*apānkhā bhūyāmsō yadupari param brahmā tatabhūt*” (he became the supreme entity as pirātti’s glances kept coming his way often).

**vāyum** – why mention about the divine mouth as soon as reference is made to his divine chest? Since emperumān is smilng at pirātti who is residing in his chest, he looks at the divine mouth after seeing the divine chest. The term *vāy* refers to the smile which engages āḻvār. It could also be said that since emperumān is comforting those who had surrendered to him in the presence of pirātti by saying the words *mā śuca*: (do not feel sad), āḻvār is speaking about the divine mouth.

**adiyiṇaiyum** – āḻvār is speaking about the divine feet where one would fall at, after losing out to the chest, the smile and the word (*mā śuca*🙂

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 55. Pannu kara talamum

*pannu kara talamum kaṇgalum pangayattin ponniyal kādōr maṇivarai mēl pūttadu pōl                                                               75*

**Пословный перевод:**

pannu kara talamum – the divine hands which are to be praised; kaṇgaḷum – the divine eyes; ōr maṇi varai mēl ponniyal pangayattin kādu pūttadu pōl – just like a forest of golden lotus blooming on top of a bluish gem coloured mountain (and shining)

**Перевод:**

**pannu kara talamum – When emperumān lifts the person who has fallen at his divine feet and embraces him with his hands, the person will exclaim “is this a hand!” in delirium. pannu karatalam would also mean that emperumān’s divine hands will multiply into hundreds and thousands, because of embracing the person who had surrendered at his divine feet. tiruvāimoḻi 8-1-10 also says “tōḷgaḷ āyirattāy” (you who are having thousand divine shoulders).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kaṇgaḷum** – the divine eyes will look comfortingly at those who he has embraced. Did not periya tirumoḻi say in 7-1-9 “*uyirkkellām tāyāyaḷikkinṟa taṇḍāmaraikkaṇ*” (the cool, comforting eye, which is like a cool lotus, which looks at all creatures like a mother)? While the hands have to stretch to the place where the surrendered person is standing \[in order to embrace him\], the eyes will feel the person, remaining in their place. If the eyes glance at a person, it would appear as if he has been felled by a hailstorm.

**pangayattin ponniyal kādu ōr maṇi varai mēl pūttadu pōl** – just like a forest of golden lotus has blossomed atop a mountain made of bluish gem stone. The forest of golden lotus comprises the divine chest, divine mouth, divine feet, divine hands and divine eyes as mentioned in the previous verses. Alternatively, this could be taken as referring to the beauty of the divine form which combines well with the beauty of the divine jewels which are to be said next.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 56. Minni oli padaippa

*minni oḷi padaippa vīṇāṇum tōḷ vaḷaiyum manniya kuṇḍalamum āramum nīṇmudiyum                                                  76 tunnu veyil viritta cūḷāmaṇi imaippa*

**Пословный перевод:**

minni oḷi padaippa – emitting radiance similar to lightning; vīḻ nāṇum – the waist cord which is much desired; tōḷ vaḷaiyum – the ornaments worn on the shoulders; manniya kuṇḍalamum – the divine ear rings which are fitting well; āramum – the chain (worn on the divine chest); nīḷ mudiyum – the huge divine crown; tunnu veyil viritta cūḷāmaṇi imaippa – the lustrous ruby which is embedded on the radiant locks

**Перевод:**

**min oḷi padaippa – just as it is said in tiruvāimoḻi 4-2-10 “minsey pūṇ mārvinan” (one whose divine chest is decorated with ornaments which are lustrous), having a radiance which will make it appear as if he is wearing lightning as an ornament.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vīṇāṇum** – pirātti is known as *hiraṇyaprākārā* (she is an attribute for emperumān who is like gold). His divine cord worn on the waist appears like a *hiraṇya prākāram* (fort made of gold) for her. His waist cord is like a wave which has been lapped by a beautiful ocean.

**tōḷ vaḷaiyum** – just like a valorous anklet for the divine feet, for the shoulders under which all the three worlds take refuge, he has armlet. These ornaments for the shoulder hide his shoulders to ward off evil eyes.

**manniya kuṇḍalamum** – the shiny, fish-shaped ear rings which appear as if they have blossomed on his ears, as a natural phenomenon.

**āramum** – the chain which appears in a similar way, fitting well on his chest.

**nīṇ mudiyum** – the crown, which has been placed on his head to indicate that he is the head of tirunaṟaiyūr, became the crown to indicate that he is the lord for both the *vibhūtis* (spriritual realm and materialistic realm). nammāḻvār, in his tiruviruttam pāsuram 50 says “*viṇ mudal nāyagan nīṇmudi*” (the long crown of emperumān who is lord of paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)).

**tunnu veyil  viritta cūḷāmaṇi imaippa** – the radiance of the jewel *cūdāmaṇi* (a jewel worn on the crown) was far superior to all the other lustrous materials. *tunnugai* – being superior to. If an entity is very beautiful, people would cast evil eyes on it. To suppress the beauty \[of emperumān\], the radiance from his jewel cūḷāmani shone far superior to all the other ornaments’ radiance, thus hiding his beauty.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 57. Mannu maragada

*mannu maragadak kunṟinmarungēyōr                                                             77 inniḷa vanjikkodi onṟu ninṟadudān annamāy mānāy aṇimayilāy āngidaiyē                                                                78*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannu maragadak kunṟin marungē – near emperumān who remains as a mountain of emerald, being the refuge for all; ōr in iḷa vanji kodi onṟu ninṟadu – pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who is distinguished, enjoyable (for emperumān) and like a youthful creeper, stood; annam āy – like a swan, in her gait; mān āy – like a deer, in her glance; aṇi mayil āy – like a beautiful peacock, in her tresses

**Перевод:**

**mannu maragadak kunṟin marungē – entire world would stand firmly, holding on to emperumān who is like a bluish green mountain.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**marungē** – in his vicinity. Since emperumān is like a huge tank, one cannot enter \[due to the fear of drowning\]. Thus, one can stay in his vicinity and enjoy. Since the *nāyaki* will not look directly at the *nāyaka* and since she cannot sustain herself if she does not look at him, pirātti, the nāyaki here, is standing nearby emperumān such that she could steal a few glances at him.

**marungē** – when one sees a huge flood, one cannot enter it; at the same time, one cannot go away from it too, because of its beauty. Hence, one has to remain nearby. In the same way, pirātti is staying close to emperumān, just as it is said in [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/06/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-1/) “*agalagillēn iṟaiyumenṟu*” (I cannot separate even for a moment)

**ōr inniḷa vanjikkodiyonṟu ninṟadudān** – a youthful creeper, being unique and sweet, stood. The reason for calling her as a youthful creeper is because, in comparison with her, all the other women will have a streak of masculinity in them. She is the epitome of femininity. Just as emperumān is described in śrimad bhāgavatam 10-22-2 as “*sākśāt manmata manmata*:” (bhagavān is like a cupid for cupid himself; he is such handsome), pirātti is the best among women and is similar to emperumān. Since there is none who could be termed this way, āḻvār, with the simile becoming very strong, is calling her as *vanjikkodi* itself \[the term *vanji* has several meanings, one of which is a woman with a very slender waist\].

Having described her overall beauty with the term *inniḷavanjikkodi*, āḻvār now describes the beauty of her inidivual limbs through similes.

**annamāy** – narrates the beauty of her gait.

**mānāy** – narrates the beauty of her glance.

**aṇimayilāy** – narrates the beauty of her tresses.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 58. Minnay ilavey

*minnāy iḷavēyiraṇḍāy iṇaicceppāy munnāya toṇḍaiyāyk keṇḍaikkulamiraṇḍāy                                                 79 anna tiruvuruvam ninṟadu aṟiyādē*

**Пословный перевод:**

idai min āy – like lightning, in the beauty of her waist; iḷa iraṇḍu vēy āy – like two bamboo shoots (for shoulder); iṇai seppu āy – like two domes (for bosoms); mun āya toṇḍai āy – like the fruit of the hedges creeper in the front (for lips); kulam keṇḍai iraṇḍāy – like two great keṇḍai (fresh-water fish, carp or barbus) (for the eyes); anna – such; tiru uruvam – the divine form of śrīdēvi (śri mahālakśmi); ninṟadu aṟiyādē – not knowing that (she) is standing (nearby)

**Перевод:**

**idaiyē min āy – just like lightning amidst clouds, her waist was seen amidst the tresses \[described in the previous verse\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**āngu** – Since there are two parts (upper body and lower body) both above and below, one could, only through guess, know that the waist exists; one cannot see it; its location is so doubtful.

**iḷa vēy iraṇḍāy** – like two young bamboo shoots. This refers to the beauty of the well rounded, long, fresh and straight hands.

**iṇaicceppāy** – the beauty of the bosoms where each golden dome matches well with the other. They are like the jewel caskets protecting the gems of the *nāyaka*. [mūnṟām tiruvandādi – 3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/02/munram-thiruvandhadhi-3/) says “*malarāḷ tanattuḷḷān*” (emperumān dwells amidst the bosoms of pirātti); tiruccandaviruttam 57 says “*sangu tangu mun kai nangai kongai tangaluṟṟavan*” (emperumān resides in the bosom of śrī mahālakśmi who has bangles in her forehand); śrī rangarājastavam says “*śrīstanābharaṇam*” (emperumān is the ornament on her bosom).

**munnāya toṇḍaiyāy** – in the contest among the limbs, the beauty of the reddish fruit like mouth will stand first.

**keṇḍaikkulam iraṇḍāy** – the beauty of the divine eyes is similar to two carps \[fresh-water fish\] with regard to their beauty, flourish, length and form.

**anna tiruvuruvam** – the divine form of such pirātti. Other than saying “such pirātti”, there are no words to describe her beauty. Describing the beauty of a few of the limbs will not tantamount to describing her beauty in full.

**tiruvuruvam ninṟadu aṟiyādē** – not knowing that such inestimable divine form is standing in close proximity. Earlier, the prabandam had spoken about *inniḷavanjikkodi*. That being the case, is it proper now to say that he is not aware? This is because, while he had spoken of a few with great similes, he is expressing his inability to speak about pirātti completely. It is possible to speak about emperumān and pirātti when they are separate; however, when they are together, it is impossible to speak about them in full. Since this is a matter beyond the grasping power of eyes and mind, and since, as  said in [periya tiruvandādi 34](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/11/periya-thiruvandhadhi-34/) “*kāl āḻum nenjaḻiyum kaṇ suḻalum*” (my legs will stagger, heart will crumble and the eyes will whirl around (in bewilderment)) it will immerse the person who sees them together in an ocean of joy and apply a smokescreen for one’s intellect, it is possible to say “Not aware”. The opinion here is that āḻvār is saying “When I came looking for emperumān, I had come without knowing that she will also be with him. Had I known, I would have stayed away and not got entangled in this ocean of beauty).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 59. Ennudaiya nenjum

*ennudaiya nenjum aṟivum inavaḷaiyum                                                                              80 ponniyalum mēgalaiyum āngoḻiyap pōndēṟku mannu maṟikadalum ārkkum madiyugutta                                                               81 innilā inkadirumenṟanakkē veydāgum*

**Пословный перевод:**

ennudaiya nenjum – my heart; aṟivum – my knowledge (which is within that heart); inam vaḷaiyum – distinguished bangles on the hand; pon iyalum mēgalaiyum – golden waist band; oḻiya pōndēṟku – for me who has lost (everyone of the above); mannu maṟi kadalum ārkkum – even the immobile ocean will roar; madi ugutta in nilā in kadirum – the sweet light emitted by the rays of the moon; en tanakkē – only for me; veydu āgum – emitted heat

**Перевод:**

**ennudaiya nenjum – my heart, which will not get affected by anything, broke.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**nenjum aṟivum** – heart is the casket for knowledge. I lost my wealth along with the casket.

**inavaḷaiyum** – bangle, which is an ornament

**ponniyalum mēgalaiyum** – distinguished waist band.

**āngu oḻiyap pōndēṟku** – I became worthless.

**nenjum aṟivum oḻiya** – I became blind

**ina vaḷaiyum mēgalaiyum oḻiya** – I lost my wealth.  Just as when someone goes to a place to make a living, loses whatever (s)he had, I lost everything. Just like straw is thrown here and there by the beautiful waves of an ocean, I ended up engaging in madal.

**mannum maṟikadalum ārkkum** – even the ocean, which used to be calm earlier, makes an uproarious noise now.

**āngu oḻiyap pōndēṟku mannum maṟi kadalum ārkkum** – Ocean, which was created by emperumān to guard the world, is expected to sympatise with those who suffer. Instead of doing that, the ocean roared as if it had seen a traitor to the land and welcomed me.

**madi ugutta innilā inkadirum enṟanakkē veydāgum** – The sweet light emitted by the cool rays of moon will be hot, only for me.

**in nilā – in kadir enṟanakkē veydāgum** – what is comforting for all the people in the world became hot only for me. The term *madi* refers to moon and the term *nilā* refers to its rays. What does the term *kadir* refer to? Since the rays of moon are radiant, this term (*kadir*) refers to the quality of light. If the text is *innilā* (as a single word instead of two words as in *in nilā*), it would mean “this moon” pointing out to the moon which is seen above in the sky.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 60. Tannudaiya tanmai

*tannudaiya tanmai tavirat tān enkolō                                                     82 tennan podiyil seḻum sandin tādu aḷaindu mannivvulagai manangaḷippa vandiyangum                                                  83 inniḷam pūndenṟalum vīsum eri enakkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya tanmai tavira tān en kol – Did anyone do anything to convert the natural quality of coolness of the moon to a hot state like this?; tennan podiyil – in the malaiyamalai (podigai mountain) of the king of pāṇḍyas, who is the chief of the southern region; seḻu sandin tādu aḷaindu – mixing the pollen from the beautiful sandalwood trees; man i ulagai manam kaḷippa vandu iyangum – roaming such that the minds of the people, who are in this permanent world, are happy; in iḷa pū tenṟalum – the southerly breeze which is sweet, youthful \[full of vigour\] and fragrant; enakkē eri vīsum – is blowing fire only for me

**Перевод:**

**tannudaiya tanmai tavirattān enkolō – Just as sītāppirātti had willed the hot agni (deity for fire) to remain cool for hanumān, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 53-29 “śeetōbhava hanūmata:” (Oh agni! Remain cool for hanumān), did anyone will the cool moon “uṣṇōbhava” (remain hot)? Alternatively, instead of considering this only for moon, one could assume this to be true for ocean which had been spoken of earlier and wind etc, which are going to be spoken of next, so that it could be said that someone had willed that the property of various entities should change.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tennan podiyil seḻum sandin tādu aḷaindu mannivvulagai manam kaḷippa vandiyangum inniḷam pūndenṟalum vīsum eri enakkē** – the southerly wind carries with it the pollens of beautiful sandalwood trees which are planted in the podigai hills of pāṇḍya  king. Such breeze, which is sweet, young and fragrant, blows its coolness abundantly on the people of this permanent world in such a way that their minds are overjoyed. However, that breeze blew fire only on me.

**mannivvulagam** – aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār, one of the two commentators for this prabandam, considered the usage as given here and interpreted it as “this world, which gets inundated often as its norm, is permanent” while the other commentator, periyavāccān piḷḷai considered the usage as *manni ivvulagam* and interpreted as “just as enabling everyone in this world to live, as its permanent feature, blowing fire only for me became its permanent feature”. The words should be combined as *manni iyangum*.

**vīsum eri** – (the breeze) standing at quite a distance from me so that the heat of separation that I am suffering from does not singe it, blew fire on me.

**enakkē** – it did this \[blowing fire\] only for me.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 61. Munniya pennaimel

*munniya peṇṇai mēl muḷ muḷarik kūttagattup                                          84 pinnum avvanṟil pedai vāyc ciṟukuralum ennudaiya nenjukkōr īrvāḷām enseygēn                                                            85 kannavil tōḷ kāman karuppuc cilai vaḷaiya*

**Пословный перевод:**

munniya peṇṇai mēl – on the palm tree seen in front; muḷ muḷari kūdu agattu – in the nest made with lotus stem which has thorns; pinnum avvanṟil siṟuvāyc ciṟu pedaiyum – feeble voice emanating from the mouth of the female love bird which has locked its beak with that of the male; ennudaiya nenjukku ōr ir vāḷ Am – it is like a unique sword which cut my heart; en seygēn – what means will I carry out (to escape from this)?; kal navil tōḷ kāman – manmada (cupid) who has (firm) shoulders which are like mountain; karuppuc cilai vaḷaiya – drawing (his) bow made of sugarcane

**Перевод:**

**munniya peṇṇai mēl – on top the palm tree which is in front**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**muḷ muḷarik kūttagattu** – inside the nest made with lotus stems having thorns. The opinion is that since they are inside the nest of thorns, the characteristic of torturing others seems to have come naturally.

**pinnum** – beyond what has been stated already. The male love-bird and its female have intertwined their beaks; their joy of union nurtures her (āḻvār in the mood of parakāla nāyaki) distress of separation.

**avvanṟil pedaivāyc ciṟu kuralum** – with their happiness increasing with their union, the feeble voice from the mouth of the female bird . . . .

**ennudaiya** – already destroyed, my  . . . . .

**nenjukku ōr īr vāḷām** – whatever had been stated earlier had tortured my physical body. This (voice from the female bird) cuts asunder my heart.

**en seygēn** – what will I, a weak woman, do? There seems to be no way to survive.

**kal navil tōl kāman** – kāma (cupid), whose shoulders become harder and firmerlike a stone, as he keeps torturing women repeatedly

**karuppuc cilai vaḷaiya** – even though it \[bow held by him\] may be sweet like sugarcane, there is no shortcoming in its cruelty

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 62. Konnavilum punganaigal

*konnavilum pūngaṇaigaḷ kōttup podavaṇaindu                                 86 tannudaiya tōl kaḻiya vāngit tamiyēnmēl ennudaiya nenjē ilakkāga eyginṟān                                                                 87 pinnidanaik kāppīrdān illaiyē pēdaiyēn*

**Пословный перевод:**

kol navilum pūngaṇaigaḷ kōttu – (on that bow) stringing arrows made of flowers which are capable of killing; podavaṇaindu – standing close (to the target) so that the bows will pierce through well; tannudaiya tōḷ kaḻiya vāngi – pulling (the string of the bow) to reach his shoulder; ennudaiya nenjē ilakkāga tamilyēn mēl eyginṟān – keeping my heart as the target he (cupid) is shooting those arrows on me, who is having none to support; pin idanai kāppīrdān illaiyē – there is none who could now enable me to escape from this danger

**Перевод:**

**kol navilum pūngaṇaigaḷ kōttu – even though these are arrows of flowers, they will not fail from killing**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**poda aṇaindu** – standing closely in such a way that if the arrow is released, it will enter one side, pierce through the target without missing, and exit from the other side.

**tannudaiya tōḷ kaḻiya vāngi** – since the chest is pulled back while releasing the arrow, the shoulders will bend back a lot further; alternatively, this implies that the arrow is pulled back (prior to releasing) such that the hands reach the shoulder.

**tamilyēn mēl** – Look at the target of his arrow! He is a valorous soldier who will torture a helpless damsel.

**ennudaiya nenjē ilakkāga** – he excels śrī rāma in his valour! śrī rāma will target only those which (or who) have a form. He is targeting my formless heart!

**eyginṟān** – since this term is in the present tense, it implies that *kāman* (cupid) continues to target her and does not step back. Just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 94-18 “S*aravarśam vavarśa ha* ” (he rained a hailstorm of arrows), śrī rāma showed his valour when he saw the main army of rāvaṇa while *kāman* shows his valour on seeing my heart.

**ennudaiya nenjē** – even if one of the arrows that he shot at me had fallen on him, he would have realised how the suffering is; since that did not happen, he is indulging in this act of cruelty.

**pin idanaik kāppīrdān illaiyē** – When śrī rāma threatened to invert the world as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 64-78 “*jagat saśailam parivatayāmyaham*” (I will destroy the world with its mountains), there was an intelligent person such as lakśmaṇa as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-4 “*lakśmaṇasya ca dīmata*:” (lakśmaṇa too, who is wise), who spoke comforting words to śrī rāma and took his bow. Now, she is wailing whether there is none who would take the bow from kāman’s hand; she is lamenting the absence of one who would carry out the role of puruśakāram (one who recommends/advises another person).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.
