## 63. Kannavilum kattagattor

*kannavilum kāttagattōr vallik kadimalarin                                                    88 nannaṟu vāsam maṟṟu ārānum eydāmē mannum vaṟu nilattu vāḷāngu uguttadupōl                                             89 ennudaiya peṇmaiyum ennalanum en mulaiyum*

**Пословный перевод:**

kal navilum kādu agattu – in the forest which is full of stones; ōr valli – (blossomed) in a creeper; kadi malarin – in a flower full of honey; nal naṟu vāsam – great fragrance; maṟṟu ārānum eydāmē – not being useful for anyone; mannum vaṟu nilattu āngu vāḷā uguttadu pōl – being wasted on the hard ground; pēdaiyēn ennudaiya peṇmai – my femininity, I being ignorant; en nalanum – my qualities; en mulaiyum – my bosom

**Перевод:**

**pēdaiyēn – I am a helpless girl, born only to suffer from this danger. While I should perish since I am unable to sustain myself being separated from him, I am still desirous to live with the hope of seeing him once again.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kal navilum kāttagattu** – Inside the forest which is full of stones. The *svāpadēśa* (inner) meaning for this is just as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-8 “*perum tūṟāy*” (this samsāram is like a huge bush), inside the forest of this samsāram which is full of suffering.

**ōr valli** – A unique creeper in that forest. The *svāpadēśa* meaning for this is – a jīvātmā (sentient entity).

**kadi malarin** – a flower which is full of honey in that creeper. The *svāpadēśa* meaning for this is – the sweet knowledge of the jīvātmā.

**nannaṟu vāsam** – distinguished fragrance from that flower. *svāpadēśa* meaning – bhakti (devotion) etc which are born out of that knowledge.

**maṟṟārānum eydāmē** – this fragrance is not meant for the flower but for others; but they are not attaining it. In *svāpadēśam* this indicates that emperumān is not the focus for that bhakti.

**vaṟu nilattu vāḷāngu uguttadu pōl** – just as it falls on the coarse ground and gets wasted. In *svāpadēśam* this implies that the bhakti which is meant for emperumān is wasted on lowly entities (other deities\].

**ennudaiya peṇmaiyum en nalanum en mulaiyum** – In *svāpadēśam*, *peṇmai* refers to the basic nature of *pāratantriyam* (exclusively dependent on emperumān); *nalam* refers to the distinguished qualities of ātmā and *mulai* refers to bhakti which is enjoyable \[for emperumān\].

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 64. Mannu malarmangai

*mannu malarmangai maindan kaṇapurattup                                                           90 pon malai pōl ninṟavanṟan ponnagalam tōyāvēl ennivai tān vāḷā enakkē poṟaiyāgi                                                                                   91*

**Пословный перевод:**

malar mangai mannum mandan – being the supreme being who is firmly attained by periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who resides on a flower; kaṇapurattup ponmalai pōl ninṟavan than – emperumān who stands as a golden mountain in tirukkaṇṇapuram, his; pon agalam tōyāvēl – we have not attained his beautiful, divine chest; vāḷā ivai tān en – what is the use of these wasted things?; ivai – these bosoms; enakkē poṟaiyāgi – they have become an unwanted burden even for me

**Перевод:**

**mannum malarmangai maindan – the words are to rearranged as malarmangai mannum maindan – he is the forever young entity in whom pirātti who resides on a lotus flower dwells permanently.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**maindan** – Just as viṣṇu sūktam in rig vēdam says “*yuvā kumāra*:” (he is forever youthful )and just as yajur vēdam says “*yuvā suvāsā*:” (he is always youthful since periya pirātti dwells in him forever), he is forever young.

**kaṇapurattup ponmalai pōl ninṟavan** – one who stood at tirukkaṇṇapuram forever, with a distinguished form such as mēru mountain (a celestial mountain).

**kaṇapurattu** – it is not a mountain which is very difficult to reach such as mēru. He is a mountain who is seen by everyone.

**pon agalam** – When water is very sweet to taste, one who drinks it will repeatedly cry out “Water”. In the same way, his divine chest is so distinguished that pirātti who resides on it permanently, will keep thinking about it.

**tōyāvēl** – I have not attained! The long syllable at the end (*ēl*) denotes the pain suffered by parakāla nāyaki in not embracing with that divine chest.

**tōyāvēl** – Since emperumān is referred to as “*maragada maṇittadam*” (pond made of emerald gem), “*vāsattadam*” (pond where  pirātti dwells) and “*śītamiva hradam*” (cool pond), she laments that she has not wet herself in the cool pond of his chest to get rid of the pangs of separation. Just as tirunedundāṇḍagam 13 says “*melliyal tōl tōyndāy*” (embracing the slender pirātti’s shoulders), whenever emperumān feels the pangs of separation, it is on the cool pond of her shoulders that he embraces. aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār, one of the commentators for this prabandam says “ Since he has beautiful chest, she thinks that she can embrace him when she melts down due to the pangs of separation. īsn’t she too a *hiraṇyavarṇai* (golden hued)?” *hiraṇyavarṇām* is from śrīsūktam. Based on the ślōkam from śrī viṣṇu purāṇam “*strīnāmnī lakshmi*” (all women are attributes of lakshmi), this interpretation assumes prakāla nāyaki to be pirātti herself.

**ennivaidān vāḷā** – just as śrī rāma told lakśmaṇa in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-44 “*paśya lakśmaṇa puśpāṇi niśpalāni bhavanti mē*” (lakśmaṇa! Look at the flowers which are of no use to me), since the flowers which sītāppiratti did not enjoy  are of no use to him, parakāla nāyaki too says that there is no use of her qualitites, her femininity and her bosoms which are not enjoyed by him.

**enakkē poṟaiyāgi** – I carry these bosoms because they are to be enjoyed by him. When he is not enjoying them, could I carry them on my own? They are an unnecessary burden even for me. Did not āṇḍāḷ too say in nācciyār tirumoḻi 13-8 “*koḷḷum payan onṟillāda kongai tannaik kiḻangōdum aḷḷippaṟittittu avan mārvil eṟindu en aḻalai tīrvanē*” (I will uproot these useless bosoms , throw them on that kriṣṇa’s chest and get rid of my suffering)?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 65. Munnirundu mukkinru

*munnirundu mūkkinṟu mūvāmaik kāppadōr mannu marundu aṟivīr illaiyē – māl vidaiyin                                                                    92*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun irundu – being present right in front of my eyes; mūkkinṟu – ageing more and more; mūvāmai kāppadu ōr – to prevent it from aging like that; mannu marundu aṟivīr illaiyē – no one is there who has seen a great medicine; māl vidaiyin – among the bulls which are infatuated (with cows)

**Перевод:**

**munnirundu mūkkinṟu – it is ageing right in front of my eyes. Should they (bosoms) not be embraced at the appropriate time instead of delaying?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**munnirundu** – if they are located behind, on the back, and get aged, one could tolerate that. But they are present right in the front, on the chest, and losing their freshness.

**mūkkinṟu** – getting aged. She is speaking just the way by which śrī rāmapirān said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 5-5 “*na mē du:kkam priyā dūrē na mē du:kkam hrudēti vā* I *ētad ēvānuśōcāmi vayō’syā hyativarttatē* īī ” (I am not feeling distressed that the one who is dear to my heart is at a far away place or that she has been abducted. I am only feeling sad that she is ageing). It is similar to nācciyār tirumoḻi pāsuram 1-5 “*unnitteḻunda en tadamulaigaḷ*” (my large bosoms which have risen only for the sake of kriṣṇa).

**munninṟu mūkkinṟu** – Should they lose their freshness right in front of my eyes? I could tolerate if they lose their freshness away from my eyesight.

**mūvāmai . . . . .aṟivīr illaiyē** – Is there none who would protect them from ageing by bringing him \[to me\]?

**mannu marundu aṟivīr illaiyē** – emperumān is called as *bhēśajam* (medicine) in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam. Is there none who knows this medicine which never fails? Just as vibhīśaṇa instructed in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “*samudram rāghavō rājā śaraṇam gandumarhati*” (śrī rāma should surrender to the king of ocean) isn’t there anyone who would say “Surrender to the one engaging in madal”? nāyanār, the commentator for this prabandam, quotes nampiḷḷai who has mercifully said  “I have heard bhattar mercifully saying for this pāsuram – is’nt there any *ōrāndarāḷigan* in this world?” *ōrāndarāḷigan* is a messenger who resolves the love-fights between the *nāyaka* and the *nāyaki*. Another meaning: just as it is said in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-7-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvaimozhi-5-7-2-angurren-allen/) “*anguṟṟēnallēn inguṟṟēnallēn*” isn’t there a mumūkśu (one who desires to reach mōkśam) who has neither reached śrīvaikuṇṭam nor going with the people of the world, who could act as a messenger?

After she has cried out *mannu marundu aṟivīr illaiyē*, parakāla nāyaki narrates incidents which deteriorated her state further.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 66. Tunnu pidareruttu

*tunnu pidareruttut tūkkuṇḍu vantodarāl kanniyar kaṇ miḷirak kattuṇḍu mālaivāyt                                                        93 tannudaiya nāvoḻiyādu ādum tanimaṇiyin*

**Пословный перевод:**

tunnu pidar eruttu – in the hump of the large nape; tūkkuṇḍu – being hung; kanniyar kaṇ miḷira – such that young girls will roll their eyes (on seeing it); van todarāl kattuṇḍu – being tied by a strong rope; mālai vāy – during evening time; tannudaiya nā oḻiyādu ādum tani maṇiyin – with the gong of the unparalleled bell striking continuously

**Перевод:**

**tunnu pidar eruttut tūkkuṇḍu – hung from the hump on the dense neck**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**van todarāl kattuṇḍu** – being tied by a strong chain such that even if some righteous people, after seeing me suffer on hearing the sound of the bell, wish to remove the bell, it cannot be removed.

**kanniyar tam kaṇ miḷirak kattuṇḍu** – young girs, who are unaware of love, would get up from their sleep with a start on hearing the bell; it is tied to that extent.

**mālaivāy** – it is that time when the love-quarrel between the bull and the cow is settled and their union takes place. It is that time when the *nāyaki* (female lead in an event or story) feels distressed that the *nāyakan* (male lead in the event or story) is not uniting with her.

**tannudaiya nā oḻiyādu ādum** – the cow, not agreeing to the union, is running away. The bull, since it desires the cow, is chasing it. Due to this, the bell, tied to the neck, is ringing continuously. This noise reminds the nāyaki about the union that she had in the past with the nāyaka and, torments her.

**nā oḻiyādu ādum** – Even if the bell does not ring for some time, she could be without these thoughts. But it is ringing continuously and is torturing her.

**tani maṇiyin** – it is a bell which has nothing equivalent to it in torturing her.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 67. Innisai osaiyum

*innisai ōsaiyum vanden sevi tanakkē                                                             94 konnavilum ehkil kodidāy nedidāgum ennidanaik kākkumā solleer – idu viḷaitta                                                 95*

**Пословный перевод:**

in isai ōsaiyum vandu – coming with a sound as sweet as music; en sevi tanakkē – only for my ears; kol navilum ehkil kodidy āy nedidu āgum – it is longer than the spear which is capable of killing, and more cruel than that; idanai kākkum A en – what is the way to escape from this grave danger?; solleer – Please say (if there is a means available); idu viḷaitta – having created such a situation (by southerly breeze etc \[which have been mentioned earlier\])

**Перевод:**

**innisai ōsai – the sound which melted me just as those who listen to sweet music melt. For the others, this would mean a sound which has sweet music.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vanden sevi tanakkē** – what is sweet music for the others was cruel only for my ears.

**sevi tanakkē** – before it strikes the heart, it is coming too hot even for the ears.

**kol navilum ehkil kodiyadāy** – it is more cruel than a spear which will kill even by merely looking at it.

**nedidāgum** – Spear will kill a person by piercing just once; with that, the sorrow will be over. However, this tortures for a long time.

**ennidanaik kākkumā** – by which way will I protect my femininity?

**solleer** – You people, please tell me.If I have to protect my femininity, the torture continues. If I have to avoid this torture, I have to engage in madal, destroying my femininity.

**ennidanaik kākkumā** – How will I, a weak person, escape from this?

**solleer** – Only you peope, who are not affected in any way, after hearing this sound \[of the bull’s bell\] should show me the means.

Now, she says that she will not step back from getting him, even if she has to engage in madal, say ignominious things about him and torturing him.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 68. Mannan narundulay

*mannan naṟunduḻāy vāḻmārban māmadikōḷ munnam vidutta mugil vaṇṇan kāyāvin                                                       96 cinna naṟum pūndigaḻvaṇṇan vaṇṇam pōl*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannan – being great; naṟu tuḻāy vāḻ mārban – having his chest decorated with the fragrant tuḷasi; munnam mā madi kōḷ vidutta mugil vaṇṇan – having the complexion of rain bearing clouds, who, in earlier times, had removed candra (moon)’s distress; kāyāvin cinna naṟu pū tigaḻ vaṇṇan – having a complexion similar to the small flower with sweet fragrance, from the flowering plant kāyā (a bilberry plant); vaṇṇam pōl – similar to his complexion

**Перевод:**

**idu viḷaitta mannan – he is a great, valorous person who made entities such as southerly breeze, love-birds, sound from the bull’s bell, moon, sandalwood paste etc to wage a war on me, who by nature is very weak. Alternative explanation – it was he who made me engage with madal. Another interpretation – he created in me love for him, made me like this and enslaved me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannan** – he is the king for making those who see him, to engage in madal, to attain him.

**mannan** – he shines more and more as his beloved becomes distressed more and more.

**narum tuḻāy** – this is the brahmāśtram (a highly potent weapon) which he donned in order to torment me like this. As soon as he wears this garland on his chest, the fragrance pervades everywhere. īsn’t he the one with all fragrances!

**vāḻ mārban** – Should only tuḷasi survive on his chest? ṣouldn’t I survive?

**vāḻ mārban** – Once tuḷasi touches his chest, it sprouts more than ever before. If one sees his shoulders and the garland on the shoulders, how is it possible not to engage in madal?

**naṟum tuḻāy vāḻ mārban** – he is donning the garland to manifest his victory of making her to engage in madal. Just as madal is for her \[inseparable from her due to her distress\], the crown and garland are for him \[signs of his supremacy\].

**māmadi** – did she not say that there were enemies for them when she stated at the beginning of this verse *idu viḷaitta*? She is now citing an example of such an enemy.

**māmadi** – He \[*māmadi*, the moon\] is great in distressing weak girls. Even if you \[this term refers to emperumān, as stated by parakāla nāyaki\] had not removed their sorrow, he is capable of tormenting us. If sorrow is removed for such a person \[moon\], should one ask for the extent to which he will go to cause trouble to others?

**māmadi kōl munnam vidutta mugil vaṇṇan** – emperumān is the magnanimous entity who removed candran’s sorrow caused by *rāhu*.

**munnam** – once upon a time. He had removed candran’s sorrow once upon a time. emperumān, who was a protector for others earlier, has now become the trouble creator. He, who had protected a  *prayōjānantaparan* (one who looks for other benefits) such as candran earlier, is hurting an *ananyaprayōjanan* (one who considers only emperumān as the ultimate benefit) such as prakālanāyaki, now.

**mugil vaṇṇan** – one who has the nature of cloud. Just as cloud pours its rain on a water body (such as a river or ocean) and keeps the fields dry, emperumān also protects a *prayōjānantaparan* and causes distress to an *ananyaprayōjanan.* Alternative explanation: one who has the nature of cloud, who protected candran earlier without expecting anything in return, is hurting me now.

**kāyāvin cinna naṟumpūndigaḻ vaṇṇan** – one who has the divine complexion of the small *kāyām* pū (flower of a bilberry plant) which is small and very fragrant. Even if the entire town is against her, his divine form is such that he cannot be given up.

**cinna pū** – small flower. Even though emperumān is a great entity, he makes himself easily approachable. *cinnam* – pollen dust; flower with pollen grains.

**cinna naṟum pū** – even though it is small, the flower has lot of fragrance. Fragrance is not limited to the size of flower; in the same way, emperumān’s fame is not limited to him.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 69. Anna kadalai

*anna kadalai malaiyittu aṇaikatti                                                                            97 mannan irāvaṇanai māmaṇḍu venjamattu*

**Пословный перевод:**

anna kadalai – ocean which has that complexion; malai ittu – throwing mountains; aṇai katti – building sētu (and mercifully entering lankā); mā maṇḍu vem samattu – in the cruel battle which was full of four types of armies \[chariot, elephant, horse and foot soldiers\]; mannan irāvaṇanai – the demon king rāvaṇa

**Перевод:**

**vaṇṇam pōl anna kadalai – he, of course, killed rāvaṇa; but did he have to agitate the ocean which matched him in depth, being the repository for all entities, pride and complexion?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**malaiyittu aṇai katti** – one who destroyed the profundity of the ocean by throwing whatever stones one could lay his hands on, and making the army of monkeys walk over those stones. The opinion here is that, he destroyed my profundity too and made me engage in madal.

**mannan irāvaṇanai** – ravaṇa, who had a self-respect of being the king of demons.

**mā maṇḍu venjamattu** – the term *mā* refers to animals with four legs such as elephants and horses. Here this refers to all the four divisions of the army (chariot, elephant, horse and foot soldiers). In the cruel battle, where the four divisions of the army were closely held together.

**venjamattu** – it is not that he killed the ignorant rāvaṇa in any clairvoyant manner. He stood face-to-face with rāvaṇa in the battle which was termed as “*rāmarāvaṇayōr yuddam rāma rāvaṇayōriva*” ((match for the) battle between śrī rāma and rāvaṇa is this battle between śrī rāma and rāvaṇa; in other words, there is no equal) and killed rāvana.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 70. Pon mudigal pattum

*pon mudigaḷ pattum puraḷac caram turandu                                    98 tennulagm ēṟṟuvitta sēvaganai āyirakkaṇ mannavan vānamum vānavar tam ponnulagum                                          99 tannudaiya tōḷ valiyāl kaikkoṇḍa dānavanai*

**Пословный перевод:**

pon mudigaḷ pattum puraḷa – making the ten beautiful heads to roll (on the ground); saram turandu – raining arrows; then ulagam ēṟṟuvitta sēvaganai – as a great warrior who made (that rāvaṇa) to reach yamalōka (hell); āyiram kaṇ mannavan vānamum – the svargalōka (heaven) belonging to the thousand eyed indra; vānavar tam ponnulagum – the divine words of (other) dēvatās (celestial deities); tannudaiya tōḷ valiyāḷ kaikkoṇḍa dānavanai – hiraṇyāsura who made them his, through the strength of his shoulders

**Перевод:**

**pon mudigaḷ – not respecting his opponent, śrī rāmapirān, thinking “He is, after all, only a human being” and came to the battlefield with crowns on his heads.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pattum puraḷa** – \[śrī rāma\] separated the ten heads as well as his life \[from rāvaṇa’s body\]. The syllable *um* (in *pattum*) refers to ‘life’ which has not been stated directly. This implies the speed with which the arrow pierced rāvaṇa’s body such that life also could not escape. Another interpretation is that \[śrī rāma\] did not spare even one head and made them all bite dust.

**saram turandu** – due to the boon that rāvaṇa had been given, once his head is severed, it will grow back again. This term *saram turandu* denotes the speed with which śrī rāma rained the arrows on rāvaṇa so that even as each head reappreaed, it was severed once again.

**tennulagam ēṟṟuvitta sēvaganai** – when rāvaṇa was the all-powerful demon, all the people in *yamalōka* (hell) left it, so that it was in ruins, and carried out menial tasks for rāvaṇa. Now, rāvaṇa and his army, having been annihilated, went to hell and carried out menial tasks there, making it full and active again. śrī rāma is celebrated for his valour, by this verse. Due to śrī rāma, that world which was in ruins, became *tennulagam* (beautiful world).

**sēvaganai** – the opinion here is  “You are all going to see how such a valorlous person is going to suffer at my hands” \[since parakāla nāyaki is going to engage in madal on emperumān, by spoiling his reputation\].

(*āyirakkaṇ . . . . vīranai*) Starting with the world *āyirakkaṇ* and ending with *vīranai*, parakāla nāyaki says that she is going to destroy the fame of emperumān who killed the demon *hiraṇya* \[hiraṇya kaśyap, the father of prahlāda\].

**āyirakkaṇ** – he is provided with a thousand eyes since he has to rule over the long *svargalōka* (heaven).

**āyirakkaṇ mannavan vānamum** – the three worlds over which indra, who has been given a thousand eyes, has to be aware of and rule over. *vānam* – svargam. The *um* in vānamum refers to the other two worlds, earth and *antarikśa*, the intermediate world, which have not been explicitly mentioned.

**vānavar tam ponnulagum** – the beautiful worlds of other deities such as yama, sūrya et al.

**tannudaiya tōḷ valiyāl** – these \[worlds\] were not granted \[to hiraṇya\] as a gift by anyone or received as a boon from any deity or obtained through penance. These were obtained through his valour. If he marks the boundaries of his kingdom (in the celestial worlds) those deities \[who were the original owners of such worlds\] would leave their lands on their own.

**kaikkoṇḍa dānavanai** – the demon whose nature is such that he will usurp

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 71. Pinnor ariyuruvamagi

*pinnōr ariyuruvamāgi eri viḻittuk                                                     100 konnavilum venjamattuk kollādē vallāḷan mannu maṇikkunji paṟṟi varavīrttut                                               101*

**Пословный перевод:**

pin – after passage of some time; ōr ari uruvam āgi – incarnating as an incomparable narasinga mūrtty (lion face and human body); eri viḻittu – looking with fiery eyes; kol navilum vem samattuk kollādē – not killing in battlefield where people are killed; vallāḷan – that hugely strong demon’s; mannu maṇi kunji paṟṟi vara īrttu – pulling closely, the hair which is decorated with crown made of gems

**Перевод:**

**pin – after some time had passed. Since emperumān is an ocean of mercy, he waited patiently with the hope that the demon would mend his ways.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**ōr ari uruvam āgi** – He became an incomparable lion after the demon crossed the limit of insulting emperumān’s devotee. He became part lion, part human, a form unseen before in the world.

**ōr ari** – lion, without any parallel. Even if emperumān, who is a *satya sankalpan* (whatever he wills, would happen without fail) desires, he cannot take a form like this again.

**āgi** – he combined human and lion entities which are incongruous in nature in order to destroy hinraṇya who was vain since he had taken boons \[from other deities\] that his death will not come about either at the hands of humans or animals etc.

**eri viḻittu** – his eyes are always cool like lotus, since he is the mother for all the entities, by his nature. However, when he sees any disrespect shown to his devotees, his eyes would turn fiery due to anger.

**kol navilum vem samattuk kollādē** – If hiraṇya were to be killed in a battlefield by shooting an arrow at him and making his head roll on the earth, that would be like a crown on his head. Instead of that, he had to be tortured.

**vallāḷan** – he had strength in his heart which could not bear the fame that his child acquired and a strength in his body which made even narasimha to take a step back.

**mannu maṇikkunji paṟṟi vara īrttu** – \[emperumān\] first pulled the crown \[of hiraṇya\] which had made him to order his subjects to chant his name in their prayer by saying “*hiaṇyāya nama*:”

**manikkunji paṟṟi vara iṟttu** – Since hiraṇya had not been insulted by anyone until then, his life almost left him out of shame, when emperumān pulled him by his crown. Then, emperumān dragged him as if he were a corpse.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 72. Tannudaiya talmel

*tannudaiya tāḷmēl kidātti avanudaiya ponnagalam vaḷḷugirāl pōḻndu pugaḻ padaitta                                 102 minnilangum āḻip padaittadakkai vīranai*

**Пословный перевод:**

tannudaiya tāḷ mēl kidātti – making (hiraṇya) to lie on his lap; avanudaiya – his; pon agalam – beautiful chest; vaḷ ugirāl pōḻndu – parted asunder with his sharp nails; pugaḻ padaitta – getting the fame (of having protected his follower, prahlāda); min ilangum āḻi padai tadaikkai vīranai – being a great warrior who has the divine cakrāyudam (disc) which is radiant like lightning

**Перевод:**

**tannudaiya tāḷ mēl kidātti – on \[emperumān’s\] lap where periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) will recline, instead of making me \[prakāla nāyaki\] recline, will he make a rude entity to recline? One of the commentators for this prabandam, aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār quotes nampiḷḷai who has said that he had heard from bhattar (parāśara bhattar) who said that parakāla nāyaki is saying “He should have allowed people like me to approach his lap; on the divine feet which are the goal for his followers, how is he keeping a demonic entity? ”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**avanudaiya ponnagalam** – the demon’s name is hiraṇyan which means one made of gold. Thus his chest is golden hued. Alternatively, just as a sacrificial lamb is nurtured well, his chest is made to look beautiful so that it can be an apt prey for emperumān’s divine nails.

**vaḷḷugirāl pōḻndu** – Since it was golden chest, it melted under the fiery eyes of emperumān. Then, with his beautiful nails, emperumān tore it just like one tears the rind of a banana plant.

**pugaḻ padaitta** – emperumān gained the fame that even when one’s \[biological\] father turns inimical, he would be the father without any cause and that he is the generous person who would transform himself in order to protect his devotee.

**minnilangum āḻi** – the fat, huge body of hiraṇyan was a small feed for emperumān’s divine nails. The divine disc, since it could not get any prey, roared and let out effulgence, sticking out its tongue. Did not nammāḻvār too say in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-8-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/04/thiruvaimozhi-4-8-7-kilaroliyal-kuraivilla/) “*kiḷaroḷiyāl kuṟaivillā ariyuruvāyk kiḷartteḻundu kiḷaroḷiya iraṇiyanadagal mārvam kiḻittuganda vaḷaroḷiya kanalāḻi valampuriyan*” (emperumān has the divine disc with its blazing flame and the divine conch even as he appeared with a rage as narasimha and tore apart into two, the broad chest of hiraṇya who had a glowing lustre).

**vīranai** – āḻvār recalls the nrusimha anuśtup mantra which is famous in nrusimha  
tāpanīyōpaniśat “*ugram vīram*” (the valorous person who is angry).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 73. Mannivvagalidattai

*manniv vagalidattai māmudunīr tān viḻungap                                    103 pinnum ōr ēnamāyp pukku vaḷai maruppin konnavilum kūrnudi mēl vaittedutta kūttanai                                 104*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannu i agal idattai – this expansive earth where everyone is fitting well; mā mudu nīr tāṇ viḻunga – when māhapraḷayam (deluge) swallowed; pinnum – after that; ōr ēnamāy pukku – entered (the ocean) as a divine boar; vaḷai maruppin – of the curved tusks; kol navilum kūr nudi mēl – on top of the sharp edges which are capable of killing enemies; vaittu edutta kūttanai – one who did a great activity, carrying (that earth)

**Перевод:**

**mannu ivvagalidattai . . . . . kūttanai – parakāla nāyaki says that the fame of one, who protected not only prahlāda, who had knowledge and firmness of mind, but also those who were not even aware that they were suffering, by looking only at the relationship \[between samsāris and himself\] and protected them during the danger of deluge, is also going to get destroyed.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannivvagalidattai** – this property of vāmana, which has been his for a very long time. *mannu* – standing for a long time. This would also mean the earth where everyone is fitting well.

**māmudu nīr tān viḻunga** – just as it has been mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*adbhya pritvī*” (earth formed from water), water, which created earth, swallowed it. Just as a person who is posted to guard something steals it, ocean, which was the entity to protect earth, set out to swallow it.

**pinnum** – there is no end to protection.It will keep continuing.

**ōr ēnam** – Incomparable wild boar. If one were to look at the beauty of the divine body which he had created to destroy his greatness, it would make that person \[who sees that divine body\] to carry out something to ward off evil eyes.

**ēnam** – form of wild boar which lives on both marshy land and in water.

**konnavilum kūrnudi mēl** – the tusks of the boar were so sharp that one can escape if one were favourable to emperumān. However if one were inimical to him, the tusks will destroy the person by killing him.

**vaittu edutta** – carrying (earth) on the sharp edge of the tusks.

**kūttanai** ***–*** it is implied that if one were to see this event, it vouldn’t matter if deluge comes everyday.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 74. Mannum vadamalaiyai

*mannum vadamalaiyai mattāga māsuṇattāl minnum irusudarum viṇṇum piṟangoḷiyum                                                       105 tanninudanē suḻala malait tirittāngu innamudam vānavarai ūtti avarudaiya                                                              106 mannum tuyar kadinda vaḷḷalai – maṟṟanṟiyum*

**Пословный перевод:**

mannum vada malaiyai – the mantara (a celestial) mountain which was ancolred firmly; mattu āga – as a churning shaft; māsuṇattāl – with a snake (vāsuki, encircling); minnum iru sudarum piṟangu oḷiyum tanninudanē suḻala malai tiruttu – churning that mantara mountain (churning the ocean) such that the radiant moon, sun, sky and other lustrous entities spun around with him; vānavarai in amudam ūtti – offering sweet nectar to celestial entities; mannum tuyar kadinda – one who drove away their long standing fear of death; vaḷḷalai – the greatly generous entity

**Перевод:**

**From mannum vadamalaiyai to vaḷḷalai, emperumān had, more than protecting the samsāris who did not obstruct when he saved them from the dangers of deluge, carried out beneficial act, hurting his divine body, for the vain celestial entities who had the egotistical attitude of “I am the lord”. parakāla nāyaki says that she is going to destroy this fame too.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannum vadamalaiyai** – the *mandara* mountain which holds earth firmly. This refers to emperumān anchoring it firmly so that it sustains itself well and also to the fact that it did not disintegrate when it was used for churning the ocean.

**mattāga māsuṇattāl** – emperumān used the mountain as the churning staff and *vāsuki*, the snake, as rope for churning the mountain.

**minnum irusudarum viṇṇum** – shining moon and sun, the two sources  of light, and sky, which is their place of dwelling

**piṟangoḷiyum** – other stars etc which shine and emit light

**tanninudanē suḻala** – churning the ocean in such a way that these entities also spun around with him, due to the pace of churning

**malai tirittu āngu innamudam vānavarai ūtti avarudaiya mannum tuyar kadinda vaḷḷalai** – when the celestial entities became enervated after churning for a short time, emperumān, the supremely generous entity, churned the ocean with his thousand shoulders, killed the demons who were inimical to the celestials, offered nectar, a wondrous medicine,  to the celestials and removed their long standing sorrow.

**mannum tuyar kadinda** – death to the celestials is like poverty in this samsāram; the opinion here is that he removed this poverty of celestials by offering them nectar. Alternative explanation is that he removed the firmly established poverty \[of celestials\] which was caused by the curse of sage *durvāsa*.

**vaḷḷalai** – he is such a generous entity that he will offer himself to his followers. But, when people ask him for other benefits, just as asking for a loin-cloth from *kaṟpaka vrukśam* (wish-fulfilling tree) he will bestow those benefits on such people. After helping them, he will call them as “generous people” since they had helped him (in protecting them), just as he mentioned in [bhagavat gītā ślōkam 7-18](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-18/) “*udārā*:” (they are generous). tirumaḻisaippirān too said in tiruccanda viruttam 88 “*uḷḷa nōygaḷ tīrmarundu vānavarkkaḷitta em vaḷḷalār*” (emperumān who is the generous entity who gave the medicine of nectar to celestials to cure their disease).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 75. Tannuruvam arum

*tannuruvam ārum aṟiyāmal tānangōr                                           107 mannum kuṟaḷ uruvinmāṇiyāy māvali tan ponniyalum vēḷvikkaṇ pukkirundu pōṟvēndan                           108*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṟṟu anṟiyum – apart from that; than uruvam ārum aṟiyāmal – making his nature unknown to anyone else; tān ōr mannum kuṟaḷ uruvin māṇi āy – he, who is the most supreme among all men, made himself into a divine dwarf bachelor; māvali than – mahābali’s; pon iyalum vēḷvikkaṇ pukkirundu – taking abode in the yāgabhūmi (the place where mahābali conducted the ritual) where gold was being gifted

**Перевод:**

**Starting from maṟṟanṟiyum until tāḷānai, the narration is about emperumān’s vāmanāvatāram \[incarnation as vāmana, the dwarf\]. When he churned the ocean and helped the vain celestial entities, he did not lower his greatness. However, when he helped them again, he gave up all his fame, transformed himself as a vāmana (dwarf) and sought alms from mahābali. parakāla nāyaki says that she will destroy this great quality of simplicity too, in emperumān.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**maṟṟanṟiyum** – More than that. This narrates emperumān’s quality of simplicity which is the exact opposite of his quality of greatness.

**tannuruvam ārum aṟiyāmal** – those who saw him \[emperumān, as vāmana\] thought that he was always like this, seeking alms. None would know that he is the one who gave the four *puruśārtams* (ultimate benefits) through his four huge shoulders.

**ārum aṟiyāmal** – even periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) and nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam) did not know that \[he is vāmana\].

**tān angu pukku** – he, who is to be attained by everyone, went to the yājñaśālā (place where mahābali was conducting the ritual).

**ōr mannum kuṟaḷ uruvil** –  disguising himself as *vāmana* (dwarf), without any comparison and with such beauty that those who saw him could not take their eyes off him and look at any other person or object.

**māṇiyāy** – keeping his countenance with equanimity and with a blossomed face, whether the person from whom he sought alms offered him anything or shut the door on his face. A bachelor whose form had got scarred while taking alms \[as if he had known only seeking alms all his life\].

**māvali than pon iyalum vēḷvikkaṇ** – in the beautiful ritual of mahābali. Unlike parakāla nāyaki who had to attempt at engaging in madal in order to attain him \[emperumān\], the ritual was such great that it was able to bring emperumān, who is the goal of the ritual, to it. The opinion is that had she not undertaken madal but had undertaken a ritual like mahābali, emperumān would have come to her too. Alternatively, this would mean a ritual where gold was being gifted. A ritual where whatever was sought was given.

**pukku** – he entered that place in such a way that earth had to bend for each step that he took while reaching the place.

**irundu** – Just as [tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-9-kolvan-nan/) said “*koḷvan nān māvali mūvadi tā*” (Oh mahābali, give me three steps of land as measured with my foot; I will accept it), even if mahābali had refused, he would have remained there like a tree \[remaining stationary\], refusing to leave the place without getting what he had asked for.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 76. Mannai manam kolla

*mannai manangoḷḷa vanjittu nenjurukki ennudaiya pādattāl yānaḷappa mūvadimaṇ                                            109*

**Пословный перевод:**

pōr vēndar mannai – that māvali who is the chief of kings who have the strength to indulge in war; manam koḷḷa vanjittu – bewildering him to make him believe (that this bachelor has come only to seek alms); nenju urukki – softening his heart (through the beauty of his gait, speech etc); manna – Oh King!; ennudaiya pādattāl yān aḷappa – by measuring with my foot; mū adi maṇ taruga enṟu – saying ‘give me three steps of land’

**Перевод:**

**pōr vēndar mannai – the chief king among all the kings who are great warriors.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**manam koḷḷa vanjittu** – While people such as śukra bhagavān (guru \[preceptor\] for demons) tried to dissuade mahābali from granting anything, saying “this (vāmana) is no ordinary bachelor; he is the deceitful viṣṇu himself”, vāmana deceived mahābali through his beauty. Alternative interpretation – vāmana deceived mahābali by making him believe fully thinking “He has come only for seeking alms; he hasn’t come to deceive”.

**nenjurukki** – just as it is said in [tiruvāimoḻi 1-8-6](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/09/thiruvaimozhi-1-8-6-kalanthu-ennavi/) “*pulan koḷ māṇāy*” (in the guise of a dwarf and a bachelor at that, stealing the sensory perceptions), making mahābali feel that he cannot sustain himself unless he grants whatever vāmana asks for, by softening mahābali’s mind which was disturbed by śukra (his guru).

The following conversation \[as given in the *arumpadavurai* (explanatory note) by puttūr svāmy\] explains the way in which vāmana softened mahābali’s heart:

**ennudaiya pādattāḷ yān aḷappa . . . . .**

**manna** – vāmanā drew the attention of mahābali by calling him “Oh King!”

māvali stared at him from head to foot.

**mūvadi** – the child-like vāmana said “You must give me three steps”

māvali asked him “What should I give you?”

**maṇ** – the child told him “I want three steps of land”

māvali asked him “Through whose foot should I grant you three steps of land?”

**ennudaiya pādattāl** – the child told him “Through my foot”

māvali told him “Would you not take three steps of land as measured by my foot which is big?”

**yān aḷappa** – vāmana advised māvali “Is it not your duty to grant me whatever I desire?”

māvali kept quiet for sometime, with the desire “I should listen to his conversation for some more time”

**taruga** – the child spoke as if nectar were dripping from each word “give now itself”

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 77. Manna taruga enru

*mannā taruga enṟu vāy tiṟappa maṟṟavanum ennāl tarappattadenṟalumē attuṇaikkaṇ                                                            110 minnār maṇimudi pōy viṇ tadava mēledutta ponnār kanai kaḻaṟkāl ēḻulagum pōykkadandu                                              111*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāy tiṟappa – as he asked, opening his mouth; avanum – that mahābali (who heard that); ennāl tarappattadu enṟalum – as he said that “the three steps of land have been given by me”; attuṇaikkaṇ – at that very moment; min Ar mudi pōy viṇ tadava – the radiant crown went and stroked the ethereal layer; mēl edutta – that which was lifted high up; pon Ar kanai kaḻal kāl – the coveted divine foot, decorated with valorous anklet and making a sound; ēḻ ulagum pōy kadandu – going across all the worlds above

**Перевод:**

**taruga enṟu vāy tiṟappa – who will not soften when he (emperumān as vāmana) opens his radiant mouth?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**maṟṟavanum** – this implies that māvali, who was fully engaged in the beauty of vāmana opening his radiant mouth and in the sweetness of the speech, changed into a totally new person, just as gold is changed by being melted in a furnace \[and is purified fully\].

**ennāl tarappattadu enṟalumē** – just as śrī rāma said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-34 “*dattamasyābhayam mayā*” (refuge has been granted by me to vibhīśaṇa) and in 18-35 “*abhayam sarva bhūtēbhyō dadāmi*” (I grant refuge from all creations), māvali said “I have given already”. Since he had already taken a vow that he would grant whatever anyone asked for, he says that he has already given it.

**attuṇaikkaṇ** – vāmana started growing at that very moment and started measuring \[all the worlds\] since the one who had granted his wish, being a demon, could change his mind any time.

**minnār maṇi mudi pōy viṇ tadava** – the form of vāmana, who was a bachelor, changed into the crowned form of trivikrama and that radiant crown, full of gems, pervaded all the upper worlds.

**viṇ tadava** – since the capability of the protector is not limited by the capability of the protected, the foot pervaded even śrīvaikuṇṭam. ḍidn’t puruśa sūktam-1 say “*bhūmim viśvatō vrutvā atya tiśta daśāngulam*” (pervading entire earth, he measured to an unlimited extent)!

**mēl edutta . . . ēḻulagum pōy** – Just as [tiruvāimoḻi 7-4-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/08/thiruvaimozhi-7-4-1-azhi-ezha/) said “*mudi pādam eḻa*” the divine foot which was lifted, competed with the divine crown and went higher and higher, up.

**ponnār kanai kaḻal kāl** – the divine foot which was golden, desired by everyone, and decorated with valorous anklet which seemed to roar “there is no one to equal me in protecting the followers”

**ēḻulagum pōy kadandu** – the divine foot too went beyond the shell of the universe.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 78. Onna asurar

*onnā asurar tuḷangac celanītti mannivvagalidattai māvaliyai vanjittut                                   112 tan ulagam ākkuvitta tāḷānai – tāmarai mēl*

**Пословный перевод:**

angu onnā asurar tuḷanga – making the inimical demons (such as namuci et al) in those seven worlds to suffer; sela nītti – extending further (to go beyond); mannu i agal idattai – this permanent, expansive bhū maṇḍalam (region of world); māvaliyai vanjittu – deceiving mahābali (like this); than ulagam ākkuvitta tāḷānai – having divine feet, which made \[such worlds\] as his world

**Перевод:**

**angu onnā asurar tuḷangac cela nītti – when demons such as namuci (son of mahābali) came opposing him exclaiming as in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-8-8 “ennidu māyam” (what is this clairvoyance?)\[when emperumān, as vāmana, asked for three steps of land with his small foot and grew up instantaneously as trivikrama and measured all the worlds\], stretching his foot such that they ran in fear, just as it is mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi  1-8-8 “mannu namuciyai vānil suḻaṟṟiya” (spinning the persistent namuci around, in the sky) and in śrī viṣṇu darmam 43-28 “dravanti daityā:” (the demons are running).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mannivvagalidattai** – this world which was, for a long time, belonging to mahābali

**māvaliyai vanjittu** – carrying out the deceitful act of showing a small foot and measuring with a huge foot.

**tannulagam ākkuvitta** – since vāmana does not see any distinction between his followers and himself and since indra is not going to argue \[claiming ownership of the worlds\] the pāsuram says that *tannulagam* (his world) instead of *indranulagam*  (indra’s world). This is actually neither māvali’s *maṇ* (land) nor indra’s *maṇ*; isn’t it actually “*vāmanan maṇ*” (the land of vāmana) as mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 4-4-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/thiruvaimozhi-4-4-1-mannai-irundhu/)! Hence, she is saying that it is “*tannulagam*”

**tāḷānai** – these are all related to his divine foot. She says that she will destroy that too.

Until now, she was saying that she will destroy his fame which he attained during his incarnations. Now she goes to all the divine abodes where he has taken residence with the fame “he is at the limit of *saulabhya sauseelyādi* (easy to approach and being very simple etc) qualities” and says that she is going to tell all the world that “he is deceiving everyone saying that he has all those qualities; he doesn’t have these qualities”.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 79. Minnidaiyal nayaganai

*minnidaiyāḷ nāyaganai viṇṇagaruḷ ponmalaiyaip                         113 ponni maṇikoḻikkum pūngudandaip pōrvidaiyait tennan kuṟungudiyuḷ sembavaḷak kunṟinai                                   114 manniya taṇsēṟai vaḷḷalai māmalrmēl*

**Пословный перевод:**

tāmarai mēl min idaiyāḷ nāyaganai – the consort of pirātti who was born on a lotus and who has a waist similar to lightning; viṇṇagaruḷ ponmalaiyai – one who is shining like a golden mountain at tiruviṇṇagar; ponni maṇi koḻikkum pūngudandai pōrvidaiyai – one who is reclining like a bull which has got tired after waging a war, at tirukkudandai, where the river kāviri brings precious gems; then nan kuṟungudiyuḷ sembavaḷam kunṟinai – one who is shining like a reddish coral like mountain at tirukkuṟungudi which is a distinguished divine abode in the southern direction; taṇ sēṟai manniya vaḷḷalai – the supremely generous entity who has fittingly taken residence in the cool tiruccēṟai

**Перевод:**

**tāmarai mēl minnidaiyāḷ nāyaganai – the reason for taking incarnations like these \[seen in the previous verses\] is due to pirātti’s connection. The meaning implied is that āḻvār says that even connection with her is not beneficial for him \[since emperumān is not coming to āḻvār and she \[āḻvār in feminine mood\] has to engage in madal\]. Alternatively, this could mean that emperumān has the greatness because of his connection with pirātti.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**viṇṇagaruḷ ponmalaiyai** – \[the golden mountain at tiruviṇṇagar\] Since he is together with pirātti at tiruviṇṇagar, the golden hue from her is falling over him as a shadow, converting his form from that of a blue mountain to a golden mountain. Did not vēdam say in taittirīya upaniśat nārayaṇavalli “*neelatōyata madyastā vidyullēkā*” (pirātti is like a lightning amidst the bluish form of emperumān)?

**ponmaṇi koḻikkum pūngadandaip pōr vidaiyai** – kudandai is the town where river kāviri brings with her very expensive gems such as rubies, just as it is mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/08/thiruvaimozhi-5-8-9-isaiviththu-ennai/) “*tisaivil vīsum seḻumāmaṇigaḷ sērum tirukkudandai*” (tirukkudandai where very expensive gems which are effulgent in all directions, reach). In that beautiful town, he is reclining to get over the ennui of having fought with enemies during his incarnations.

**pōrvidaiyai** – (āḻvār, as parakāla nāyaki says) I will destroy the honour of one who is reclining with the fame of having annihilated his enemies.

**then nan kuṟungudi** – one which is in the southern direction. tirukkuṟungudi which has such a beauty about it that one cannot take his/her eyes off it. If the text is *tennan* kuṟungudi, it would mean tirukkuṟungudi which is in the pāṇḍya kingdom.

**sem pavaḷak kunṟinai** – one who has a beautiful form which has to be attained by engaging in madal.

**taṇ cēṟai manniya vaḷḷalai** – One who is most generous, offering himself to his followers who are living amidst the unfavourable samsāris, at tiruccēṟai, which is cool because of its proximity to the one who has the complexion of cloud.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 80. Annam tuyilum

*annam tuyilum aṇi nīr vayal āli                                                              115 ennudaiya innamudai evvuḷ perumalaiyaik kanni madiḷ sūḻ kaṇamangaik kaṟpagattai                                      116 minnai irusudarai veḷḷaṟaiyuḷ kallaṟai mēl*

**Пословный перевод:**

māmalar mēl annam tuyilum – swans sleeping on distinguished lotus flowers; aṇi nīr vayal Ali – tiruvāli, the divine abode, which has (agricultural) fields, full of water; ennudaiya innamudai – the supreme enjoyer, who is giving me darśan (for me to worship); evvuḷ perumalaiyai – one who is reclining at tiruvevvuḷ (present day tiruvaḷḷūr) as if a huge mountain were reclining; kanni madiḷ sūḻ kaṇamangai kaṟpagattai – one who is dwelling mercifully like a kalpaka vrukśam (wish-fulfilling divine tree) at tirukkaṇṇamangai which is surrounded by newly built compound wall; minnai – one who has periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who is resplendent like lightning; iru sudarai – divine disc and divine conch which appear like sūrya (sun) and candra (moon); veḷḷaṟaiyuḷ – at tiruveḷḷaṟai; kal aṟai mēl – inside the sannidi (sanctum sanctorum) made of stones

**Перевод:**

**mā malar mēl annam tuyilum – the rājahamsam (regal swan) and its female bird, desiring to engagein love-quarrel, choose a lotus flower and descend on it. The flower is also huge, capable of giving place for the love-quarrel. However, once they alight on the lotus, due to the softness of the bed (provided by the flower), they forget the quarrel and go to sleep. Since the flower provides comforts, which are given to regal members, such as softness, coolness, fragrance etc the swans sleep on the bed just like kings.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**aṇi nīr vayal Ali** – at tiruvāli which has abundance of water bodies.

**ennudaiya innamudai** – being the place of her (parakāla nāyaki’s) birth and the place that she entered after marriage, it became hers totally (when a land is cultivated on lease, the owner of the land gets a portion of the produce and the lessee gets the remaining portion; in this case, the entire benefit goes to one person, parakāla nāyaki).

**evvuḷ perumalaiyai** – one who is reclining in the divine abode of tiruevvuḷ like a mountain where no one can enjoy him, being satisfied fully.

**kanni madiḷ sūḻ kaṇamangai kaṟpagattai** – one who gave himself to me, at tirukkaṇṇamangai, which is surrounded by compound wall which appears to be new always. The commentator says “*enakkē tannaittanda kaṟpagattai*” [tiruvāimoḻi 2-7-11](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/05/thiruvaimozhi-2-7-11-parpanaban/) (he is the kalpaka vrukśa who gave himself to me). Has this āḻvār not said in periya tirumoḻi 7-10-10 “*kaṇgaḷ āraḷavum ninṟu kaṇṇamangaiyuḷ kaṇḍukoṇḍēn*” (I saw emperumān at tirukkaṇṇamangai for such a length of time that my eyes became fully satiated)!

**madiḷ sūḻ** – since he is a *mangaḷāśāsanaparar* (one who is engaged in praising emperumān) he is feeling satisfied that even when he is engaging in madal, emperumān is well protected.

How does emperumān stand there?  
**minnai iru sudarai** – he is like lightning and like sun and moon. This refers to emnperumān standing there with pirātti and with divine disc and divine conch.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 81. Pannai maradagattai

*ponnai maradagattaip putkuḻi em pōrēṟṟai                                           117 mannum arangattem māmaṇiyai vallavāḻ*

**Пословный перевод:**

ponnai – shining like gold; maradagattai – having a greenish form matching emerald; putkuḻi em pōr ēṟṟai – dwelling in \[the divine abode of\] tirupputkuḻi, as my lord and as a bull ready to wage a war; arangattu mannum – residing permanently at tiruvarangam; em māmaṇiyai – one who we can handle, like a blue gem; vallavāḻ – one who has taken residence at tiruvallavāḻ

**Перевод:**

**veḷḷaṟaiyuḷ kallaṟai mēl ponnai – one who is dwelling inside the sanctum sanctorum, made of stones, at tiruveḷḷaṟai, apt to be desired.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kallaṟai** – aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār, one of the commentators for this  prabandam, calls it as a room made of carbuncles.

**ponnai** – Similar to those who string an ornament of gems using gold and precious stones, āḻvār  is stringing the divine abodes of emperumān.

**maradagattai** – one who has a divine form similar to a huge mountain of greenish gem which will be soothing for those who see.

**putkuḻi em pōrēṟṟai** – one who is dwelling in tirupputkuḻi, a divine abode near kāncīpuram, like a bull ready to wage a war such that enemies would not even venture anywhere near.

**em pōrēṟṟai** – this would also mean that he is one who defeated him (āḻvār).

**mannum arangattu** – tiruvarangam, the divine abode, where followers such as toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār would forget all the other divine abodes and dwell only here. Is this divine abode not spoken of as in tiruccanda viruttam 52 “*aṟṟapaṟṟar suṟṟi vāḻum andaṇīr arangam*” () and as in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-5 “*nallārgaḷ vāḻum naḷiraranga nāgaṇaiyān*” (periya perumāḷ who is having the divine mattress of ādiśēśan at tiruvarangam where great people are living)?

**mannum arangattu** – Alternatively, this is the divine abode he chose to recline permanently, foregoing śrīvaikuṇṭam, tiruppāṟkadal and other divine abodes.

**em mā maṇiyai** – Since this gem (emperumān) is most invaluable and since it can be rolled into the corner of one’s dress, being that simple, he is much similar to a great bluish gem in terms of its colour and radiance.

**arangattu emmāmaṇiyai** – Even if he is the lord of the entire world, that he is my lord too, would equal his other lordship.

**mā maṇiyai** – a huge gemstone; it could also be considered that he is a blackish gem. toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār called him in [tirumalai 21](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/12/thiruvaimozhi-nurrandhadhi-21/) as “*kōyil maṇiyanār*” (one who is like a bluish gem in the temple); kulasēkarapperumāḷ referred to him in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 1-1 as “*teṇṇīrpponni tiraikkaiyāl adi varudap paḷḷikoḷḷum karumaṇi*” (the bluish gemstone whose divine feet are gently massaged by the waves in the river kāviri, whose water is very clear); this āḻvār himself called him in periya tirumoḻi 11-8-8 as “*aṇiyār poḻil sūḻ aranga nagarappā – maṇiyē maṇimāṇikkamē*” (Oh my father who is dwelling in tiruvarangam which is surrounded by beautiful orchards!  Oh one who has the greatness of a gem such as ruby!).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 82. Pinnaimanalanai

*pinnai maṇāḷanaip pēril piṟappiliyai                                                      118 tonnīrk kadal kidanda tōḷā maṇiccudarai en manattu mālai idavendai īsanai                                                 119*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnai maṇāḷanai – being the consort of nappinnai pirātti (nīḷā dēvi); pēril piṟappu iliyai – dwelling at tiruppērnagar, being ready forever \[to protect his followers\]; tol nīr kadal kidanda – one who reclined on the ocean during the time of great deluge; tōḷā maṇi sudarai – being the radiance of gem which has not been pierced; en manattu mālai – one who has deep love for me and who never leaves my mind; idavendai īsanai – supreme being who has taken residence at tiruvidavendai

**Перевод:**

**vallavāḻ pinnai maṇāḷanai – one who is standing \[taking residence\] at tiruvallavāḻ, manifesting the beauty after marrying nappinnaip pirātti.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pēril piṟappiliyai** – one who is dwelling at tiruppēr nagar, with the desire to take thousands of incarnations, even though he has taken many incarnations earlier for the sake of his followers, considering them as not being  purposeful.

**piṟappili** – this shows that the supreme being considers in his divine mind that in the incarnations that he had undertaken earlier, he has not done anything.

**tol nīr kidanda tōḷā maṇiccudarai** – even though he is constantly with nityasūris in paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam), the supreme being thinks that he is alone and feels sorrowful, as mentioned in the sayin g “*sa ēkākī na ramēta*” (that supreme being does not enjoy being alone); thus, he creates cētanas (those with knowledge) and in order to uplift them, takes the first incarnation in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean), shining effulgently, like the radiance of a gemstone which had not been pierced.

**tol nīr** – this is the seedling for the crops of vibhava and arcāvatāras (incarnations in tiruppāṟkadal and various divine abodes).

**tōḷā maniccudarai en manattu mālai** – this would also mean one who has deep affection for me and took residence in my heart, as a result of which he became splendorous. For sarvēśvara (the supreme entity), places such as tiruppāṟkadal are meant for carrying out certain tasks \[for the sake of his followers\] and are like bālālaya \[temporary place where emperumān is located when the sanctum sanctorum is under maintenance/repair\] whereas the hearts of followers are the desired places to attain. taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli says “*viśvasyāyatanam mahat*” (for sarvēśvaran, heart is the huge temple); nammāḻvār says in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-2-nenjame/) “*nenjamē nīṇagarāga irunda en tanjanē*” (Oh my companion, who considers my heart as a huge city!); there are also others who had prayed as in [iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 54](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/11/irandam-thiruvandhadhi-54/) “*iḷam kōyil kaividēl*” (just because you have taken residence in my heart, please do not give up your residence in places such as tiruppāṟkadal).

āḻvār cites an example for the last mentioned . . . .

**idavendai īsanai** – one who is shining at tiruvidavendai since he is with his beloved, *bhūmippirātti* (bhūdēvi). Since he is the consort of bhūmippirātti, he is dwelling there manifesting that he is the supreme being.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 83. Mannum kadanmallai

*mannum kadanmallai māyavanai vānavartam senni maṇiccudarait taṇkāḷ tiṟalvaliyait\`                                            120 tannaip piṟar aṟiyāt tattuvattai muttinai annattai mīnai ariyai arumaṟaiyai                                                                  121*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadal mallai mannum māyavanai – the amazing entity who has taken permanent residence at tirukkadanmallai (present day mahābalipuram); vānavar tam senni maṇi sudarai – one who is radiant as the prime jewel for nityasūris; taṇkāl tiṟalvaliyai – the hugely powerful entity who has taken residence at tiruttaṇkāl; tannai piṟar aṟiyā tattuvattai – one whose nature cannot be known by others (who are not under the shadow of his divine mercy); muttinai – one who is like a pearl; annattai – one who incarnated as a swan; mīnai – one who incarnated as a fish; ariyai – one who incarnated as hayagrīva (horse face, human body); arumaṟaiyai – one who has the vēdas (sacred texts) as his form

**Перевод:**

**mannum kadal mallai māyavanai – the amazing entity who is shining well at tirukkadal mallai since it is an abode which is very dear to his follower. emperumān is reclining there on the floor, leaving aside the inseparable anantāḻvān (ādiśēśan).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

Who is the entity who is lying on the floor like this, as if he is without anyone?

**vānavar tam senni maṇiccudarai** – he is the head of nityasūris who are tireless. He shines on their head as an ornament. Was he not referred to as “*amarar sennippū*” (the flower on the heads of nityasūris)?

**taṇkāl tiṟalvaliyai** – one who is shining with valour and strength which will enable him to win over everyone, at the divine abode tiruttaṇkāl by shining brightly as the epitome of valour and strength.

**tannaip piṟar aṟiyāt tattuvattai** – the supreme being who cannot be known by anyone unless he manifests himself to such a person. vēdas said of him “*yamēvaiśa vruṇutē tēna labhya*:” (this supreme being can be attained only by one who he (the supreme being) has thought of in his mind). He is such that when he makes himself easily approachable and remains as a simple entity, people such as śiśupāla et al, without knowing his greatness, speak of him in low terms which will singe the ears of those who hear them, just as emperumān himself said in [bhagavat gītā 9-11](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/9-11/) “*param bhāvamajānanta*:  *avajānanti mām mūdā*:” (fools, not knowing my greatness, despise me).

**muttinai** – one who has a cool, comfortable divine form such that his followers can don him \[as an ornament\].

**annattai mīnai ariyai** – when brahmā lost vēdas to the demons *madu* and *kaitaba*, emperumān incarnated as *hayagrīva* (horse face and human body), killed them, incarnated as fish, seized the vēdas, and incarnated as swan and instructed the vēdas \[to brahmā\]. *ari* – horse.

What is the reason for taking such efforts for vēdas?

**arumaṟaiyai** – he himself is the vēdas. Just as he mercifully said in [bhagavat gītā 15-15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-15/) “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya*:” (only I am known through all the vēdas).

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 84. Munnivvulagunda

*munnivvulaguṇḍa mūrttiyaik kōvalūr mannum idai kaḻi em māyanaip pēyalaṟap                                     122 pinnum mulaiyuṇḍa piḷḷaiyai aḷḷal vāy annam irai tēraḻundūr eḻum sudarai                                          123*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun i ulagu uṇḍa mūrttiyai – in an earlier time, as the lord, keeping all these worlds in his divine stomach and looking after them; kōvalūr – tirukkōvalūr; idai kaḻi mannum em māyavanai – as our tirumāl (emperumān) who has taken permanent residence in the corridor \[of mrigaṇḍu maharishi’s hermitage\] at tirukkōvalūr; pēy alaṟa mulai uṇḍa piḷḷaiyai – as an infant who ate \[from\] the bosom of the demon pūtana such that she cried out in pain; aḷḷal vāy – in marshy places; irai tēr aḻundūr – at tiruvaḻundūr, to seek prey; eḻum sudarai – as an effulgent lamp

**Перевод:**

**mun ivvulaguṇḍa mūrttiyai – to ensure that the people of this world do not bring more harm their way, emperumān annihilated them and protected them by keeping them in his divine stomach, due to which his divine form became splendorous.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**mun** – this implies that his quality of protecting the people is always available, from time immemorial.

**mun** – he protected them before they suffered during deluge.

**ulagu** – he protected all the entities which or who were existing at that point of time.

**kōvalūr mannum idai kaḻi em māyavanai** – since tirukkōvalūr is the place where emperumān jostled with the first three āḻvārs and became extremely blissful, he took permanent residence there, reminiscing that incident. Such an amazing entity, he is.

**pēy alaṟap pinnum mulai uṇḍa piḷḷaiyai** – yaśōdā would always praise him whenever she nurses him. When pūtanā, the demon who was commanded by kamsa to kill infant kriṣṇa, smeared poison on her bosom and came to nurse kriṣṇa, she screamed aloud in pain as he bit her bosom. Taking that (screaming noise made by pūtanā) as a song of praise on him, he drank her milk. He had such childishness in him.

**aḷḷal vāy annam irai tēr aḻundūr** – the swans would normally detest entering muddy places; but these swans entered such marshy places in tēraḻundūr, in search of prey.

**aḻundūr eḻum sudarai** – he is the supreme effulgence who does not let go of his followers, who showered affection on me who is very lowly and has nothing good in him. Alternative meaning: the sun which rises from tēraḻundūr.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 85. Ten tillai

*ten tillaic cittira kūdattu en selvanai minni maḻai tavaḻum vēngadattem vittaganai                                   124 mannai mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanai konnavilum āḻip padiyānaik kōttiyūr                                                                 125*

**Пословный перевод:**

then tillaic cittirakūdattu en selvanai – the wealthy entity (who has taken residence) at tillai cittira kūtam, which is in the southern direction; maḻai – clouds; minni – shining brightly; tavaḻum – coming to, gently; tiruvēngadam – at tiruvēngadamalai (one who has taken residence); em vittaganai – one who has amazing qualities and activities, for us; mannanai – as the supreme lord; mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanai – as the bridegroom who has taken residence at tirumālirunjōlai; kol navilum āḻi padaiyānai – one who has as his weapon, the divine disc which is capable of annihilating enemies; kōttiyūr – at tirukkōttiyūr

**Перевод:**

**then tillai cittirakūdattu en selvanai – one who is reclining at the beautiful tillai tirucittirakūdam as a very wealthy entity. Alternatively, one who has the wealth of beauty.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**minni maḻai tavaḻum vēngadattu em vittaganai** – the cloud, which is an *acētana* (one without knowledge), crawls through the key hole at tirumalai after the temple has been closed, to carry out *tiruvārādanam* (vorśipping emperumān as per the procedure laid down in śāstras), carrying lightning with it to provide light. He is such an amazing entity that the cloud does all these. Alternatively, just as it is mentioned in various pāsurams (from the collection of 4000 divya prabandam), such as nācciyār tirumoḻi 8-4 “*vēngadattut tannāgat tirumangai tangiya sīr mārvaṟku*” (to emperumān in whose divine chest, śrī mahālakśmi has taken residence); [tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/07/thiruvaimozhi-6-10-10-agalagillen-part-5/) “*alarmēlmangai uṟai mārvā – tiruvēngadattānē*” (Oh one who is dwelling at tiruvēngadam and in whose divine chest pirātti is dwelling); periya tirumoḻi 6-10-6 “*minnotta nuṇmarungul melliyalait tirumārvil mannattān vaittugandān*” (emperumān who is very joyous after fittingly carrying śrī mahālakśmi who has a waist similar to lightning and who has a very soft nature, on his divine chest), it could be considered that āḻvār is referring to emperumān’s dark, divine form in which he is carrying the golden hued pirātti as *minni maḻai tavaḻum*. Did not the ancient vēdas refer to him as in nārāyaṇa sūktam “*neelatōyata madyastā vidyullēka . .* ” (emperumān has a dark form across which mahālakśmi resides as a ray of lightning) and in tamiḻ vēdam (tiruvāimoḻi) as “*neelamuṇḍa minnanna mēni* ” (divine form of emperumān with a streak of lightning)!

**mannanai mālirunjōlai maṇāḷanai** – the person who held my hand \[as in marriage\] is the king for the whole land! The implied meaning here is that he has given up on me just because he is the king of the land.

**konnavalum āḻippadaiyānai** – one who has the deadly weapon of divine disc which is feared much because of its restiveness in killing enemies such as *namuci* (son of mahābali) et al.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 86. Anna uruvil ariyai

*anna uruvil ariyait tirumeyyattu innamuda veḷḷattai indaḷūr andaṇanai                                 126 mannu madiḷ kacci vēḷukkai āḷariyai manniya pādagattu em maindanai – vehkāvil                             127*

**Пословный перевод:**

anna uruvil ariyai – as narasimhamūrtty (emperumān’s divine form with lion face and human body) who has such (distinguished) divine form; tiru meyyattu – in tirumeyyam; in amudam veḷḷattai – being greatly enjoyable as a sweet ocean of nectar; indaḷūr – at tiruvindaḷūr; andaṇanai – being supremely merciful; kacci – in the town of kāncīpuram; vēḷukkai āḷariyai – as narasimha in the divine abode of tiruvēḷukkai; pādagattu manniya em maindanai – as our youthful entity at tiruppādagam where he has taken permanent residence; vehkāvil – at tiruvehkā

**Перевод:**

**kōttiyūr anna uruvil ariyai – one who has taken residence at tirukkōttiyūr with the beautiful form of narasimha which cannot be described by words, other than saying “He is like that”.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kōttiyūr ariyai** – the place got the name of *kōttiyūr* since the *gōśti* (group of people) gathered at that place to discuss the means to eliminate the demon iraṇiyan (hiraṇya kaśyap). periyāḻvār too celebrated emperumān there in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-4-6 “*tirukkōttiyūr nādanai narasinganai*” (narasimha, the lord of tirukkōttiyūr).

**ariyai** – this refers to the wondrous narasimha form with which emperumān has taken residence here, going by the divine names of *teṟkāḻvār* and *kōḷariyāḻvār*.

**anna uruvil ariyai** – even this āḻvār (tirumangai āḻvār) who sang the wondrous verse while composing a hymn in order to praise śrī rāmapirān, as in periya tirumoḻi 8-5-6 “*muriyum veṇḍhirai mudugayam tīppada muḻangaḻal eriyambin varikoḷ venjilai vaḷaivitta maindanum*” (the great warrior śrī rāma who drew his beautiful bow which appeared cruel \[at that time\] such that the fiery arrows which were making dreadful sounds from it, would burn down the ocean which was in a state of agitation with its whitish waves ) could not compose a verse on tirukkōttiyūr emperumān and merely said “appadippatta uru” (a form similar to that).

**tirumeyyattu innamuda veḷḷattai** – one who is like a sweet nectarean ocean who removes the craving of those who are going on their way to tirukkōttiyūr.

**innamudam** – if nectar \[from ocean\] were consumed, it will satiate one’s thirst. But this nectar, even when merely thought of, will sweeten one’s heart and satisfy the person’s craving.

**indaḷūr andaṇanai** – one who is merciful and has taken residence at tiruvindaḷūr. *andaṇan* – one who has a beautiful quality (of being merciful). tirukkuṟaḷ (a tamiḻ literary work composed around 2000 years ago) says “*andaṇar enbar aṟavōr maṟṟevvuyirkkum sendaṇmai pūṇḍu ozugalāl*” (the term *andaṇar* refers to one who lives by the path of righteousness and who is merciful towards all the other creations). The term *andaṇan* also refers to being sacred.

**mannum madiḷ kacci vēḷukkai āḷariyai** – the term *vēḷirukkai* transformed to *vēḷukkai*. *vēḷ* – one who is so beautiful that he is like a cupid for all the cupids. *āḷari* – *āḷ ari* – narasingam (form with lion face and human body). *narasimha* is vorśipped with the verse *ugram vīram mahāviśnum* (mahāviṣṇu who is valorous and who is very angry). But, hiding that valour, emperumān manifests his beauty \[at the divine abode tiruvēḷukkai\] which makes his followers to be fearful as to what will happen to him and hence they built a high fort around the divine abode. emperumān has taken residence in such a divine abode.

**mannum madi** – the commentator nāyanār says that the fort is such that it would not get destroyed even during deluge

**manniya pādagattu em maindanai** – one who has taken permanent residence at tiruppādagam, manifesting his form with which he carried out the activity of a messenger for pāṇḍavas.

**manniya maindanai** – also refers to one one who is permanently youthful

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 87. Unniya yogattu

*unniya yōgattuṟakkattai ūragattuḷ annavanai attabuyagarattu emmānēṟṟai                                     128 ennai manam kavarnda īsanai vānavar tam munnavanai mūḻikkaḷattu viḷakkinai                                          129*

**Пословный перевод:**

unniya yōgattu uṟakkattai – one who is sleeping in yōga \[body and mind joined together\] while being fully aware; ūragattuḷ annavanai – being very distinguished in tiruvūragam \[a divine abode in kāncīpuram\]; atta buya karattu emmān ēṟṟai – being our lord at \[the divine abode\] attabuyakaram; ennai manam kavarnda īsanai – the entity complete \[in all aspects\] who stole my heart; vānavar tam munnavanai – being the leader of celestial entities; mūḻikkaḷattu viḷakkinai – one who is shining at tirumūḻikkaḷam \[a divine abode in kēraḷa\]

**Перевод:**

**vehkāvil unniya yōgattuṟakkattai – one who is asleep and awake at tiruvehkā – in other words when tirumaḻisaippirān told him “painnāgappāy suruttikkoḷ ”(roll your mattress of ādiśēśan), he rolled up his mattress and when the same āḻvār later told him “painnāgappāy virittukkoḷ” (spread out your mattress of ādiśēśan) he spread out his mattress. The implied meaning is that he behaves as desired by his devotees.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**unniya yōgattu uṟakkattai** – Alternative interpretation is that he is one who is reclining  \[being awake and asleep\], thinking about the protection of his devotees.

**ūragattuḷ annavanai** – taking the form of one who is measuring the worlds, he has taken residence at tiruvūragam with a huge form which even āḻvār, with his ability to envision events which would take place later, could not see (him) fully. He could not be seen completely and hence āḻvār could only say “like him”.

**attabuyakarattu emmān ēṟṟai** – one who came when his follower gajēndra āḻvān called out to him in distress, to protect him, transforming himself with his dress in disarray and hair diśevelled, manifesting the form with which he showered mercy on gajēndran, and standing with his esteem in full display.

**ennai manam kavarnda īsanai** – one who stole every belonging of mine and who thinks that he has attained completeness because of that.

**vānavar tam munnavanai** – one who cannot be enjoyed fully even by nityasūris who do not have any shortcoming in their knowledge. When vēdam (sacred text) started expounding his qualilties at every stage, just as it is mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli “*śrōtriyasya cākā mahatasya*” while describing his quality of bliss, it compared gradually with the bliss of nityas and muktas, taking it all the way to the bliss of supreme entity and roared very clearly “*yatō vācō nivartantē*” (from which supreme being, the words return), saying that it cannot describe the bliss of supreme being. puruśa sūktam too said “*pūrvō yō dēvēbhya*:” (one who is earlier than nityasūris).

**mūḻikkaḷattu viḷakkinai** – one who is more resplendent than ever earlier, after incarnating at tirumūḻikkaḷam. vēdam said “*śrēyān bhavati jāyamāna*:” (one who attains greatness after incarnating). tiruvāimoḻi, the tamiḻ vēdam, said in [1-3-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/06/thiruvaimozhi-1-3-2-elivarum-iyalvinan/) “*palapiṟappāy oḷivarum muḻu nalam*” (as he keeps taking incarnation after incarnation his radiance keeps increasing as do his auspicious qualities). If the text were *mūḻikkaḷattu vaḷattinai*, the meaning would be that he is the opulence of tirumūḻikkaḷam.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 88. Annavanai adanur

*annavanai ādanaūr āṇḍaḷakkum aiyanai nennalai inṟinai nāḷaiyai nīrmalai mēl                                                                   130 mannum maṟai nāngum ānānaip pullāṇit tennan tamiḻai vadamoḻiyai nāngūril                                                            131*

**Пословный перевод:**

annavanai – one who cannot be defined that he is like this; ādanūr – at tiruvādanūr; āṇḍu aḷakkum aiyanai – one who controls passing of time; nennalai inṟinai nāḷaiyai – one who is the controller of yesterday, today and tomorrow; nīrmalai mēl mannum – one who has taken residence at tirunīrmalai; maṟai nāngum ānānai – having the form of four vēdas; pullāṇi – one who has taken residence at tiruppullāṇi; tennan tamiḻai vadamoḻiyai – one who is described by both tamiḻ and samaskruta languages; nāngūr – at tirunāngūr

**Перевод:**

**annavanai ādanūr āṇḍaḷakkum aiyanai – one whose beauty cannot be described in words and who is the lord for determining kālam (time) which is measured in terms of years.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**āṇḍu aḷakkum** – time will bring to order all the entities which are *prākrutam* (natural; perishable) while emperumān brings to order such time itself. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-20-54 says “*kālam sa pacatē*” (he cooks time; he controls time).

āḻvār states next as to how he controls time.

**nennalai inṟinai nāḷaiyai** – he controls time by dividing into parts, viz.  yesterday, today and tomorrow.nammāḻvār says in his [tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-5](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/07/thiruvaimozhi-3-1-5-varundhadha/) “*varungālam nigaḻgālam kaḻigālamāy ulagai orungāga aḷippāy*” (Oh one who protects the world in a focussed way by controlling future, present and past times!) and in [tiruvāimoḻi 3-8-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2016/10/thiruvaimozhi-3-8-9-kolvan-nan/) “*senṟu sellādana munnilām kālamē*” (Oh one who is the controller of past, future and present times!).

**nīrmalai mēl mannum maṟai nāngum ānānai** – one who has taken residence atop tirunīrmalai, and who is spoken of by the everlasting, faultless vēdas. *kaṇṇan* (kriṣṇa) says in [bhagavat gītā 15-15](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/15-15/) “*vēdaiśca sarvairahamēva vēdya*:” (I am the only one who is known through all the vēdas).

**pullāṇit tennan tamiḻai** – one who is sung about by tamiḻ vēdam in tiruppullāṇi, which is in the kingdom of pāṇḍiyas, as in periya tirumoḻi 9-4-1 “*kāvār madalpeṇṇai*” (palm trees with their palm leaves seen densely inside the orchards) and as in periya tirumoḻi 9-3-1 “*tannai naivikkilēn*” (I am unable to put an end to myself).

**tennan tamiḻai** – alternative meaning: one who is sung through agattiyar’s tamiḻ \[agattiyar (agastya) is the patron sage for tamiḻ language\].

**vadamoḻiyai** – one who is spoken of by the śāstras in languages of the north \[of vindiya mountains\] such as śrī rāmāyaṇa etc. īsn’t śrī rāma residing at tiruppullāṇi!

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.
