## 89. Mannum manimada

*mannu maṇimādak kōyil maṇāḷanai nannīrt talaicanga nāṇmadiyai nān vaṇangum                                    132 kaṇṇanaik kaṇṇapurttānait tennaṟaiyūr mannu maṇi mādak kōyil maṇāḷanai                                                            133*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇimādak kōyil mannu maṇāḷanai – standing forever at maṇimādakkōyil (divine abode in tanjāvūr) as a bridegroom; nal nīr talaiccanga nāṇmadiyai – as the nāṇmadiyapperumāḷ at talaiccangādu which is surrounded by good water; nān vaṇangum kaṇṇanai – as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) who I worship; kaṇṇapurattānai – one who is dwelling at tirukkaṇṇapuram; then naṟaiyūr maṇi mādak kōyil mannu maṇāḷanai – one who has taken residence as a bridegroom in the famous tiruraṟaiyūr maṇi mādak kōyil

**Перевод:**

**nāngūril mannu maṇimādakkōyil maṇāḷanai – the bridegroom who has taken residence permanently at tirunāngūr maṇimādakkōyil, making me as a servitor such that there is no need for any further support for me**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**nannīrt talaiccanga nāṇmadiyai** – one who shines beautifully at talaiccangādu, which has abundant water bodies, just like the moon rising from the ocean on a full moon day,

**nān vaṇangum kaṇṇanaik kaṇṇapurattānai** – sovrirāja emperumān, who is also referred to as kaṇṇan, who has taken residence at tirukkaṇṇapuram and who made me to exist exclusively for him just as it is said in periya tirumoḻi 8-9-3 “*kaṇṇapuram onṟu udaiyānukku adiyēn oruvarkku uriyēnō*” (will I belong to anyone other than the emperumān who has as his divine abode, the distinguished tirukkaṇṇapuram and for whom I am a servitor?)

**tennaṟaiyūr mannu maṇi mādak kōyil maṇāḷanai** – the bridegroom who made me to engage with madal at the temple which is like a mansion made of gemstones and which is shining in the beautiful tirunaṟaiyūr.

**maṇāḷanai** – this also implies that he is the bridegroom at the place which is also referred to as *nācciyār kōyil* (temple of emperumān’s consort) where it is the pirātti’s writ that runs and not his.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 90. Konnavil tol

*kannavil tōḷ kāḷaiyaik kaṇḍāngu kai toḻudu ennilaimai ellām aṟivittāl emperumān                                                                             134*

**Пословный перевод:**

kal navil tōḷ kāḷaiyai – one who is a youth with his shoulders similar to a mountain; āngu kaṇḍu kai toḻudu – seeing him at that tirunaṟaiyūr and vorśipping him; en nilaimai ellām aṟivittāl – if I appeal to him, stating my dispositions; emperumān – that perumān \[supreme entity\]

**Перевод:**

**After mentioning that divine abode \[tinaṟaiyūr\] which made him engage with madal, āḻvār, without speaking about any other divine abode, makes known his firmness \[of mind\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kannaviḷ tōḷ kālaiyai** – one who is celebrating the strength of his shoulders, which are like a rock, having won over a weak woman.

**kālaiyai** – he is proud of his youthful state which made her engage with madal.

**kaṇḍu** – just as āḻvār had started \[earlier\] “*en kaṇ kaḷippa nōkkinēn* ” (I saw \[emperumān\] such that my eyes rejoiced), he says that he will not rest until he saw fully.

**āngu kai toḻudu** – vorśipping him in his divine presence in order to attain him. This is the first step for destroying his *svarūpam* (basic nature) and engaging in madal. Performing *anjali* \[saluting with cupped palms\] to emperumān with the interntion of attaining him through one’s own means is equivalent to the queen of an emperor going in for alms.This āḻvār who said in periya tirumoḻi 9-3-9 “*ōdi nāmam kuḷittu ucci tannāl oḷimāmalarppādam nāḷum paṇivōm*” (after taking a bath and reciting his divine names, we will worship his effulgent, divine, lotus-like feet, every day) next said “*namakkē nalamādalil*” (it will be our ultimate benefit). \[Here, the end syllable of the first word is lengthened (namak***kē***) since the person who has surrendered is not expected to do anything on his/her own as a means to attaining emperumān. However, āḻvār had waited for quite a long time for emperumān to show himself up to him. Since he did not come, āḻvār decided to engage with activities which could be construed as *anyōpāyam* (engaging with other means). emperumān will not tolerate this\]. nammāḻvār, in  his [tiruvāimoḻi 9-3-9](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/07/thiruvaimozhi-9-3-9-thozhudhu-mamalar/), says “*toḻudu māmalar nīrsudar dūpam koṇḍu eḻudum ennumidu migai ādalin* ” (if we worship you with distinguished flowers, water, lamp and smoke, and fall at your divine feet, it would appear to your divine mind that this is excessive \[since we are not expected to engage with any means other than your divine feet to attain you\]).

**kaṇḍu āngu kai toḻudu** – nammāḻvār who had dared to engage with madal, had said in [tiruvāimoḻi  5-3-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/06/thiruvaimozhi-5-3-7-valaiyul-agappaduththu/) “*nam kaṇgaḷāl kaṇḍu talaiyil vaṇangavum āngolō taiyalār munbē*” (will it be possible for us to see him and worship his divine feet with our head?) sītāppirātti, being angry with śrī rāma,  said \[to hanumān\] as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-55 “*tam mamārttē sukham prucca śirasā cābhi vādaya* ” (convey my enquiries to him on my behalf; worship (on my behalf) his divine feet).

**en nilaimai ellāl aṟivittāl** – If I express all my conditions until the time of engaging with madal. One of the commentators for this prabandam, aḻagiya maṇavāḷap perumāḷ nāyanār has given a number of quotes from periya tirumoḻi under which \[tirumangai\] āḻvār has stated his condition to emperumān.

- periya tirumoḻi 1-9-1”*unnaik kāṇbadōr āsaiyināl vandadaindēn*” to show how he desired to see emperumān
- periya tirumoḻi 2-7-1 “*āgilum āsai vidāḷāl*”, to show how that desire continued
- periya tirumoḻi 3-5-1 “*en sindanaikku iniyāy*” and 3-5-6 “*pugundāyaip pōgalottēn*”, to show how emperumān tied him down completely
- periya tirumoḻi 4-8-3 “*āyar mādar kongai pulgu seṇḍan*” and 4-8-4 “*kollaiyānāḷ parisaḻittā*” to show how she \[āḻvār in feminine mood\] crossed limits and lost the esteem of womanhood for his sake
- periya tirumoḻi 4-9-3 “*ummaik kāṇum āsai ennum kadalil vīḻndu*” to show how he fell into the ocean of desire even as others were castigating him
- periya tirumoḻi 5-5-1 “*vāy veruvi vēngadamāē vēāngadamē*”, 5-5-2 “*adiyēnai vēṇḍudiyō vēṇḍāyō*”, 5-5-6 “*tādādu vanamālai tārānō*” to show how (s)he was babbing to get his mercy
- periya tirumoḻi 7-3-1 “*sinavil sengaṇ*” to show how he was seeing emperumān in his dream
- periya tirumoḻi 7-5-9 “*ennaimbulanum eḻilum koṇḍu ponnangalaigaḷ meliveyda*” to show how emperumān left him in distress after taking away the sensory perceptions and beauty \[of parakāla nāyaki\]
- periya tirumoḻi 8-2 “*teḷḷiyīr dēvar*” to show how he wizened after speaking about tirukkaṇṇapuram and melting inside
- periya tirumoḻi 8-3 “*karaiyedutta*” to show how he lost his bangles \[in feminine mood\]
- periya tirumoḻi 8-5 “*tandai kālil*” to show how the time passed on with his heart desiring emperumān’s garland
- periya tirumoḻi 9-4 “*kāvār madal*” to show how he \[in the mood of parakāla nāyaki\] suffered mentally after hearing tiruppullāṇi emperumān speaking lies to him and how he lost his sleep, losing his complexion, bangles and the dress not fitting the body \[since it became so loose\]
- periya tirumoḻi 9-5 “*tavaḷa iḷampiṟai* ” to show how he lost everything
- periya tirumoḻi 9-9 “*mūvaril mun mudalvan*” to show how he desired to unite with tirumālirunjōlai emperumān but couldn’t
- periya tirumoḻi 10-10 “*tiruttāy sembōttu*” to show how he was looking for an auspicious time and

how he is now engaging in madal, thus revealing all his states to emperumān.

**emperumān** – the relationship between emperumān and me is such that I have to attain him, even if it means that I have to destroy my basic nature. He is asking whether he could wait for emperumān to come to him.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 91. Tan arulum agamum

*tan aruḷum āgamum tārānēl tannai nān*

**Пословный перевод:**

than aruḷum āgamum tārānēl – if he does not grant me his divine grace and his divine chest; tannai – that emperumān; nān – I (who know all his activities)

**Перевод:**

**than aruḷum āgamum tārānēl – if he does not shower his mercy on me and if he does not embrace my bosom with his divine chest and give me fresh lease of life, just as people build sheds during summer to donate water to the needy ones which is an activity intended to do good for themselves instead of to the ones who take water from these sheds. The implied meaning is that if emperumān does not do this \[showering his mercy and embracing āḻvār\], āḻvār’s life will not remain in his body.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tannai**  – emperumān who is acting as if he is very loving while in truth he does not have any love for āḻvār, just like puffed rice has no sweetness in it even though it is coated with a layer of jaggery. Alternative interpretation – emperumān who has such greatness that he has to be attained even if one were to engage with madal.

**nān** – I, who am going to disclose all the lies about him who is acting as if he were very true. Alternatively, this would also mean – I, who will not remain without attaining him, even if it means that I have to destroy both his and my nature.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 92. Minnidaiyar seriyilum

*minnidaiyār sēriyilum vēdiyargaḷ vāḻvidattum                                       135 tannadiyār munbum taraṇi muḻudāḷum konnavilum vēlvēndar kūttattum nāttagattum                                     136*

**Пословный перевод:**

min indaiyār sēriyilum – in the gatherings of women; vēdiyargaḷ vāḻvidattum – in the places where vaidikas (those who follow vēdams) live; than adiyār munbum – in the presence of emperumān’s followers; taraṇi muḻudāḷum konnavilum vēlvēndar kūttattum – in the courts of those kings who rule over the entire eartern region and who have cruel armies; nādu agattum – in all the other places

**Перевод:**

**minnidaiyē sēriyilum – I will first go to the women who have lost out their hearts to him, just like I did, and tell them “He has told me ‘I do not know anyone other than you’; acting as if he were deeply in love with you, he has told you ‘I do not know anything other than your waists which are like lightning’; know how deceptive his words are”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**minnidaiyār** – women such as āṇḍāḷ, cow-herd women et al. Has he not earned the name of being a great appreciator of their beauty? I will destroy that name by showing them as to how he has appreciated my beauty.

**vēdiyargaḷ vāḻvidattum** – in places which are inhabited by great people such as vyāsa, parāsara et al, who carry out devotion, spiritual rites, mangaḷāśāsanam (praising) etc  after knowing that he is the supreme entity, through authentic sources such as śruti (vēdas) etc. I will go to their gatherings where they say that he is the supremely generous entity and show them my body \[which has become so weak, in separation from him\].

**tannadiyār munbum** – I will go to gatherings of people such as iḷaiyaperumāḷ \[lakśmaṇa\], nammāḻvār et al, who have lost out to his auspicious qualities and given themselves up to him as his servitors and tell them “Look how his qualities are”.

The term *vēdiyar* refers to those who are deeply engaged in activities which are mentioned in vēdas in order to attain emperumān and the term *than adiyār* refers to those who are fully engaged with emperumān’s divine feet as the readily available means to attain him.

**taraṇi muḻudāḷum kol navilum vēlvēndar kūttattum** – vouldn’t kings such as kulasēkara āḻvār, toṇḍaimān cakravartty et al,  who are great warriors born into the lineage of great clans and who have earned the name of Kings since they are ruling over various countries on this earth, celebrate about emperumān’s qualities of valour and protection? I will go to gatherings of such kings and destroy his qualities of valour and protection by telling them “Look at the valour that he, who was born in the clan of ikśvāku \[the first king in the clan in which śrī rāma was born\], has shown on a weak woman such as I; look at how he, who protects everyone, has protected me”.

**nāttagattum** – Will I go to only a few places which have been mentioned above? I will go to all the places where he has established himself as a supreme being. I will go to all places where he has established himself as a supreme warrior.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 93. Tan nilaimai ellam

*tan nilaimai ellām aṟivippan tān munanāḷ minnidai āycciyar tam sērikkaḷavinkaṇ                                                         137*

**Пословный перевод:**

than nilaimai ellām aṟivippan – I will expose all his qualities; munam nāḷ – in earlier time (during kriṣṇāvatāram); minnidai āycciyar tam sēri – in the hamlet of cow-herd girls whose slender waists were like lightning; kaḷavin kaṇ – in a cunning way

**Перевод:**

**than nilaimai ellām aṟivippan – I will expose everything – how he showed his divine form, then separated from me, created distress in me, made me engage with madal, and even after that, not showing up for me. Since he has decided that I alone am not required for him, I will make him not required for this world, by exposing his bad qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

Now, tirumangai āḻvār (as parakāla nāyaki) intends to prove that all the divine activities of emperumān which made āḻvārs like him to become bewildered saying “*ettiṟam*” (how amazing!) as lowly activities.

**tān munanāl . . .  peṟṟimaiyum** – I will make it known to the whole world as to how he had stolen butter from the hamlets of cow-herd girls and how he was caught and tied to the mortar.

**tān** – how shameful that he has indulged in this activity \[of stealing butter\] and is proud that “I have done it”

**muna nāḷ** – did not this activity come about at a very young age? Is he remaining calm since he thinks that the worldly people would have forgotten it?

**minnidai** – was it not to see how the lightning-like waists of the cow-herd girls trembled when they came chasing him that he indulged in stealing butter!

**minnidai āycciyar tam** – It is true that he indulged in stealing. But was it carried out with valorous men like me? Was it not from weak women that he stole?

**āycciyar tam sērikkaḷavin kaṇ** – Did he steal like I did, crossing nine well guarded streets in nāgappattaṇam? \[tirumangai āḻvār, in order to feed thousand brāhmaṇas every day, had to indulge in stealing valuable materials; one such activity was in stealing a golden statue of budda from a budda vihāra in nāgappattaṇam\] Was it not from the hamlets of cow-herd boys and cow-herd girls which had only huts, without any protection?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 94. Tunnu padal

*tunnu padal tiṟandu pukkut tayir veṇṇey tan vayiṟāra viḻungak koḻungayaṟkan                                                        138 mannu madavōrgaḷ paṟṟiyōr vānkayiṟṟāl*

**Пословный перевод:**

tunnu padal tiṟandu – by opening the gate woven with densely packed palm leaves; pukku – entering; tayir veṇṇey – curd and butter; than vayiṟu Ara tān viḻunga – as he gobbled these in order to fill up his stomach; koḻu kayal kaṇ – eyes which are like carp (a fresh water fish); mannu madavōraḷ – women of the hamlet; paṟṟi – catching him; ōr vān kayiṟṟāl – with a beautiful rope

**Перевод:**

**tunnu padal tiṟandu – did he enter like I did by turning the dome on top of the turret in budda vihāra, and damaging it, and steal? The herdspeople would have woven a densely packed palm gate and would have supported it with the trunk of a tree. Was it not by damaging this dome that he indulged in stealing!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**padal tiṟandu pukku** – normally, he would not even know the mechanism by which the gate with a stick across it is opened; he only knows to slip in under the gate and steal. However, since it was his house and since he had been stealing for a long time, he was able to carry out the difficult task of opening the gate!

**tunnu padal tiṟandu pukku** – Since it was densely woven, he entered by opening it. Had it been kept open, he would have entered by closing it!

**tayir veṇṇey** – did he steal the golden image of bhudda like I did? Was it not the lowly curd and butter!

**than vayiṟu Ara viḻunga** – He did not steal for the sake of others \[for the sake of emperumān\]; he did for his own sake!

**kozum kayal kaṇ** – the happiness was seen in the eyes of the herdsvomen on catching the person who stole.

**mannu madavōrgaḷ paṟṟi** – as soon as he is caught, a thousand girls will hold on to his one hand.

**ōr vān kayiṟṟāl** – unlike him who would not be easily accessible, it was a beautiful rope which was caught as soon as one thought of it. It was not a great rope just because it was praised as “*kaṇṇinuṇ siṟuttāmbināl*” ([kaṇṇinuṇ ciṟut tāmbu 1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2015/01/kanninun-chiru-thambu-1-kanninun/)), “*kaṇṇikkuṟungayiṟu*” (periya tirumoḻi 11-5-4) “*kaṇṇiyār kuṟungayiṟu*” (periya tirumoḻi 5-9-7); it was a strong, nice rope to tie the mischievous person.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 95. Pinnum uralodu

*pinnum uralōdu kattuṇḍa peṟṟimaiyum                                             139 annadōr būdamāy āyar viḻavinkaṇ tunnu sagadattāl pukka perunjōṟṟai                                            140 munnirundu muṟṟattān tuṟṟiya teṟṟanavum*

**Пословный перевод:**

uralōdu – (tied up) with the mortar; kattuṇḍa peṟṟimaiyum – how great it was being tied down; āyar viḻavin kaṇ – in the ārādanai (worship) carried out by the herdspeople to indira, the chief of celestial entities; tunnu sagadattāl pukka peru sōṟṟai – the huge quantum of food carried by many carts; annadu ōr būdamāy – taking the form of a demon which cannot be spoken of in words; mun irundu – right in fornt of them; muṟṟa – completely; tān – he himself; tuṟṟiya – eaten; teṟṟanavum – what a shame!

**Перевод:**

**pinnum uralōdu kattuṇḍa peṟṟimaiyum – it is not that the girls just caught hold of his hand \[as seen in the previous part\]; more than that, they tied him to a mortar, an acētana (insentient) entity. I will recount the greatness of that act too, for the entire world to hear.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**kattuṇḍa peṟṟīmaiyum** – The opinion here is that ”the sinner that I am, I did not get to see this greatness at that time itself”.  *peṟṟimai* – greatness (or fame). Here, it refers to the lowliness of the act due to *viparīta lakśaṇam* (ironical, conveying the opposite meaning).

On top of this, I want to speak about one more activity.

**annadōr** – it was such a despicable act that emperumān put on, which cannot be described by words.

**annadu** – unable to look at that grotesque form, she (parakāla nāyaki) turns her face away.

**būdamāy** – will anyone take on the role of a very lowly demon?

**āyar viḻavin kaṇ** – since he took on the role of a demon, he went to all places because of his extreme hunger. He had to gobble up the food meant for the herdspeople who were holding on to him.

**tunnu sagadattāl pukka perunjōṟṟai** – was the quantum of food such that a couple of people could carry it and he ate that? He swallowed the food which was heaped like a mountain, brought by a huge line of carts. Just as periyāḻvār had mentioned in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-5-1 “*attukkuvi sōṟṟup paruppadamum tayir vāviyum neyyaḷaṟum*” it was a combination of a mountain of cooked rice, a tank of curd and a pond of ghee which he swallowed.

**munnirundu** – what a shameless act! If he had a bit of sense, would he not have put a screen between himself and the herdspeople when he swallowed all that food? Since even that was not there, he swallowed all the food in the presence of all the herdspeople.

**muṟṟattān tuṟṟiya** – he did not offer even a handful of food to anyone! What a glutton he was!

**tān tuṟṟiya** – he ate it all up, without even getting his relatives together.

**teṟṟanavum** – he did not have any sense of shame even after doing all this! *teṟṟanavu* – clarity. Here, it means śamelessness.

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 96. Mannar perunjavaiyul

*pramēyam (goal) – https://koyil.org pramāṇam (scriptures) – http://grantams.koyil.org pramātā (preceptors) – http://acaryas.koyil.org śrīvaiṣṇava education/kids portal – http://pillai.koyil.org*

**Пословный перевод:**

munam nāḷ – during an earlier time; vāḻvēndar tūdanāy – as pāṇḍava’s messenger; tannai igaḻndu uraippa – to be spoken of despicably; mannar peru savaiyuḷ senṟadum – the way he went to the court of great kings (such as duriyōdanā et al); mangaiyar tam kaṇ kaḷippa – eyes of the (herd)ladies’ to rejoice; mannu paṟai kaṟanga – the drum tied (to the waist) to sound

**Перевод:**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

## 97. Konnavilum kuttanay

*konnavilum kūttanāyp pērttum kudamādi ennivan ennappaduginṟa vīdaṟavum                                                                   143 tennilangaiyātti arakkar kulappāvai mannan irāvaṇan tan nal tangai vāḷeyiṟṟut                                                  144 tunnu sudusinattuc cūrppaṇakāc cōrveydi*

**Пословный перевод:**

kol navilum kūttanāy – carrying out the dance which kills (women); pērttum – further; kudam ādi – dancing with pots; en ivan ennappaduginṟa īdaṟavum – getting ruined of character with people wondering “Will anyone dance like this?”; then ilangaiyātti – the queen of lankā in the south; arakkar kulapopāvai – one who looked like an idol, born in the clan of demons; mannan irāvaṇan than nal tangai – the affectionate sister of King rāvaṇa; vāḷ eyiṟu – having teeth like a sword (being resplendent); tunnu sudu sinattu – having an anger which will singe; sūrppaṇakā – called as sūrppaṇakā

**Перевод:**

**konnavilum kūttanāy – while dancing, he had a beauty which was capable of killing women**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pērttum kudamādi** – more than that, taking up pots and dancing with them

**ennivan ennappaduginṟa īdaṟavum** – he also had the simplicity which would make the women to think “What is this? Would he not stop dancing and killing us in the bargain, and leave this place?”

Leave aside what had been said till now. Hear this terrible incident:

**tennilangaiyātti** – Is there anyone born like this person? Having the name “rāvaṇa is the king of lankā”, did he not truly control lankā? Was he not born to enjoy the wealth of lankā?

**arakkar kulappāvai** – Was she not born as the ‘apple of the eye’ as far as the clan of demons was concerned?

**mannan irāvaṇan** – How remarkable! Was there anyone who lived as the King among kings just as rāvaṇa lived?

**than naltangai** – was she not born as his sister, being very dear to him?

**vāḷ eyiṟṟu** – How resplendent were her teeth! If there ever were a beautiful girl, the beauty of her teeth should be like what she had.

**tunnu sudu sinattu** – forget her external beauty. How great was her inner beauty! Did not the anger which she had towards him \[śrī rāma\], who could not understand the love of a forlorn woman, not reflect the beauty of her mind!

**sūrppaṇakā** – she had nails resembling a winnow!

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 98. Ponnirangondu

*ponniṟangoṇḍu pulandeḻunda kāmattāl                                               145 tannai nayandāḷait tān munindu mūkkarindu manniya tiṇṇenavum vāytta malai pōlum                                                 146 tannigar onṟillāda tātakayai māmunikkā*

**Пословный перевод:**

pularndu – her body withered \[lifeless\]; eḻunda kāmattāl – due to the disease of lust which swelled; pon niram koṇḍu – her complexion became pale \[lost its colour\]; sōrvu eydi – going into a trance; tannai nayandāḷai – that demonic woman who desired him; tān munindu – becoming fiery; mūkku arindu – cutting off her nose; manniya tiṇṇenavum – how firm he became!; vāytta malai pōlum – one who had the form similar to a mountain; than nigar onṟu illāda – being incomparable; tātakaiyai – a woman called as tātakā; māmunikkā – for the sake of sage viśvāmitra

**Перевод:**

**sōrvu eydi – did she not become tired since she fell in love, just like I did?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pon niṟam koṇḍu** – since her love did not fructify, did she too not lose her complexion, like I?

**pularndu** – did she also not wither, just like I

**eḻunda kāmattāl** – with the love which blossomed, larger than an ocean. What a great prize she got for the love that she had for him!

**tannai nayandāḷai** – the mistake she committed was in being desirous of him!

**th9n munindu** – one should reciprocate love. Even if that is not done, is it proper for one to get angry?

**mūkku arindu** – even if one became angry, we can forget it. But didn’t he cut off her nose?

**manniya tiṇṇenavum** – did he, at the least, feel remorseful for such a cruel act? No; did he not stand firm as if he had carried out a valorous act? Is this an act of valour? *tiṇṇenavu* – robustness.

Finally, I will narrate another incident, which is the peak of all cruel acts. Listen:

**vāytta malai pōlum** – what a great physique did she have!

**tannigar onṟu illāda tātakaiyai** – Is there anyone to match her so that one could console oneself even if she went.

**māmunikkā** – did he not carry out this act in such a crafty way that if one asked him forcefully “You killed a woman” he would shift the blame by responding “A brāhmaṇa asked me to do it and hence I did ”? The commentator aḻagiya maṇavāḷapperumāḷ nāyanār has said “I have heard from nampiḷḷai that he has heard this explanation from \[parāsara\] bhattar”.

**māmunikkā** – Was it not for the great *tapasvi* (one who carries out penance) *viśvāmitra* that he brought such a disgrace?

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 99. Tennulagam erruvitta

*tennulagam ēṟṟuvitta tiṇḍiṟalum maṟṟivaidān                                 147 unni ulavā ulagaṟiya ūrvan nān munni muḷaitteḻundu ōngi oḷi paranda                                                  148 manniya pūm peṇṇai madal*

**Пословный перевод:**

then ulagam ēṟṟuvitta – so as to reach yamalōkam (hell); thin tiṟalum – what great strength; maṟṟu ivai tān – various other activities which are similar to these; unni ulavā – they cannot be thought of completely; ulagu aṟiya – announcing such activities so that people of the world would know; nān – I, parakāla nāyaki; munni – standing up front; muḷaittu eḻundu ōngi oḷi paranda manniya pū peṇṇai madal ūrvan – I will engage with madal from beautiful, firm and splendorous palm leaf which has been taken from a well sprouted, high grown pam tree

**Перевод:**

**tennulagam ēṟṟuvitta – he let sūrppaṇaka to live in this world; in the case of tātakā, he sent her to the world of yama \[hell\] itself. How cruel!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**tiṇ tiṟalum** – how to praise his valour that he shows towards helpless women?

**maṟṟivaidān unni ulavā** – it is not possible to complete the incidents that I have gathered of this emperumān, just like one gathers wealth which will help during times of distress. It will suffer the same way that vēdam suffered when it tried to estimate the bliss of emperumān and returned empty handed. Since he is likely to come scurrying and fall at my feet after I start revealing these, I consider them as my wealth during distress.

**ulagu aṟiya ūrvan nān** – I will engage with madal such that all *cētanas* (sentient beings) will come to know of them.

**munni** – Standing right up-front, saying “I am going to engage with madal”. The meaning implied is that it is not possible to wait for him \[tirumangai āḻvār\] any more.

What sort of madal is this?

**muḷaittu eḻundu ōngi oḷiparanda manniya pūm peṇṇai madal** – I will engage with madal which has sprouted from earth, grown tall, remained resplendently, remained firm.

**ūrvan nān madal** – I have a great brahmāśtra (very potent weapon) in my hand. What shortfall do I have now? This implies that there is no hurdle for my goal being reached. She is praising this \[madal\] because she feels that it will help her in uniting with emperumān, just like viśvāmitra bhagavān united sītāppirātti with perumāḷ \[śrī rāma\].

In the next article, we will discuss the next part of this prabandam.

## 100. Ennilaimai ellam

*ṭhis pāsuram is similar to te one at te end of siṟiya tirumadal. ṭhis pāsuram, composed by te tamiḻ poet kambar as if it vere recited by parakāla nāyaki herself, is recited at te end of periya tirumadal. ṭhese pāsurams \[tis and te one vhic appears at te end of siṟiya tirumadal\], śov te devotion tat kambar had tovards tirumangai āḻvār.*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nilaimai ellām – all my various states; aṟivittāl – after informing; emperumān – the lord of all, my svāmy; than aruḷum – his mercy; āgamum – his divine chest; tārān ēl – if he does not grant; pinnai pōy – after that, going to; oṇḍuṟai nīr vēlai ulagu aṟiya – making it known to the people of the world, surrounded by ocean which has effulgent places for taking a dip and which is full of water; nān – I; vaṇḍu aṟai pūm peṇṇai madal ūrvan – I will engage with a beautiful madal which is resounding with the humming of beetles

**Перевод:**

**en nilaimai . . . . . . tārānēl – these two lines are taken from part nos 90 and 91 in periya tirumadal. vyākhyānam for these two lines can be seen there. We can see that in kamba rāmāyaṇam too, there are many pāsurams where the words and meanings are common with tirumangai āḻvār’s pāsurams.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**pinnaip pōy** – If he does not carry out what he is expected to do, I will do what I am not supposed to do. While he should come to the place where I am present, I will go to the place where he is residing.

**oṇḍuṟai nīr vēlai ulagaṟiya** – to make it known to the people who are in the world surrounded by ocean with splendorous steps for taking bath and which is full of water.

**oṇḍuṟai** – since pearls and corals are strewn across the ocean, it has effulgence.

**ulagu** – people who are living in the world.It voud also mean “great people”

**ūrvan nān . . . .madal** – I will engage with madal which is resounding with the humming of beetles.

This brings us to the end of translation for periya tirumadal.

*periya pirātti samēda periya perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*  
*tirumangai āḻvār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*  
*periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*  
*aḻagiya maṇavāḷapperumāḷ nāyanār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam*
